\begin{abstract}
In Observer Patch Holography a conserved stress carried by overlap
records would enter the fixed-volume entropy balance at the gravitational
coupling measured on ordinary matter. The hypothesis is that this
stress accounts for the extra attraction inferred from clocks, orbits
and lensing. On a finite pressureless sphere whose matter action weighs
coincidence records, two independent measurements recover the same
increase in gravitational mass. In the native material, comparisons of
ordinary insertion work detect modular charge at fixed local load.
Material propagation varies at fixed modular charge.
Its conversion to regional gravitating stress therefore needs an
independent measurement.

The proposed source must also predict an abundance and a spatial
distribution. Six radial acoustic distances and three galaxy-age rates
require more than $1.278$ extra units of gravitating dust per baryon in
a flat background with the stated baryon, neutrino and radiation bounds
and a free acoustic ruler. The sphere's equal-mass coincidence rule
permits at most one. On 1,288 outer rings in 136 SPARC galaxies,
even freely chosen nonnegative multiples of the gas, stellar disk
and bulge contributions conflict with 23 galaxies when each speed
can vary by five quoted errors. Under explicit scale covariance and
quadrature composition a spherical deep-regime profile gives flat
rotation curves and the fourth-power baryonic mass law. Its
normalization, physical source distribution and common response of light
and matter require records independent of the orbits used to test them.

\end{abstract}

\section{What Sources Gravity in OPH}

The finite classification, recovery and profile identities are checked under
the stated hypotheses in the Lean formalization~\cite{ophlean}. Their physical
identification is an additional premise throughout.

In the executable studied here, finite observers occupy overlapping
patches of a spherical federation of icosahedral cells. Interval Echo
repairs disagreements at shared ports and retains a physical record on
each serviced seam~\cite{ophmodel}. Two prepared loads can interrogate
that record by interference. In the dark-sector hypothesis, the
candidate gravitational source is the anomalous modular charge of the
overlap. The probe reads the record on which a finite version of that
charge can be measured.

The conditional Einstein arrow of the spacetime paper ends in
\begin{equation}
G_{ab}+\Lambda g_{ab}=8\pi G\langle T_{ab}\rangle,
\label{eq:einstein}
\end{equation}
where \(\langle T_{ab}\rangle\) is a supplied local, covariant, conserved
stress on the physical branch~\cite{ophEinstein}. Its smooth form requires
same-family tensor-curvature convergence or the continuum
small-ball/null-balance identification; scalar-curvature convergence alone is
diagnostic. Finite positive-null
tomography exactly reconstructs a quadratic form from directional charges,
but identifying that form with the same local stress additionally requires
charge--stress matching, locality and covariance, and a weak Ward law. In the
rest frame the relation then follows from three further premises on a small
geodesic ball of radius \(\ell\): generalized entropy is declared stationary,
the bulk entropy variation equals
\((8\pi^2\ell^4/15)\,\delta\langle T_{00}\rangle\), and the fixed-volume area
variation equals \(-(4\pi\ell^4/15)\,\delta G_{00}\). Exact arithmetic gives
\(\delta G_{00}=8\pi G\,\delta\langle T_{00}\rangle\).

On that conditional branch, the stress entering this relation is identified
with the same modular charge. The screen does not
consult the electromagnetic readout of a source before responding to it. That
is the universal-coupling premise, and it is the only input the dark-sector
question takes from the gravity derivation.

Consider two preparations of one region with identical ordinary stress
and different overlap stress in the same observer frame. Subtracting
their rest-frame equations leaves $8\pi G$ times the difference of
overlap stresses. A calculation from the ordinary records alone
assigns identical curvature responses to these preparations. It can
agree with the total balance on both only if the overlap difference
vanishes. Thus the extra response is required on this branch and
ordinary matter fixes its coupling. Whether the records in a galaxy realize
the required stress, with the attractive sign and measured spatial
profile, is the physical prediction to test.

\section{The Generic Dark Sector}
\label{sec:generic}

Let a finite family of screen sources be indexed by \(i\), each with
rest-frame modular-charge contribution \(q_i\) and a flag recording whether
it carries the electromagnetic readout. Write \(Q_{\rm lum}\) for the sum over
flagged sources and \(Q_{\rm dark}\) for the sum over the others, so that
\(Q_{\rm tot}=Q_{\rm lum}+Q_{\rm dark}\).

\begin{premise}[Universal coupling]
\label{pre:universal}
The stress contraction entering the rest-frame Einstein relation equals
\(Q_{\rm tot}\), with no readout-dependent weight.
\end{premise}

\begin{proposition}[Total and non-luminous response]
\label{thm:all-charge}\label{thm:iff}\label{cor:excess}\label{prop:sign}
Under Premise~\ref{pre:universal} and \(G>0\),
\(\delta G_{00}=8\pi G(Q_{\rm lum}+Q_{\rm dark})\).
The luminous-only equation holds exactly when \(Q_{\rm dark}=0\).
If every non-luminous charge is nonnegative, its weak-field contribution
has the attractive sign.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Substitute the finite-sum decomposition of \(Q_{\rm tot}\) in the assumed
response law. Cancel \(8\pi G\) for the equivalence and sum nonnegative
terms for the sign.
\end{proof}
This classifies a geometric excess if one exists; it does not establish a
nonzero charge, its abundance, or a physical carrier.

\section{The Modular-Anomaly Source}
\label{sec:source}

On the support-visible branch the cap modular generator has the local form
\begin{equation}
K_C=2\pi B_C+K_C^{\rm anom}+\mathrm{const},
\label{eq:split}
\end{equation}
with \(B_C\) the bulk boost generator and \(K_C^{\rm anom}\) the nonadditive
part carried on the overlap collar~\cite{ophEinstein}. At finite regulator
stage the separately supplied collar-recovery model carries this part as a
remainder and bounds it:
\begin{equation}
\bigl|\delta\langle E_C^{(\delta)}\rangle\bigr|\le C_C\,\eta_\delta,
\label{eq:bound}
\end{equation}
where \(\eta_\delta\) is the collar recovery defect and \(C_C\) depends only
on the collar model and the bounded observable class.

\begin{definition}[Anomalous stress and density]
\begin{equation}
\delta\langle T_{00}^{\rm anom}\rangle
:=\frac{15}{8\pi^2\ell^4}\,\delta\langle E_C^{(\delta)}\rangle,
\qquad
\rho_A:=\frac{1}{c^2}\,\delta\langle T_{00}^{\rm anom}\rangle .
\label{eq:tanom}
\end{equation}
\end{definition}

On a family of positive shrinking proper radii $\ell_n$, let $E_n$
denote the measured collar-energy variation on the corresponding ball
in one fixed observer frame. Equation~\eqref{eq:tanom} gives the exact
criterion for a finite rest-frame stress $\tau$,
\[
  \delta T^{\rm anom}_{00,n}\longrightarrow\tau
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  \frac{E_n-(8\pi^2/15)\ell_n^4\tau}{\ell_n^4}\longrightarrow0 .
\]
This follows by multiplying the rescaled difference by the positive
constant $15/(8\pi^2)$. In particular, a recovery bound
$C_n\eta_n=o(\ell_n^4)$ forces $\tau=0$. For nonzero $\tau$ the
leading collar coefficient belongs in the total stress of the Einstein
equation, while the residual after its subtraction must satisfy the
small-ball error bound. A fluctuation measured on a galactic cut
requires a map to these local contractions, including their sign,
normalization and transport. The galactic radius in the enclosed-mass
profile and the local proper radius $\ell_n$ serve different measurements.

The dark-sector adapter identifies this anomalous modular energy with the
non-luminous charge of Section~\ref{sec:generic}. Its absence of an
electromagnetic readout is a source-localization hypothesis: it is assigned to
the part of the modular generator that no local record exposes. Neither
identification follows from the finite Einstein-shape theorem. Three consequences follow from
\eqref{eq:bound} and \eqref{eq:tanom}.

\begin{theorem}[Bounded source]
\label{thm:bounded}
\(\bigl|\delta\langle T_{00}^{\rm anom}\rangle\bigr|
\le 15\,C_C\,\eta_\delta/(8\pi^2\ell^4)\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Full recovery switches the source off]
\label{thm:off}
If \(\eta_\delta=0\) then \(\delta\langle T_{00}^{\rm anom}\rangle=0\).
Along any family with fixed \(\ell\), bounded \(C_C\), and
\(\eta_\delta\to0\), the anomalous stress tends to zero.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Cold scaling]
\label{thm:cold}
A conserved anomalous charge in a comoving cell of physical volume
\(\propto a^3\) has density \(\propto a^{-3}\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proofs]
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded} multiplies \eqref{eq:bound} by the positive kernel
inverse. Theorem~\ref{thm:off} is the case \(\eta_\delta=0\) of the bound and
a squeeze along the family. Theorem~\ref{thm:cold} is
\(\rho\,a^3=Q\).
\end{proof}

The split \eqref{eq:split} has a machine-checked finite counterpart. On a
finite carrier of microstates labelled bulk or collar, with strictly
positive weights, a declared boost datum vanishing on the collar, and
modular generator \(K=-\log w\), the decomposition
\(K=2\pi B+K^{\rm anom}+c\,\mathbf 1\) exists with \(c\) the bulk-weighted
mean of \(K-2\pi B\), is unique among splits whose anomalous part has
vanishing bulk mean, and is invariant under the additive-constant
quotient \(w\mapsto e^{-t}w\). When the bulk weights are exactly Gibbs for
\(2\pi B\), the anomalous part vanishes on the bulk and is carried
entirely on the collar, and its expectation is the remainder bounded in
\eqref{eq:bound}. The finite carrier, the boost datum, the normalization
clause, and the Gibbs condition are model premises; the receipt is the
finite counterpart of \eqref{eq:split}, at exactly that strength.

For a static weak field with pressures negligible compared with the
rest-energy densities, take $\Phi$ relative to the common vacuum
potential. The anomalous mass density then enters the Poisson equation
beside the baryons,
\begin{equation}
\nabla^2\Phi=4\pi G\,(\rho_b+\rho_A),
\label{eq:poisson}
\end{equation}
with the attractive sign of Proposition~\ref{prop:sign} whenever the
anomalous charge is nonnegative. In units $c=1$, the equation for the
unsubtracted potential includes $-\Lambda$, and appreciable principal
pressures add $4\pi G\sum_i p_i$ at this order. The identification
of the full stress enters before taking the slow-motion limit.
The ordinary and overlap terms partition the same regional modular
variation; counting the measured overlap variation in both terms would
overestimate the source.

\section{Galactic-Scale Collars and the Solar System}
\label{sec:solar}

The collars in \eqref{eq:split} are overlaps between physical observer
patches. Their recovery defect measures how well the records on an
overlap determine neighbouring records. The relevant density counts
records made and retained by the modeled system. Consider two patches
carrying the same fair bit. Suppose their shared region retains that bit
in four fifths of the trials and carries a blank in the rest. Reading the
retained bits and guessing on the blanks gives one wrong reconstruction
in ten trials on average. No predictor reading only this record can do
better. Reading the same blank a second time supplies no missing bit.
The exact result for retention probability $p$ is $(1-p)/2$. Here $p$
describes the local record channel. Telescopes at different distances
viewing otherwise identical galaxies would infer the same intrinsic
gravitational response after accounting for distance and projection.

The galactic hypothesis assigns small defects to densely recorded
regions where repair saturates, and persistent defects to sparsely
recorded cuts in settled galactic outskirts. A recovery defect bounds
the magnitude of the anomalous charge. An attractive excess requires
a nonzero charge of the appropriate sign and its universal coupling
to the reconstructed geometry. The consensus-layer provenance package
supplies a narrower finite statement~\cite{ophconsensus}: an injected overlap
mismatch persists at full strength through any continuation in which no later
commit writes a register in that overlap support. This is an unwritten-support
persistence theorem. It does not show that a physical galactic cut satisfies
the no-write hypothesis or remains long-lived. The proposed physical
identification assigns this anomalous record state to galactic collars.
The map from screen collars to nested three-dimensional galactic shells is
part of this identification.

In Interval Echo, a private contrast record and its stored energy
can persist even when both endpoint loads are absent. The agreement
coupling is 8 in exchange units. An observer can distinguish the
energy of the record, the response of a probe to it, and the extent
to which it can be recovered from shared readouts. The dark-sector
hypothesis concerns the last comparison through the anomalous
modular generator.

For a double-contrast record, the two coin branches of the native
write have identical endpoint loads. Their antisymmetric combination
is orthogonal to the image of the write for every load input. On
this coin sector the stored energies $0,32,33$ multiply the identity
on the endpoint loads. Changing the energy therefore gives identical
free load statistics throughout the sphere. The symmetric coin gives
a different result. A probe may place two units on the marked endpoints
or park one of them on a spare rail. The relative phase between these
alternatives reads the stored energy. At an exposure of $\pi/32$ the
returns for energies zero and $32$ are disjoint, using a supplied
interference reference and local service windows. On a protected
antisymmetric eigenword with scalar energy $E$, a classically scheduled
marked exposure of integrated duration $s$ contributes the factor
$e^{-iEs}$ to every matter amplitude. Even an adaptive sequence of
load-only measurements has the same transcript law at these energies:
condition on a common past, cancel the scalar phase in the next
probability, and iterate. This remains true on each refinement
preserving the source and instrument. A coherent reference that controls
whether the marked seam is serviced supplies another interferometric
experiment. If the protected energy differences are admitted as stresses
with a nonzero physical normalization, load-only flight geometry under
classical schedules cannot satisfy universal coupling for those stresses.

This gives a finite recovery test. Let $Z$ record a fair preparation
bit, $C$ the actual collar bit after an independent native flip of
probability $1/4$, and $B$ a material probe outcome. On this commuting
record algebra the conditional recovery distribution is
$P^{\rm rec}(z,c,b)=P(z,b)P(c\mid b)$. Its total variation distance
from the joint record is $1/4$ for a blind probe and zero for the
two-load interferometer. The expectation change of the logarithmic
remainder $\log(P^{\rm rec}/P)$ is bounded by $2\log(3)$ times this
distance. The stored collar energy survives full recovery.

At an intermediate probe exposure of $\pi/64$, hold the reference
flip probability at $1/4$ and vary the fraction of repaired histories.
At flip probabilities $1/8$ and $3/8$ independent patch samples give
changes in the expectation of the same log remainder of
$-0.09319\pm0.00156$ and $+0.09049\pm0.00174$ nats (one standard
error). The entire joint distribution of the actual collar word and
probe outcome is identical in the two arms. The retained preparation
record carries the distinguishing correlation. At $\pi/32$ the
remainder vanishes for both histories, with mean stored energy fixed at 16.
This finite record observable supplies an operational test of recovery.
Identifying it with $K_C-2\pi B_C$ in physical units requires an
independent boost normalization, a proper radius and the response of
the same geometry to the signed charge.

A second test retains coherences on three ports of one prepared cap.
A quantum recovery map constructs a comparison state from overlapping
two-port marginals. Direct local tomography of the logarithmic
difference on the matter support resolves six signed perturbations in
simulated counts. The held charge at port 9 and exposure 4 is
$+0.0000963\pm0.0000224$ nats (simultaneous 95 percent half-width),
against $+0.0001063$ computed from the states. Dephasing the reference
reverses the calculated sign. The precision run represents
$2.93\times10^{16}$ accepted reference preparations. This comparison
tests a finite quantum source observable under a supplied recovery map.

A propagation test puts both correlated words on physical neighbouring
seams. Changing their disagreement probability leaves their individual
distributions fixed and shifts the expectation of a reference
logarithmic charge by $\pm0.1373$ nats. Every freely propagating
one-load signal has the same channel for these preparations at
each native exposure. An independently released two-load probe
distinguishes the histories through a local coincidence interaction.
In a three-level control, both individual energy distributions and
the complete histogram of their sum are fixed. An oriented change of
the joint records gives opposite signed charge changes of $0.09155$
nats. With a calibrated phase pulse the local return changes from
$0.35566$ to $0.45189$. The single-load channel is identical.
A disjoint material hand and the oriented probe can be read in one
trial. On this three-arm family, the measured hand phase and the
imaginary part of the fringe give $-0.09095\pm0.00162$,
$+0.00088\pm0.00117$ and $+0.09039\pm0.00085$ nats at the
well-conditioned setting (one standard error conditional on this reference
and source family). The inverse abstains at the three
weak settings. It consumes the finite reference and native contact
frequencies. All arms have the same locally injective real fringe
near launch and unequal imaginary third derivatives. Thus even a
source-dependent reparametrization of one common clock cannot explain
this complex response. The probe reads an oriented material correlation.
Its identification with a gravitational source requires an independent
metric response with the same normalization as ordinary matter.

The cut amplitude in the galaxy prescription is a fluctuation. Its
choice requires an observer record. Let a physical tag $T=\pm1$ on a
disjoint parked seam select the oriented history, and compare with a tag
independent of the histories. Both preparations give the same untagged
word density and the same complete score variance, $0.154671$ nats$^2$.
The variance between conditional means, $\operatorname{Var}_T
\mathbb E[Q\mid T]$, is $(\log(3)/12)^2=0.008382$ nats$^2$ in the
tagged selection and zero in the control. The within-tag variance supplies
the complementary part of the total. Local word readings give a
between-history amplitude of $0.09156\pm0.00037$ nats. Independent
tagged interference readings give $0.09086\pm0.00091$ nats. The
tag and its source preparation are supplied. A physical per-cut mass
variance requires a specified cut record and a gravitational conversion.

\begin{proposition}[Which record can measure a fluctuation?]
Let a fair material tag label two conditional score means $\mu-a$ and
$\mu+a$, with $a>0$. Exchange the labels in a second preparation and mix
the two preparations equally in a third. Every fixed measurement on one
complete tag and word has a mean that differs from their between-tag
standard deviation by at least $a/2$ on one of the three preparations.
Two conditionally independent writes under one retained tag measure
the corresponding variance through their centred score product.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The middle state is the midpoint of the other two, including the tag.
Linearity of outcome probabilities makes its mean the midpoint of the
two endpoint means. The three target amplitudes in endpoint, midpoint,
endpoint order are $a,0,a$. If all discrepancies are at most $e$, the
middle mean is both at least $a-e$ and at most $e$. Hence $e\ge a/2$,
attained by the constant $a/2$. For writes $Q_1,Q_2$ with the same
conditional law and independent noise given $T$,
\[
 \mathbb E[(Q_1-\mu)(Q_2-\mu)]
 =\mathbb E[(\mathbb E[Q_1\mid T]-\mu)^2].
\]
\end{proof}
Here $a=\log(3)/12$ nats. At full positive tag correlation the paired
read gives $0.00851\pm0.00016$ nats$^2$ against $0.00838$ predicted.
A fresh independent tag on each write gives $0.00004\pm0.00015$,
while rereading the identical word gives $0.15438\pm0.00020$, the
entire word variance. These acquisitions use the supplied conditional
preparation twice and save the first classical outcome. A physical
stress law based on this fluctuation must specify how successive
repairs preserve the conditioning history and how geometry responds to
the accumulated record. A nonlinear inference from many readings can
also estimate the amplitude.

The native interaction can itself preserve a shared cause for two
readings. Interrogate the same three-level collar word twice with
fresh probes. Three fixed exchanges store the first outcome and reset
the probe without consulting it. The word remains an eigenword of both
contacts. Conditional on it, the two returns are independent Bernoulli
trials of probability $p$. Hence their connected covariance is
$\operatorname{Var}(p)$. The native probabilities give $2/27$ and the
simulated paired records give $0.07389\pm0.00552$ (simultaneous 99\%
radius). Independently replacing the source word between the probes
gives $-0.00004\pm0.00552$. The return probability and the logarithmic
cut charge are different observables. This experiment establishes
persistence of the covariance under a fixed service without selecting
the second probe on the first outcome.

A separate timing experiment parks one ordinary load at the common port
of the two source contacts. Six unmarked addresses send and return a
second load; a local material hand times completed trips, and the
detectors also retain outward hits and timeouts. Adding the ordinary
load changes one mean half-trip by $+0.04287\pm0.00096$ native time
units. At fixed ordinary load, opposite signed sources change the same
mean by $+0.000006\pm0.000938$. These radii have simultaneous 99\%
coverage under the experiment's sampling model. A fresh high-count
comparison enrolled the same calibrated local pair and a pair
in the neighbouring cell. At the local pair the hand gives
$(3.83\pm2.13)\times10^{-6}$ native time units for the signed change
against $3.74\times10^{-6}$ predicted. At the neighbouring pair it gives
$(0.03\pm1.27)\times10^{-6}$, consistent with zero. The intervals
hold jointly at 99\% for the two pairs and the fixed preparations.
Each source arm, flight direction and hand setting uses $2^{54}$
simulated enrollments drawn in batches from the computed detector law.
This establishes a finite timing response with ordinary matter fixed.
Native propagation on 80- and 320-cell screens predicts the same
$3.74\times10^{-6}$ local shift at the same microscopic addresses and
service durations. The larger screens test finite-size sensitivity.
A shrinking proper radius and a common spacetime curvature tensor
require further geometric measurements.
A separate outward
detector in two directions resolves probability changes of order
$10^{-7}$ under this intervention. The contact scatters the probe.
The complete untagged flights of the single stored word remain identical under the two fluctuation
preparations, whose reduced source states agree. This apparatus measures
ordinary-load and contact responses; a universal metric coupling cannot
be inferred from them. The two interventions also have different spatial
distributions of source stress and different native units. A measurement
of their common gravitational coupling needs independently normalized
source distributions and a geometric observable with the predicted
response to each. The signed shift survives a precision test. The
single-word fluctuation ensembles have identical tag-independent record
distributions at every precision.

The complete terminal outcomes distinguish the two interventions even
where both increase the mean travel time. For one launch direction,
adding ordinary load raises the probabilities of an outward hit followed
by a return timeout and of a completed trip by $0.001031$ and $0.000876$,
respectively. The signed change gives $-4.80\times10^{-8}$ and
$+4.81\times10^{-8}$ for those same outcomes. The simultaneous 99\%
half-widths are $1.16\times10^{-5}$ for the ordinary contrasts and
$2.27\times10^{-8}$ for the signed contrasts. Multiplying the two
positive ordinary contrasts by one real number gives two entries of the
same sign or two zeros, excluding the measured pair. Thus an effective
amount of added load fitted to this clock cannot reproduce the complete
flight record. Universal gravitational coupling concerns normalized
stress distributions and a geometrically calibrated response on a common
domain.

\paragraph{Two physical writes and the choice of modular reference.}
Four collar memories surround one stationary ordinary load on a twenty-cell
screen. A fair tag selects the conditional distributions $P_0+sD$ and
$P_0-sD$ of one two-seam word, where $0\le s\le1$. Two writes are
independent given that tag. Averaging over a shared tag gives
\[
 W_s=P_0\otimes P_0+s^2D\otimes D .
\]
Fresh independent tags give $W_0$ at every strength. Reversing the tag
on the second write changes the sign of the connected term. Throughout,
each word marginal and the joint histogram of the two stored energy sums
are fixed. A receiver on an unmarked port detects a flying ordinary
probe with no access to the tag. The measured shared-minus-independent
arrival probability is $(1.034\pm0.214)\times10^{-9}$ against
$1.067\times10^{-9}$ from native propagation. Halving $s$ on both writes
gives $(0.245\pm0.214)\times10^{-9}$ against $0.267\times10^{-9}$.
The receiver and count budget were chosen after inspecting a numerical
forecast. These are fresh simulated binomial acquisitions with simultaneous
99\% radii conditional on the preparation and numerical detector probabilities.

For a faithful joint reference $R$ on the two physically readable words,
the connected modular score is
$L_R(i,j)=\log[R_1(i)R_2(j)/R(i,j)]$. The fixed marginals make the
shared-minus-independent mean of any sum of two single-word generators
exactly zero. The product reference gives $L_R=0$. Independent sharp
word acquisitions calibrate $R$ and measure the mean contrast on held
records. The reversed, product and shared references give
$+0.003306\pm0.000232$, $+0.000001\pm0.000166$ and
$-0.002949\pm0.000142$ nats, respectively, with joint 99\% coverage.
The same positive between-history variance therefore admits all three
signs of modular response under these reference choices. Identifying
$L_R$ with the collar part of a boost charge requires a physical
reference and the normalization in Eq.~\eqref{eq:tanom}.

Protecting the words during flight leaves their projectors central
in the block-preserving observable algebra. An equilibrium state in
each word block may therefore be mixed with arbitrary block weights
at the same temperature. Thermal calibration inside one block supplies
no relative probabilities for the words. The mutual information of the
writes is a different reference-independent statistic of their joint
law. With $C=D\otimes D$ and $R=W_0$, it is
$\sum (R+s^2 C)\log(1+s^2 C/R)
 = (s^4/2)\sum C^2/R+O(s^6)$, since $\sum C=0$.
The native probabilities give $0.001495$ nats at full strength and
$0.00009559$ at half strength. Relative entropy detects the correlation
while its first derivative about the product state vanishes. A source
preparation or thermalization service that mixes the sectors can be
tested through the relative frequencies of the written words.

\begin{proposition}[Amplitude and initial acceleration on two writes]
On the shared-tag family, any fixed unconditioned detector mean has
uniform error at least $a/8$ as an estimator of the amplitude $sa$ at
$s=0,1/2,1$. For the native mobile load, changing $s$ gives exactly
zero change in the initial acceleration of every diagonal patch
coordinate, for any launch state prepared independently of the words.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The detector mean at half strength equals three quarters of its mean at
zero strength plus one quarter of its mean at full strength. Applying
the same interpolation to the target gives $a/4$ in place of $a/2$,
hence $2e\ge a/4$ for maximum error $e$. For word $w$ the flight
generator is $H_w=H_0+V_w$, where $V_w$ is diagonal and linear in
the four stored energies. For diagonal $F$ the acceleration operator is
$-[H_0,[H_0,F]]-[V_w,[H_0,F]]$. The four energy means are fixed.
Averaging the operator therefore gives the same result for every $s$.
\end{proof}
There is a stronger short-time cancellation for this particular writer.
Its signed one-word contrast $D$ annihilates every polynomial in the
two energies of total degree at most two. Its two one-seam marginals
vanish, and its antisymmetry kills the mixed quadratic term. Thus
$D\otimes D$ annihilates every four-energy polynomial of degree below
six. The $k$-fold commutator for $F$ has energy degree at most $k-1$
for $k\ge1$. Position derivatives through order six agree with the
independent-tag control. Real symmetric generators and a real launch
give an even position mean, so the first possible contrast has order
eight. Finite-duration scattering is consistent with this identity.

Two balanced tracers with zero initial current give a change of
$+9.68963$ in their relative projected angular acceleration upon adding
the ordinary load, in native angular-coordinate units per exposure
squared. Their prediction under the shared-tag intervention is zero.
An independent instrument reads the mean half-trip among successful
reunions for each of fifteen radar pairs. In all six five-station
subsets of each of four source arms the inferred four ray directions
give a positive fourth Gram eigenvalue throughout
$|K|(6\ {\rm hand\ units})^2\le1$, with simultaneous 99\% sampling
bounds. Four directions based at one point of a three-dimensional
constant-sectional-curvature normal ball require a Gram matrix of
rank at most three. The vacant control also fails this test.
The exclusion extends to variable sectional curvature in the same
bound if the reported lengths are minimizing geodesics in one convex
comparison neighbourhood. The comparison angles bound every entry of
the unit tangent Gram matrix. Even its least eigenvalue exceeds $0.029$
after propagating the joint range uncertainties~\cite{dyerTriangulations}.
A common gravitational calibration needs an independent geometry that
recovers its source-free distances, and a cut stress in the same units
and region as the ordinary calibrator.

The exterior detector also diagnoses a restriction imposed by the
apparatus. The parked load and protected tag leave one serviced
connection from the home cell to the exterior. At a fixed complex
frequency the exterior response to any home launch is then a scalar
multiple of one exterior Green function. This follows by solving the
exterior row of the resolvent equation. Changing the home source can
change the transmitted amplitude, but the normalized exterior
intensity pattern is fixed. Transporting the sharp tag to a neighbouring
patch with five native swaps frees a second gateway on the same
federation. Two exterior detectors then resolve different arrival
fractions for two launch sites. At a held frequency one fraction
changes by $-0.00651$ when adding ordinary occupancy. Changing the
shared history at fixed occupancy changes its numerical forecast by
less than $10^{-12}$. These frequency-resolved fractions diagnose the
local propagation instrument. Identifying an optical area requires
freely propagating ray bundles and a common physical length scale.

\begin{premise}[Collar scale]
\label{pre:scale}
The collars carrying \(\delta\langle E_C^{(\delta)}\rangle\) are
galactic-scale settled cuts. A compact source inside the galaxy does not
generate anomalous charge of its own; it sits in the ambient anomalous
density \(\rho_A\) of its neighbourhood.
\end{premise}

A conditional saturation theorem supports limiting suppression in the
compact-source part of this premise. The collar-recovery claim of the
spacetime paper~\cite{ophEinstein} bounds the
defect on its conditional mixing branch by an exponential envelope, which we
write in the local record density \(\rho\) as
\begin{equation}
\eta_\delta\le\kappa\,B\,
\exp\!\Bigl(-\frac{\delta\rho^{1/3}-r_0}{\zeta}\Bigr),
\label{eq:envelope}
\end{equation}
with \(\kappa\) the mixing prefactor, \(B\) the boundary size in UV cells,
\(r_0\) an offset, \(\zeta\) the decay constant, and \(\delta\rho^{1/3}\)
the collar depth counted in UV record spacings.

\begin{theorem}[Compact-source saturation]
\label{thm:saturation}
Under the envelope \eqref{eq:envelope}, fix common parameters
\(\kappa\geq0\), \(B\geq1\), \(r_0\geq0\), and \(\zeta>0\).
Along a family of collars with fixed radius \(\ell>0\) and uniformly bounded
nonnegative collar constant \(C_C\), the recovery defect and anomalous stress
tend to zero as the depth tends to infinity at fixed positive record density,
or as the record density tends to infinity at fixed positive depth.
Quantitatively,
\begin{equation}
\bigl|\delta\langle T_{00}^{\rm anom}\rangle\bigr|
\le\frac{15\,C_C}{8\pi^2\ell^4}\,\kappa B\,
\exp\!\Bigl(-\frac{\delta\rho^{1/3}-r_0}{\zeta}\Bigr),
\label{eq:rate}
\end{equation}
For \(\kappa B>0\) and \(\varepsilon>0\), the defect envelope bound falls
below \(\varepsilon\) exactly when
\(\delta\rho^{1/3}>r_0+\zeta\log(\kappa B/\varepsilon)\).
For \(\kappa=0\), the defect and stress vanish at every admissible depth.
Thus the bounded stress tends to zero in these fixed-parameter limits.
\end{theorem}

The delimitation is exact in both directions and machine-checked. The
identically zero defect satisfies every envelope hypothesis, so the envelope
never forces a nonzero defect: that the galactic-scale settled cuts carry an
order-one defect is a premise, not a consequence. And an explicit sparsely
recorded instance sits at the bound with an order-one defect and strictly
positive stress, so the same hypotheses that screen dense regions admit a
nonzero dark source on sparse cuts. What is not established is that physical
collars satisfy \eqref{eq:envelope}: the envelope is the conditional mixing
branch of the collar-recovery claim, taken here as a hypothesis.

In the erasure example at the start of this section, randomizing the
predictor leaves the same minimum error. A saturated record recovers
the bit exactly, and the defect falls below \(\varepsilon\) precisely
when \(p>1-2\varepsilon\). With unit collar radius and collar constant, and anomalous energy
equal to the model defect, the unrecorded instance carries stress
\(15/(16\pi^2)\) exactly. The identification of the model
defect with \(\eta_\delta\) is a modeling premise, and the record density
of a physical galactic-scale cut is not specified; the model shows the
mechanism class exists, with an exact defect-density law.

Under Premise~\ref{pre:scale} the Solar System is a test of the ambient
density alone.

\begin{proposition}[An isotropic ambient density has no quadrupole]
\label{prop:isotropic}
The potential of a locally uniform density is \((2\pi G\rho_A/3)\,r^2\). Its
projection on the quadrupole weight \(P_2(\cos\theta)\) vanishes,
\(\int_{-1}^{1}(\mu^2-\tfrac13)\dd\mu=0\), so the quadrupole coefficient
\(Q_2\) is zero. The only footprint is the isotropic tidal scale
\(4\pi G\rho_A/3\).
\end{proposition}

With the local dark density
\(\rho_A\simeq6.8\times10^{-22}\,\mathrm{kg\,m^{-3}}\), the value inferred
from Galactic kinematics~\cite{desalas2021}, the tidal scale is
\begin{equation}
\frac{4\pi G\rho_A}{3}=1.9\times10^{-31}\,\mathrm{s^{-2}},
\label{eq:tidal}
\end{equation}
For a dimensional scale comparison, the Cassini trace-free quadrupole result
is \(Q_2=(1.6\pm1.8)\times10^{-27}\,\mathrm{s^{-2}}\)~\cite{park2026}, four
orders of magnitude above the isotropic scale. It is not a direct bound on
the traceful isotropic channel, whose quadrupole is identically zero. The
anomalous mass enclosed within ten astronomical units is
\(5\times10^{-15}\,M_\odot\). Both numbers are machine-checked inequalities
in the formalization. The Solar System constrains the premise of
Section~\ref{sec:solar} and nothing else in the construction.

\paragraph{Independent curvature and collar measurements.}
A controlled test of the geometric excess uses a six-mode quantum scalar
with a declared semiclassical ADM coupling. Withhold the two $z$ modes
from the ordinary field detector. At one fixed time, compare a ground
and a $z$-excited source on geodesic rest-frame balls with common
proper volume $4\pi\ell^3/3$. Measure the area at that volume, convert
its deficit to $G_{uu}$, difference the two preparations, and subtract
the measured changes in the other modes using a coupling calibrated
on separate states. At $\ell=0.1$ the inferred excess density is
$0.00674881$ in box units. The independent reserved meter reads
$0.00674591$. A $y$ excitation gives a small negative reserved change,
$-0.00005782$, and the independent geometric procedure gives
$-0.00005481$. This tests the inference of a withheld source when all
modes gravitate universally. The omitted modes are ordinary scalar
matter in this experiment. The collar operator and its proper-radius
scaling are additional identifications in Eq.~\eqref{eq:tanom}.

A second box experiment tests how a correlation can carry gravitational
energy while all individual field records agree. Two real fields $A,B$ have
an interaction density $\lambda\langle AB\rangle$. Prepare opposite
cross-field covariances at identical single-field reduced states and
initial scale factors. A flat-box frequency splitting measures $\lambda$.
The gravity coefficient comes from the earlier one-field calibration. In
box units the individual quadratures predict a change of $0.00000020$ in
$G_{uu}$. Including the independently measured covariance gives
$0.00296540$. Radar gives $0.00296837$ and the rim areas at common proper
volume with $\ell=0.1$ give $0.00296947$. Removing the interaction makes
both geometric records exactly identical across the preparations. In this
semiclassical source the field Hamiltonian specifies how the correlation
enters the stress. Applying the result to a collar requires an independent
determination of the stress carried by its modular charge and a measured
response of the same geometry to ordinary and collar stress.

In a separate homogeneous comparison, clocks publish a joint bit that
sets the density of a second population of parallel strings. Its conserved
tension and energy gravitate at the ordinary coupling in the supplied
rod geometry~\cite{ophEinstein}. Three exchanges with a local fair-bit
reservoir select the same material entropy at fixed stock even from
correlated initial memories. The resulting metric has constant
$g_{tt}$ and vanishing $g_{ti}$. A probe released from rest has zero
initial spatial acceleration there. Predicting a galaxy's circular
speed requires a source profile and a measured attractive response
in the same geometry.

\paragraph{Light and freely falling probes outside the same body.}
A spherical material tests attraction due to coincidences that are
invisible in each observer's separate clock counts. Two classical
records published by Interval Echo supply an ordinary count of $1/3$.
A change in the joint frequency from zero to $8/33$ preserves both
single frequencies. The modification assigns each counted unit
pressureless rest mass, gives it the same radial inverse-square
coupling, and specifies local Lorentz propagation relative to the
falling material. A probe released on the ordinary body calibrates
$G=1/4$ in the source units. Freezing that calibration predicts how
the additional joint mass changes two readings. Between stationary
spheres at area radii two and four, the received-to-emitted frequency
ratio changes from $\sqrt{22/23}$ to $\sqrt{226/245}$. At radius two
the signed second derivative of the released probe's area radius
with respect to its proper time changes from $-1/48$ to $-19/528$.
Independent simulated clock and release records give
$GM=0.143939394$ for the held source. Making light blind to coincidences
leaves its inferred $GM$ at $0.083333333$ while the released probe
detects the full mass~\cite{ophEinstein}.

This body supports a regional entropy test throughout its homogeneous
interior. Paired clocks emit individually fair signs whose joint
frequencies depend on the conformal time between their local readings.
With a common blank register, their regional entropy gives
$2\pi C_\ell(T^{\rm ord}+T^{\rm joint})_{uu}+O(\ell^5)$ on shrinking
proper balls in moving frames, where $C_\ell=4\pi\ell^4/15$.
The clock law specifies this material response. Omitting the joint
stock from its sign probabilities leaves a leading discrepancy with
the independently measured area~\cite{ophEinstein}.

The original source posts admit a separate modular read. At fixed
single-post frequencies the four probability changes, in order
$00,01,10,11$, are $(d,-d,-d,d)$. With the archived source reference
$(12,10,3,8)/33$, the change of its modular expectation is $d\log(5/16)$.
The ordinary-to-held comparison gives $-0.281976$ nats, recovering
$d=8/33$ units of counted mass at that calibration. The reference
used by the regional paired-clock apparatus is a separate measurement.
At the independently calibrated ordinary coupling, this source read
predicts the ten components reconstructed from rod records. The
largest absolute discrepancy falls from $2.094\times10^{-5}$ at radius
$1/512$ to $8.179\times10^{-8}$ at $1/8192$ in the model units.

On the 81-word overlap detector, equal single-station frequencies
allow two independent correlation responses. Write $K_{ij}=-\log q_{ij}$
for its opposite-reference cost, and subtract its first row and column
as $D_{ij}=K_{ij}-K_{i0}-K_{0j}+K_{00}$. One coincidence from
independent local encodings would give
$D_{ij}=g(a_i-a_0)(b_j-b_0)$ for its gain $g$ and a zero determinant
on every two-by-two minor. The calibrated class minor lies in
$[0.00169740,0.00169745]$. Two independently counted binary settings,
one for each remaining class row, recover the modular change at fixed
marginals. Assigning the resulting weighted count a conserved
gravitating stress needs an independent physical identification.
A second instrument measures the mixed work of inserting ordinary loads
at the two ends of a native seam, $E_{11}-E_{10}-E_{01}+E_{00}$.
On the resolved marks this gives $0,-32,-33$ in native energy units;
the blank seam gives $-17$. Four contacts distinguish the nine local
words. Correlations of these work readings at two stations recover the
shared-source modular change $\log(169/105)/144=0.00330513$ nats.
An independent experiment releases two ordinary loads on the bridge
between neighboring source cells. At one native time unit the original
shared source changes the probability of joint return by about
$-5.0074\times10^{-8}$ relative to the fresh source. Removing only the
measured binding on the marked seams changes that contrast by about
$1.90\times10^{-10}$ in magnitude. The native binding therefore
contributes to free propagation. A single arrival or return probability
also depends on passability and ordinary scattering. Two sources with
the same full reference-cost histogram and hence the same modular
charge give distinct through-going material transfer probabilities in
the exterior at time 16. Both exterior clocks retain the same gaps.
Thus the scalar charge alone does not fix that propagation observable.
A comparison at the original positive shared source keeps each
station's complete record distribution, the joint modular classes
and mixed-work flags, and all 900 cross-seam energy products fixed.
Seven of 190 station pairs change their relay-time distribution.
The unit-time joint return of the two ordinary loads changes by
$-4.3601\times10^{-7}$. Spatially resolved data therefore carry
source information beyond the equal modular read. This test uses
finite propagation probabilities on the native board.%
\footnote{Matched controls and exact short-time coefficient in
\nolinkurl{openwave/interval_echo/row_19/selected_collar/matched_charge_transport.json}.}
A control preserves all polynomial moments of the raw seam energies
through degree three while changing the charge by $0.00231338$ nats.
This identifies a measurable local material response through which
observers can read the source. Each one-station distribution is fixed
in the control. A gravitational explanation must determine how the
joint response enters the regional stress seen by independent clocks.%
\footnote{Derivation and replay in
\nolinkurl{openwave/interval_echo/row_19/selected_collar/parked_energy_charge.py}.}

There is an independent abundance test for the spherical construction.
The same-service coincidence $LR$ can carry at most the unit ordinary
traveller mass $L$ on every binary record, even after nonnegative
aggregation across regions. Eleven of 33 archived addresses have a
traveller and eight have a coincidence, giving $M_c/M_o=8/11$.
If the ordinary stock represents all baryons and these coincidences
supply the entire extra pressureless component, the ceiling is one.
On the fixed repair-cost vacuum branch with a Planck-calibrated drag
ruler and BBN baryon density, DESI's radial acoustic distance at
$z=2.33$ requires a ratio of $5.39$ at the central inputs. More
generally write $(D_H/r_d)^{-2}=A s^3+K s^2+B+C s^4$, $s=1+z$.
Linear combinations of three radial readings can eliminate both
the constant curvature and vacuum amplitudes while retaining $A$.
Simultaneous 96\% bands on the six radial DESI DR2 measurements, at
$r_d\le147.87$ Mpc, $\omega_b\le0.02383$, $\omega_\nu\le0.003$
and $\omega_r\le0.00005$, require at least $1.234$ additional
pressureless mass units per baryon. The coverage is conditional on
this external envelope and on the dust, radiation, curvature and
constant-vacuum expansion law. The sharpest triple is at
$z=0.510,0.934,2.33$. Even allowing arbitrary coincidence frequencies
on this unit-weight source fails the astronomical mass budget. A
different source of gravitating stress or a different relation between
ordinary posts and baryonic matter would change the comparison.%
\footnote{\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14738}{DESI DR2 distance measurements};
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.06209}{Planck drag ruler}.
All twenty contrasts and the source census are in
\nolinkurl{model/candidate_laws/interval_echo/openwave/row_28_acceleration/collar_abundance.json}.}

The same inventory can be tested without a calibrated drag ruler.
Six DESI radial acoustic distances and three absolute chronometer
rates from stellar ages constrain $P(x)=(H/100)^2$ at $x=1+z$,
with $H$ in km/s/Mpc. Write
$P(x)=\omega_m x^3+\omega_k x^2+\omega_\Lambda+\omega_r x^4$ and
$q=(c/(100r_d))^2$. A distance interval $[l,u]$ gives
$q/u^2\le P\le q/l^2$; an age-rate interval $[L,U]$ gives
$(L/100)^2\le P\le(U/100)^2$. Use $2.774$ marginal standard
deviations on all nine readings. Their joint Gaussian-marginal
coverage exceeds 95\% by the union bound even with unknown
cross-survey correlations. With the preceding baryon, neutrino and
radiation caps and nonnegative vacuum, minimizing over the allowed
coefficients on a flat background gives $\omega_m\ge0.05728571$.
Thus the required extra-to-baryon ratio exceeds $1.278$, with $r_d$
free. The active data boundaries are the radial bins at $0.934$ and
$2.33$ and the lower age rate at $0.67$. If spatial curvature is
free, the lower ratio falls to $0.188$. Keeping the unit-ticket
ceiling then requires $\omega_k\ge0.03144972$ in the convention
where positive $\omega_k$ denotes open geometry. These are
conditional background bounds; stellar-age calibration and the
connection from native material to gravitational dust enter separately.%
\footnote{\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.02730}{S.~I.~Loubser, cosmic chronometers,
Eqs.~4--6, including correlated systematic uncertainties};
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14738}{DESI DR2 radial distances}. Exact certificates and their input intervals are in \nolinkurl{code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/astronomical_budget.py}.}

The spatial assignment faces a separate test. At 1,288 outer rings in
136 SPARC galaxies, the fixed $19/11$ rescaling of the visible squared
speed templates conflicts with 40 galaxies even when every measured speed
may move by five quoted errors. Giving each galaxy independently chosen
nonnegative multipliers on gas, disk and bulge leaves 23 conflicts.
An additional allowance of 5 km/s at each ring leaves seven. Adding a
spherical squared-speed term $H+C/r$, with $H\ge0$ and nonnegative
enclosed mass on the measured annulus, leaves two conflicts at five
quoted errors. This comparison allows separate halo parameters for
each galaxy. It tests a radial law with free normalization.
In NGC~1003 both visible templates fall between 14.49 and 26.08 kpc
while the measured speed rises from $97.20\pm1.30$ to
$110.00\pm2.40$ km/s. Under these five-error bounds an additional
inward component supplies at least $34.51\%$ of the outer radial
acceleration. A further independent allowance of 10 km/s at each
ring reduces the bound to $2.36\%$. These are deterministic interval
tests of the catalogued tracer shapes. Warps and tracer-dependent
systematics require independent measurements.%
\footnote{\href{https://doi.org/10.3847/0004-6256/152/6/157}{F.~Lelli,
S.~S.~McGaugh and J.~M.~Schombert, SPARC mass models and rotation curves}. The full sample and certificates replay in \nolinkurl{code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/finite_halo/tracer_shapes.py}.}

The finite body gives a decreasing exterior orbital speed. An extended
galaxy requires a radial distribution of gravitating correlations that
can be inferred independently of its measured rotation. The additional
source in this experiment is a measured joint frequency assigned mass by
the law. Equating it with the anomalous modular charge of the galaxy
model requires a separate energy and occupation measurement. Telescope
distance does not enter the spherical experiment or its force law.

\paragraph{A measured correlation energy and its gravitational coupling.}
\label{par:seam-energy-test}
Which part of a joint record is physical energy? For a finite pair
with Hamiltonian $h$ choose a faithful thermal reference
$\sigma=e^{-\beta h}/Z$ restricted to the admitted product sector.
Set $K=-\log\sigma+\log(\sigma_A\otimes\sigma_B)$.
If two preparations have identical marginal density matrices, their
difference annihilates both local logarithms and the constant $\log Z$.
Consequently $\Delta\langle K\rangle/\beta=\Delta\langle h\rangle$.
The choice of physical Hamiltonian and inverse temperature fixes the
units before any geometric measurement.

There is an interacting example on the 99-state repair seam. Two even
memory words of magnitudes 0 and 2 and two endpoint-load words
$(2,0),(1,1)$ give four exact eigenwords with energy rows $(0,0)$ and
$(32,0)$. The high state puts probabilities $3/8$ on each off-diagonal
word and $1/8$ on each diagonal word. Interchanging these probabilities
gives the low state. Every one-register distribution stays uniform,
and the two mean energies are 12 and 4. Reversing the coin phase gives
energy rows $(0,0),(32,32)$ and means 16 and 16. These statements
follow by acting on the full native contact matrix.

Place this internal energy homogeneously in a comoving cell with
volume $W$ and supply a semiclassical flat Einstein geometry. The
energy is independent of strain, hence its pressure is zero.
With $q=\log a$ and $W=V_0e^{3q}$, the canonical constraint reads
$-\kappa p^2/(12W)+\Lambda W/\kappa+E=0$ and
$\dot q=-\kappa p/(6W)$. Eliminating $p$ gives the exact identity
\[
 \Delta[W(G_{00}-\Lambda)]
   =\kappa\Delta E=\kappa\,\Delta\langle K\rangle/\beta .
\]
Here $G_{00}=3\dot q^2$ comes from the changing metric. The source
appears once in the total energy. A vacuum and an ordinary one-load
preparation give $\kappa=1.60000105$ by radar. The interacting states
give integrated curvature differences $12.79997$--$12.80000$ at three
held appointments. Independent thermometer and joint-state counts give
$7.95234\pm0.02032$ energy units (one counting standard error), or
$12.72375\pm0.03251$ in curvature units. The exact forecast is 12.8.
Both reversed-coin geometries agree. Keeping the interacting
Hamiltonian and the ordinary calibration while coupling gravity to the
same charge 8 in all four words also gives zero curvature difference.
These two gravitational laws give identical source counts.
The physical repair seam supplies the matter operator. The homogeneous
metric, its constraint and the choice of gravitating charge supply the
gravitational law. Spectral and clock data are in
\path{code/matched_einstein_limit/seam_overlap/}.

The corresponding test for a \emph{spatial} collar uses both
measurements on the same rest-frame balls. At common proper
volume $4\pi\ell^3/3$ the measured difference in temporal curvature is
estimated by $-15\Delta A_\ell/(4\pi\ell^4)$ from the rim areas.
At matched ordinary stress, inserting Eq.~\eqref{eq:tanom} shows that
the estimated residual tends to zero if and only if
\[
 \Delta A_\ell+\frac{\kappa}{2\pi}\Delta E_{C,\ell}=o(\ell^4).
\]
Thus positive collar charge requires a \emph{smaller} rim at the same
volume. In natural units the coefficient $\kappa/(2\pi)=4G_N$ is
fixed by ordinary matter. The identification with physical curvature
requires a controlled geodesic-ball expansion in the measured frame.
It is the regional charge and this area difference that must be read
independently; the thermal energy of a whole cell supplies no regional
area measurement.

For a controlled comparison give the homogeneous seam density
difference $\Delta E/V_0$ the leading diamond weight
$8\pi^2\ell^4/15$ when both cells have volume $V_0=(2\pi)^3$.
Send independent spacelike geodesic rulers from the central observer,
adjust each aperture to the same measured interior volume, then read
its rim on a separate angular grid. The couplings inferred from the two
rim areas and the exact source energy are $1.62320,1.60575,1.60143$
at radii $0.4,0.2,0.1$. The ordinary radar calibration is $1.60000105$.
At radius $0.1$ the excess energy reduces the area by
$4.32691\times10^{-6}$ in box units. Using the finite thermal counts
gives a leading decrease of $(4.29729\pm0.01098)\times10^{-6}$,
with counting error only. Holding the gravitational charge fixed gives
identical rims. This survey supplies the regional weight and reads
the areas independently. Identifying that weight with the native
spatial modular charge is the physical source premise.

Two native tests give different outcomes from the homogeneous example.
In a guarded annulus the timing routes avoid the two marked words.
Changing their correlation preserves every clock range but changes the
charge read by a separate word instrument. On the $327{,}680$-cell board
the lower bounds on $\Delta K/(2\pi\ell^4)$ are $0.001298$ and
$0.0002452$ for globally shared and spatially grouped tags, respectively,
at simultaneous $99\%$ coverage within each calibrated count family.
Here $\ell$ is the relative routed radius. Any area constructed solely
from those invariant ranges has zero change.

A second native experiment uses two material rulers that respond to the
words. On one transfer preserving both tetrahedral volumes, the source
charge increases by $0.161753$ nats per unit transfer, while the two
areas \emph{increase} by $7.25151\times10^{-8}$ and
$2.82552\times10^{-7}$ in their respective squared clock units.
The full single-word marginals and mean stored energy are fixed.
Any area increasing in both readings also fails the required sign.
The area derivatives follow from the conditional flight laws. Direct
clock counts do not resolve their small finite differences, while a
separate word measurement resolves the positive charge. These
measurements exclude the indicated metric and stress assignments.
Their regional algebras and tetrahedra have no established
identification with the proper geodesic balls of the Einstein argument.
The finite range comparisons give no asymptotic lower bound on the residual
along a sequence of physical balls.

There is an exact restriction on such an identification in free repair
dynamics. Each of two private seams has three antisymmetric memory
words of energies $0,32,33$ that act as scalars on all nine endpoint
load inputs. For fixed labels $i,j$ their only effect on all remaining
degrees of freedom is a phase. Every material clock or flight instrument
restricted to those remaining degrees has the same outcome law for
every distribution of the labels. The result extends through successive
classically selected Hamiltonian contacts by conditioning on each material record.
A direct read of the private memories can distinguish their joint
distribution.

For example, put $P_{ij}(t)=(1+tT_{ij})/9$ with $T_{01}=T_{12}=T_{20}=1$,
$T_{ji}=-T_{ij}$ and $T_{ii}=0$. For $|t|<1$ both marginals and the
complete total-energy histogram are fixed. Pair the labels with
probability $f$, otherwise read two independent labels, and discard the
pairing bit. At fixed reference $P(ft_0)$ the signed slope of the
joint modular expectation relative to the product is
$-(f/3)\log[(1+ft_0)/(1-ft_0)]$. Even if $f=a\ell^2+o(\ell^2)$
for positive $a$, Eq.~\eqref{eq:tanom} gives a finite nonzero slope
$-5t_0a^2/(4\pi^2)$ for $0<t_0<1$. The identical material records
give zero geometric slope. This rules out the indicated correlated
reference as a temporal source for that instrument with unchanged
ordinary stress, at any nonzero coupling. The Gibbs reference of the
additive protected two-seam Hamiltonian factorizes.

A boundary clock gives a sign test for a regional source. Take the local
geodesic rest slice and a smooth, strictly convex, nontrapping ball. Its
incoming spatial geodesics have a mean length \(\bar L\) to first exit,
weighted by inward normal flux. Integrating the conserved geodesic phase
volume from the boundary gives \(4\pi V=\pi A\bar L\): each unit tangent
sphere has solid angle \(4\pi\), and the projected solid angle of the inward
hemisphere is \(\pi\). At equal proper volume \(V=4\pi\ell^3/3\),
\[
 \Delta A=-\frac{4V}{\bar L_0\bar L_1}\Delta\bar L,\qquad
 \Delta\bar L=\frac{\kappa}{6\pi^2\ell}\Delta K+o(\ell^3)
\]
where the second relation is the predicted collar response for matched
ordinary stress, \(\Delta K=O(\ell^4)\), and a common positive coupling.
It follows by using \(\bar L_i=(4\ell/3)[1+O(\ell^2)]\) and
\(\Delta A=-\kappa\Delta K/(2\pi)+o(\ell^4)\). At fixed volume a positive
collar charge must lengthen the mean chord. The chord units, flux and
conversion from physical flight time to geodesic length are independent
apparatus inputs.

On the declared uniform triangular refinement, protected clock paths and
their boundary exits have identical distributions for shared and fresh
word tags. The ideal reversed-tag reference gives a positive joint-admission
contrast \(c=\sum(P_0-P_-)\log(P_0/P_-)\ge101/33696\) nats, with \(P_0\)
the product of the common marginals and \(P_-\) the reversed-tag law. In
full routed balls with \(M\) of \(N\) stations, the shared-minus-fresh
contrast is \(cM(M-1)/[N(N-1)]\). The ball population has a uniform lower
bound \(M/N\ge\alpha\ell_{\rm route}^2\) for some \(\alpha>0\) on
mesoscopic shrinking balls, from the bounded cost of traversing a dyadic
triangular ancestor. Here \(\ell_{\rm route}\) uses the greatest station
range as the unit. The signed discrepancy per fourth power of that radius
therefore persists under refinement. This excludes the tested charge and
protected route geometry as a source pair at positive coupling. It identifies
neither the source charge nor a proper length for distant galaxies.

Energy alone also fails to ensure visibility to a particular ruler.
A single protected memory has two zero-energy coin states at
magnitude zero and two antisymmetric-coin reversal parities at
magnitude two. Regard the magnitude as one bit and the within-pair
label as another, ordering the energies $(0,0)$ and $(33,32)$.
The same $3/8,1/8$ preparation pair has a thermal correlation energy
difference $(33-32)/4=1/4$. Every endpoint-load probe has identical
records because each word acts by a scalar at all loads. On $N$
such seams the energy contrast is $N/4$ and all joint material
records coincide. If their common proper volume grows as $Nv+o(N)$
with $v>0$, the limiting density contrast is $1/(4v)$. These two
bits are logical registers inside one memory. Identifying a spatial
collar requires a separate choice of regional algebra and metric
instrument.

In the native two-write experiment compare tetrahedra formed from the
terminal positions of four balanced material tracers in the same
parked collar word. The source provides a common history for the four
flights. Its shared and reversed signs have opposite connected
score products and the same ordinary energy law as fresh writes.
In native chord coordinates, ordinary occupancy changes release
focusing $-V''/V_0$ by $-27.65519$ per squared exposure.
Changing fresh to shared tags gives an exact change of zero, measured
as $-0.00395\pm0.06467$ (joint $99\%$ radius conditional on the clock).
The tetrahedron volume is affine in each tracer coordinate. At
release the balanced rays have zero current, and each remaining
acceleration is affine in contact energies with identical means.
After two exposures the shared and reversed preparations give
normalized volume changes $(-5.833\pm1.756)\times10^{-6}$ and
$(+5.776\pm1.756)\times10^{-6}$ relative to fresh writes. These
finite-time measurements resolve the retained collar correlation.
Physical Ricci focusing requires calibrated geodesic tracers, and
anomalous stress requires the independently normalized fourth-order
collar coefficient on shrinking proper balls. The measured chord-volume
difference supplies neither calibration.

\paragraph{A correlation generated by mobile matter.}
Prepare two independent seam memories on opposite sides of an intercell
bridge. A freely moving vacancy or excess encounters them on a unit-filled
federation with every other seam open. After four exposures the measured
coin covariances for the two source signs are $-0.001211$ and $-0.001149$
on twenty cells, and $+0.053798$ and $+0.069606$ on eighty cells.
The simultaneous $99\%$ radius is $0.0000166$. Uniform matter preserves the
initial product. The free generator also preserves the projector onto a
blank seam, so preparing the two active memories is a physical input.

Discard the original defect without selecting an outcome and release a
fresh defect from the far side of the bridge. Compare the generated
memories with independent memories drawn from different formation runs,
matching both local states and all prepared loads. After four exposures
the home-cell arrival probability falls by $0.000121$ to $0.000181$ on
twenty cells and rises by $0.000753$ to $0.001013$ on eighty cells across
the four source and probe sign choices. The joint $99\%$ radius is
$0.00000277$. Independent detector calibrations and measured Pauli
covariances predict these scattering differences.

A fresh vacancy or excess distributed uniformly over all $N$ ports tests a
different prediction. Any unitary propagation on that position space, or
classical mixture of such propagations, preserves the normalized identity
and gives $1/N$ for each rank-one detector. At twenty and eighty cells all
144 near-bridge readings for the freely formed memories resolve a change
from $1/N$. Of these, 115 also distinguish the correlated memories from
the product of their two measured marginals with simultaneous $99\%$
coverage. The distant station resolves neither contrast. The material
therefore transfers information locally between probe and collar. A
geometric account of the full measured channel would have to include
additional dynamical degrees of freedom. The gravitational component of
any such response requires independent stress and curvature measurements.

Let $p$ be the four-outcome sharp reference measured in independent copies
at the formation exposure and $q=p_Ap_B$ its independent marginal product.
The source-minus-independent expectation of $-\log(p/q)$ is
$-D(p\Vert q)-D(q\Vert p)$, where $D$ denotes relative entropy.
All four calibration distributions have strictly positive cell frequencies
with simultaneous $99\%$ lower bounds. Their held mean-score differences
are $-0.0018193$, $-0.0026556$, $-0.1575958$, and $-0.1934187$ nats
in the same board and source order. This generates a measurable
two-memory reference from ordinary matter at a specified exposure.
A physical boost reference and a coupling to properly calibrated
geometric focusing require independent measurements.

\paragraph{A visible fourth-order response and its spatial source.}\label{par:visible-quartic-source}
On the 80-cell formed collar compare a material round trip with a
reference trip through independently prepared heads. Read which trip
completed, breaking a tie with a fair material bit. If the tested
heads come from the formed pair with probability $f$, the probability
of the comparison bit is $(1+fz)/2$, with measured completion contrast
$z=-0.01437037$. Weakening the paired fraction along
$f\mapsto(1-\epsilon)f$ gives the raw modular slope
$Q=fz\,\operatorname{atanh}(fz)=f^2z^2+O(f^4)$.
The independent preparation balances the reference bit without a fitted
offset. Reading the full transcripts defines a different observable.

Suppose one common spatial population of marks admits a pair when at
least one mark lies in the observer's proper ball. Let $M_\ell$ be
the expected total number of marks near a bounded three-dimensional
region. If the ball volumes obey $V(B_\ell)\le C\ell^3$, exchanging
the integration over centers and marks gives $\int f\,dV\le CM_\ell\ell^3$.
Since $|Q|\le Kf^2$, the volume on which
$|Q|/\ell^4\ge t>0$ is at most $CM_\ell\sqrt{K/t}\,\ell$.
A bounded mark budget therefore gives zero in volume measure. In a
supplied smooth chart, drawing a number of independent marks proportional
to $1/\ell$ per fixed chart volume attains this bound in reference units. A mark
walk from a compact injection, with equal probabilities for all six
neighbors of a cubic chart and absorption at its edge, produces a
mean visit density proportional to $1/r$ well inside that edge.
The quadratic comparison then gives the radial shape $1/r^2$.
Equal unsigned injection per baryonic unit gives a coefficient
proportional to $M_b^2$.

An explicit correlated source gives a different mass dependence.
Place a fair binary record on each shared link of a compact assembly
of screen cells. Its two owners assign opposite signs to the record.
Adding the signed records over a patch cancels every internal link.
For $b$ boundary links the sum $S_b$ has variance $b$. The
$N=L^2$ cells descended from one triangular face have exactly
$b=3L$ boundary links on the native spherical federation. Each
cell has per-ball admission probability $u=a(x)\ell^2+o(\ell^2)$.
Select one admitted cell symmetrically if any hits occur, use one of
its three links to orient the comparison, and communicate the majority
sign of the patch to orient the reported bit. Randomize a tied sign.
With a pairing reference $0<s_0<1$, the response to decreasing the
pairing parameter per unit change has leading coefficient
\[
 \lim_{\ell\to0}{Q\over\ell^4}
   ={s_0 z^2 a(x)^2\over9}\bigl(\mathbb E|S_b|\bigr)^2,
 \qquad
 {\bigl(\mathbb E|S_b|\bigr)^2\over b}\longrightarrow {2\over\pi}.
\]
Here the admission limit is taken at fixed patch size, followed by the
large-patch comparison. This produces a $\sqrt N/r^2$ dependence for
these compact triangular sources in the transport chart. It predicts
different signals for patches of the same area with different boundaries.
The communicated orientation matters: averaging over the signs before
orienting the bit makes it exactly uniform. If the alignment can access only $k$ independent boundary signs, the
greatest possible aligned mean is $\mathbb E|S_k|$. A single output
bit can therefore use fluctuations from the whole patch only if its
preparation actually receives information from order $b$ links. In a
joint admission and source-size limit with $Nu\to c>0$, the fraction
of the sparse forecast is $[(1-e^{-c})/c]^2$; it vanishes for
$Nu\to\infty$. Both shape and saturation distinguish this readout from
a universal function of baryonic mass alone. The source preparation,
communication of the bit, three-dimensional chart and identification
of cell count with baryonic mass are supplied. A physical prediction
requires their realization on the same observed source and a curvature
response at the ordinary coupling.
The calculation and its controls are in
\path{code/matched_einstein_limit/collar_identifiability/refinement/}.

The density entering this mechanism would be the local density of
physical records in and around the source. Moving a telescope farther from the same galaxy changes the received
flux. The post-emission choice of telescope distance leaves the source
records and orbital histories fixed in this construction.
Interpreting differences in local record recovery as extra gravity
requires that they predict both orbital and lensing measurements on
that source. Distance from the telescope alone supplies no such law.

\subsection{A conserved inverse-square distribution on a finite guide}
A controlled interaction in the same seam law gives a way to test
whether a radially extended signal actually carries conserved energy.
Place a mobile load on an even periodic cubic guide, with the source
address excluded. Let $L_*$ be the graph Laplacian on the remaining
sites and $P$ the projector onto the six nearest neighbours of the
excluded site. A parked source rail switches their contact potential.
Degree compensation restores the ordinary contrast energy on these
neighbours. In the occupied source arm the mobile generator in exchange
units is
\[
 H=48I+\tfrac12(L_*-2kP).
\]
The logical guide, source contact, energy reference and coherent
preparation are apparatus choices. In the translation-invariant two-load
construction the same reduced potential concerns pair separation.
Each individual position marginal in that construction is uniform.

Write $G(0)$ for the zero-mass on-site Green function of the infinite
cubic Laplacian. At the two-load scattering pole
$k_*=[12(G(0)-1/6)]^{-1}=0.9682678127$, with no galaxy data in the
calibration, every finite guide has a positive stationary ground state.
It is the periodic Green function of $L+m_n^2$ restricted to the
punctured guide. The six contact equations reduce to
$g(0)=(1+2k_*)g(e)$ for one unit neighbour $e$. The resolvent is
positive, the contact equation has a unique root and $m_n$ tends to
zero. On guide sides 64, 128, 256 and 512 the occupation ratios at a
chosen interior radius and twice that radius are respectively
3.966, 4.009, 3.983 and 3.993. In the unadjusted effective
contrast-eight interaction the side-64 ratio is $3.87\times10^9$ at
the same two radii. The scattering pole requires its specified control.

There is also a normalized limit on a fixed periodic volume. At
spacing $1/n$, $n m_n$ tends to a positive constant $\mu$ fixed by
the same contact. The discrete Fourier coefficients of the rescaled
field tend to $(4\pi^2|j|^2+\mu^2)^{-1}$ with a square-summable
majorant. Removing the source site costs $O(1/n)$ in squared norm.
The position densities converge in $L^1$ to the squared, normalized
periodic Yukawa Green function. Near the source this field is
$1/(4\pi r)+O(1)$ before normalization. Its enclosed occupation
grows linearly to leading order between the source and the box or
screening scale. Its amplitude is set by occupation and box size.

For any mobile state the symmetrized site energy
$\operatorname{Re}[\bar\psi_x(H\psi)_x]$ has an antisymmetric bond
current and sums to the conserved generator energy. In the stationary
mode it equals $(48-m_n^2/2)|\psi_x|^2$. Sharp positions and six
sets of disjoint hopping measurements distinguish packets with
identical occupations and different local energies. By comparison,
squaring the mean of a classically diffusing mark loses the
conditional variance at each step. A point pulse under the specified
lazy cubic walk loses $27/32$ of its summed squared mean in one step.
The coherent mode supplies a conserved material profile on this
guide. Its energy enters the matter budget once. A distinct overlap
contribution would require a response at fixed ordinary stress,
including its pressures and its coupling to the same measured geometry.
The finite calculations and fixed-volume derivation are in
\path{code/matched_einstein_limit/coherent_halo/}.

\subsection{Does a stored correlation gravitate at the ordinary coupling?}
Two head memories on an 80-cell sphere retain correlations from a
freely propagated excess. Combining the two heads from independent
preparations preserves their individual states and their joint
ordinary energy of 16. Reading the state through a separate material
probe detects the correlation. Four clock stations also resolve
changes in material transmission. A test of the gravitational
interpretation uses one preparation for both instruments, a small
ordinary exchange-energy addition for calibration, and a further
change in pairing whose strength is held out of that calibration.

To specify exactly which charge the observer can read, let $R$ be the
faithful reference state of the two heads. Let $P$ have its parity
probabilities and, within each parity sector, the product of its
one-head marginals. For endpoint preparations $\rho_+$ and $\rho_-$
with these same marginals and parity probabilities, the finite
connected modular variation in nats is
\[
  \Delta Q_{\rm pair}
    = {\rm Tr}\big[(\rho_+-\rho_-)(\log P-\log R)\big].
\]
The marginal terms in $\log P$ cancel in this trace. Eighteen joint
Pauli readings therefore suffice for the difference, although they
do not fix the operator on arbitrary source variations. The entropy
first law gives a geometric prediction before assigning a mass to this
score. For a tangent $\dot\rho$ with fixed marginals,
$\dot S_{\rm heads}=-{\rm Tr}(\dot\rho\log R)
={\rm Tr}[\dot\rho(\log P-\log R)]$. If this is the varying part of
the regional entropy and generalized entropy is stationary at fixed
proper volume, the area must respond as
$\eta_*\dot A=-\dot Q_{\rm pair}$ with the coefficient $\eta_*$
measured on ordinary matter. An independently surveyed area can
test this equality. The strongest
positive direction in these readings at fixed first-order survey
volume was selected using source data. Its predicted endpoint
difference is $+0.00022946$ nats. Fresh simulated counts give
$+0.00022940\pm0.00003336$ nats at simultaneous 99\% coverage.
The one-head distributions and parked energy agree between endpoints.
This establishes a signed modular variation of a specified overlap.
Identifying it with the cap remainder in Eq.~\eqref{eq:split}
requires a physical regional reference and a bulk boost. Identifying
that remainder with gravity also requires the independent response.
On a separate held correlation, two probes with their own clocks and
receiver rulers give relative image-area changes of $+0.005608$ and
$-0.009164$, each normalized to that probe's ordinary-matter response.
Interchanging the clocks preserves the sign disagreement. These
measurements are in \path{openwave/interval_echo/row_19/selected_collar/}
and \path{openwave/interval_echo/row_19/screen_rulers/}.

A recovery control on the same preparations distinguishes two proposed
identifications of the charge. Supply parity and the first head to a
receiver. Transmit the second head with probability $p$ and otherwise
replace it by its reference marginal in the same parity sector. With
the routing flag discarded, the reconstructed reference is
$R_p=pR+(1-p)P$. Its trace-distance defect is
$(1-p)\lVert R-P\rVert_1/2$. At $p=0$ the recovered-minus-actual
logarithmic score is exactly $\log P-\log R$. At $p=1$ that score
and its defect vanish identically, while a separate reading of the
original connected variation gives $+0.00022939\pm0.00000472$ nats
(simultaneous 99\% counting interval). The recovery operation and
accessible registers were specified for this comparison. It establishes
when the two scores coincide and when they differ on the source.
Identifying the recovery operation physically available on a galactic
cut remains a separate measurement.

On the same physical preparation, a scalar linear response calibrated
with ordinary energy writes predicts a held-to-ordinary image slope ratio
between $0.03502$ and $0.03651$. Two independently clocked material probes
give ratios near $-0.01848$ and $-0.12370$. There is also an exact
position test. In each reversal sector the freely released load has
generator $H=H_0+L+R_c$, with head-independent $H_0$ and separate
head contacts $L,R_c$ on disjoint port pairs. For every fixed diagonal
position reading $X$, the cross terms $[L,[R_c,X]]$ and $[R_c,[L,X]]$
vanish. The release acceleration of any independently prepared probe
therefore depends on the two individual head marginals. Correlation
changes at fixed marginals leave that acceleration unchanged.

This statement also applies when a freely passing load produces the
correlation. Remove that load without conditioning on its arrival and
compare the formed state $F$ with the product $P=F_L\otimes F_R$.
Independent sharp-head counts bound the increase of
$\langle\log P-\log F\rangle$ on going from $F$ to $P$ from below by
$0.15755$ nats (vacancy formation) and $0.19338$ nats (excess formation),
at 99\% simultaneous simulated counting coverage. Each bound follows
from monotonicity of both terms in $D(P\Vert F)+D(F\Vert P)$ under
the sharp measurement. The difference of the initial accelerations of two
independent rays is unchanged in every fixed port-position coordinate.
Delayed relative motion is sensitive to correlations. On the calibrated
comparison of four ray pairs, all eight delayed responses at two clock units
exclude the single-source forecast. As a detector control, supply a
potential proportional to squared material radius times the indicator
of a mismatch of the two sharp records. Both probe species then exhibit
the same signed relative acceleration on each exterior pair, and
reversing the potential reverses that acceleration. The joint coupling
is an input to this control. These tests concern finite-board position readings and two source preparations.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/openwave/interval_echo/row_19/recovery_identity}{Source contrasts, position histograms and independent propagation checks.}}

The source can also propagate through material. A fresh mobile excess visits
the sending pair and a third head in another cell. After eight exchange times
we trace out the load and both senders. The receiving head retains a change
in its sharp mean between each freely formed pair and its re-paired control.
Closing the receiver's external links during delivery suppresses the change.
Two fresh probe species then read relative radial acceleration there. Their
equal-weight mean on the nearer rails has exact release operator
