$-14.75I-X$ on the receiving coin, where $X$ is its first Pauli component.
On the farther rails this operator is $-14.5I$ and its contrast vanishes.
The nearer shared response can be resolved. For the separately calibrated
correlation intervention its ratio to the ordinary slope lies in
$[-0.02069,-0.01503]$ at release, and in $[-0.03544,-0.01207]$ from
independent terminal position counts. Both exclude the positive modular
forecast above with at least 98\% joint simulated counting coverage.
Allowing any fixed nonnegative relative weights for the two probe species
also fails across the two readings. The vacancy fraction compatible with
the calibration must lie in $[0.4928,0.5087]$ at release and in $[0.8356,1]$
for the terminal measurement, using the same simultaneous count bounds.
Here the transferred signal is physical within the native material law.
Its coupling to these position measurements fails the proposed calibration.

Two receiving qubits allow a read of their full joint state. A fixed
courier at a fixed appointment transmits at most fifteen independent
traceless coordinates to them. At fixed sender marginals and sector
weights, eighteen independent Pauli correlation coordinates can vary.
The transfer has a kernel of dimension at least three. For any unit
Hilbert--Schmidt direction $D$ in that kernel set
$P=3I_4/16\oplus I_4/16$ and $R=P+D/64$. Both admit mixtures of the
native product preparations. Their erasure contrast is
$D(P\Vert R)+D(R\Vert P)\ge\|P-R\|_1^2\ge1/4096$ nats, while every
local reading on the two destination memories has identical probabilities.
Here the erasure reference is fixed by $R$.
In an explicit numerically selected direction, two sender measurements
resolved the erasure contrast $0.003148083$ nats and a change of
$-0.000222908$ nats in the independent fixed-reference score.
Counting the courier's arrival retains further information. Its probability
at the first receiver differed by $8.92\times10^{-10}$ on this pair.

On the ordinary and held calibration stocks the two receiving cells
supported a radar survey after removal of the courier. All six directed
round trips on a three-port triangle included timeouts in the mean.
Of the four courier/probe sign combinations, one resolved the ordinary
area response. Its ratio was $[-0.02641,-0.01967]$, outside the
independent modular forecast $[0.03502,0.03651]$. The observable is a
triangle constructed from material clock ranges.


A material archive can alter the distant reading. Before launching the
courier, swap the right sending head into storage when a local witness
accepts it. An independent head with the same conditional marginal takes
its place. Conditional on reversal parity, the two sending heads then have
the product of their marginals, $P$. If no archive is available, the
original joint state $\rho$ reaches the courier. At failure fraction $f$
the transmitted source is $f\rho+(1-f)P$. The matched intervention
changes only joint records, hence its modular contrast is exactly $f$
times the contrast without storage. Complete storage makes the two
outgoing stocks identical. A change of access confined to discarded
registers after transmission preserves every receiver probability.

Apply the same route to the ordinary calibration. With one-contact
access, reducing active occupation from 0.32 to 0.08 increases the
computed area slope of the excess probe from $1.72\times10^{-8}$ to
$5.76\times10^{-8}$ in the finite radar units. The independently
calibrated modular prediction for the held-to-ordinary ratio at
occupation 0.08 is $+0.02575$ and the radar ratio is $-0.02286$.
The sign discrepancy holds at all six sampled occupation and reach
settings in the population calculation. Five of these six ordinary
area calibrations resolve it in the simulated count intervals. Thus
a physical path carries the dependence on access to a distant probe,
while the conversion calibrated on ordinary matter fails for this survey.

This failure can be bounded continuously. Let $G_o,G_h$ denote
ordinary and held area slopes and $K_o,K_h$ the corresponding modular
slopes. Each mean range is affine in $f$, and $K_h(f)=fK_h(1)$.
Each difference of the four linear side combinations in Heron's formula
between the held endpoints also has a factor $f$. Taking the difference
of the products before taking their square roots bounds $G_h(f)/f$
at arbitrarily small $f$. The simultaneous 99\% endpoint count intervals
on the excess-courier, excess-probe arm imply
$2.53\times10^{-8}<(G_h K_o-G_o K_h)/f<9.17\times10^{-8}$
for every $0<f\le 1$. The cross product would vanish for any common
linear coupling calibrated on ordinary matter. Thus adjusting the
failure rate alone cannot recover that coupling for this material survey.

A separate material strain measures the courier's work at the blank
bridge joining the sending cells. Fixed material index assigns energies
8 and 9 to its symmetric and antisymmetric modes relative to the
outside state. The work gives a paired-to-ordinary slope ratio of
$-0.93645$ for the excess courier, compared with the remote area ratio
$-0.02284$ without storage. There are also two mixtures with identical
individual sending-head states and identical symmetric, antisymmetric
and outside bridge probabilities. They give changes of
$+2.196\times10^{-10}$ and $-2.391\times10^{-10}$ in the vacancy and
excess round-trip ranges on the same route. To obtain the two mixtures,
weight three physical interventions by the normalized cross product
of their symmetric and antisymmetric probability differences and exchange
the endpoints for negative coefficients. Every function of the two
bridge probabilities agrees on these mixtures. The distant range can
depend on further physical records.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/openwave/interval_echo/row_19/recovery_identity}{Archive switch, contact work, complete survey banks and independent calculation.}}

For a binary return comparison, the calibration has the positive
sign. Across six clock protocols the area change of the surveyed
shape at matched volume supplies at most $0.602\%$ of the
response required by this entropy. The upper bound over positive
mixtures of twelve bridge protocols and the six shapes is $3.059\%$.
For the entropy of the complete retained memories, an ordinary
addition shrinks the raw inferred volume in all 54 interior-source
and clock comparisons. At directly matched volume $2.773791400$,
two preparations give an \emph{increase} in both area
($0.000011856$) and entropy ($0.000044065$ nats).
These quantities describe a radar tetrahedron with a known
reciprocity defect. They give finite tests of the proposed sign
and coupling while leaving open a properly reconstructed
spacetime ball with other physical records. The conserved profile
above and the correlation on this sphere are different sources.
The fixed-volume data appear in
\path{code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/intrinsic_area.json}.

There are preparations on which the two scalar responses do cancel.
Correlating independent or formed heads with sharp sign measurements
at rates $0.481396$ and $0.518604$ yields a local maximum of full
head entropy plus $\eta$ times the surveyed area, on the
three-parameter preparation family. On two independent, equal-volume
directions the ordinary exchange and fixed-energy pairing changes
give the same $\eta=31.1504$. A further admissible change selected
by the forming load's final address leaves all twelve ranges in that
clock menu and the ordinary energy fixed, while changing head entropy.
This independent variation distinguishes a deliberately prepared
scalar balance from a universal source of apparent gravity.

Allowing every two-head density variation gives a further selected
equilibrium on the finite apparatus. At a separately calibrated gain
$23.10424$, a positive mixture of native preparations has the same
surveyed volume and uniquely maximizes $S+\eta A$ over every enrolled
density matrix at that volume. The certified objective gap exceeds or
equals $0.14\|Y-X_*\|_1^2$ for any feasible $Y$. A second faithful,
preparable source at the identical volume gives
$7.47<d(S+\eta A)/dq<7.49$ nats per unit odd fraction $q$.
The volume alone does not select the equilibrium.
A coherent vacancy launched from ports 0 and 5 and recorded at port 38
also exhibits pairing-to-ordinary delay ratios of $0.3002$--$0.3050$
and $0.5212$--$0.5734$ across two frequencies of the same record.
This is an observable material response to the correlation. Its
interpretation as a common gravitational delay fails for those channels. The
four directed radar cycles depart from reciprocity by as much as
$1.54\%$, and a change in the clock timeout gives a nonzero
stationarity residual on that state. Even full stationarity of
one finite observation protocol leaves the identification of a
common proper metric and its response to propagating stress open.

Connected dynamics give a selection test. Unbiased short probe flights
relax both head coins to their sector traces, with the reversal
weight conserved. One member matches the same surveyed volume and
has zero joint $Z\otimes Z$ reading. The selected entropy-plus-area
maximum gives $0.460895$. A fixed-volume change of correlation
preserving each head and ordinary energy then has generalized-entropy
slope between $-0.004816$ and $-0.004815$ at the relaxed source.
At the selected source a separate pilot detects pairing through the
modulus of a coherent delayed hopping, with pairing-to-ordinary
response ratio $0.29682$--$0.29744$. The head entropy has ratio
$0.56432$--$0.56454$. Coherent scattering from the marked contacts is
visible to both material charges. At the same exterior blank port,
the calculated real return susceptibility at unit linewidth has
opposite signs for the two pairing responses after ordinary calibration.
These quantities give no calibration of extra galactic acceleration.

Repeated use of the actual four-clock radar also selects its own
long-run state. At the same volume, its joint coin correlation is
between 0.12435 and 0.12459. Two changes preserving both individual
heads and ordinary energy require disjoint area gains,
$[12.28,16.07]$ and $[18.35,24.73]$. Combining them gives an exact
local path preserving surveyed area and volume while the head
entropy changes. A physical correction would need information beyond
these fixed readings.

In fact all twelve directed mean ranges can be held exactly constant.
One reachable transfer increases entropy by more than
$1.55\times10^{-12}$ nats at a certified small step. A joint
$Y\otimes Y$ coincidence distinguishes it. Thus the missing
information can be read independently of the ranges.

The same perturbation, selected independently of force, changes the
four-tick material marked force for both carriers. The certified
slopes are about $1.2545\times10^{-8}$ and $4.8912\times10^{-7}$
in native units per source transfer. A claim of extra gravitational
acceleration would have to predict it from a separately reconstructed
metric.

The clocks retain the information that their mean ranges discard.
Thirty-one return-time probabilities, averaged over probe charge,
determine the entire two-head state including parity. They predict
both independently calibrated material forces in this sector. On a
different accessible perturbation, the twelve mean ranges and both
material forces are all exactly constant while entropy increases by
more than $1.004\times10^{-12}$ nats. A force inferred from the
complete clock records is measurable on this source. Its proposed
gravitational role requires the independent response of proper
spatial geometry.

There is a common spatial readout for four stations on the
80-cell source. A fair charge and launch-direction lottery defines six
capped distances. Uniform bounds on their squared-length Gram matrix
give a nondegenerate Euclidean tetrahedron on every preparation.
Either probe, using the relay and return appointments on one directed
link, predicts all six independently surveyed distances. Three held
preparations give volumes $2.752409$, $2.732611$ and $2.747317$ in
cubed native range units.

A local material measurement tests the proposed entropy mechanism on
this same source. At four exchange ticks, read the actual two load
registers on the bridge. Along an accessible change the pooled
tetrahedral geometry stays fixed, both marked forces stay fixed and
both probes register the same positive increment of bridge strain
work. At a finite step of $0.01$, the regional entropies increase by
more than $4.0153\times10^{-7}$ and $5.8625\times10^{-7}$ nats.
With a fair charge lottery and the charge tag discarded the physical
two-site entropy increases by more than $4.9389\times10^{-7}$ nats.
Its compensating area response would have to be measured on a region
whose boundary changes. The four-station area is constant on the
entire change. The corresponding sharp native optical focusing also
changes with opposite signs for the two probes in either direction.
These tests distinguish a measurable modular signal from the
additional statement that this signal gravitates with the
ordinary-matter coupling. On two source changes the Hamiltonian
bridge energy current stays exactly fixed for each probe. In the
charge-unresolved optical read, forward-minus-reverse focusing
changes by $[-104.355,-103.445]$ and $[105.145,106.457]$ per unit
change respectively. Reversing a genuinely matched null direction
cancels density and normal pressure. Interpreting these raw images
and this flux as the corresponding contractions would require
incompatible clock calibrations on the two changes.

A calibration at the stationary reference can also be tested without
choosing the common clock or the strength of gravity. Use the vacancy,
excess and fair untagged images. The last combines the arrival counts
before the optical reconstruction. In each image record the
forward-minus-reverse expansion $e$, focusing $g$ and independent
energy current $j$. A common clock and scalar area conversion
require $g+u e=k j$ on all three images. The determinant of the three
measured columns $[e,j,g]$ lies in $[0.00020771,0.00022816]$.
Consequently this instrument assignment fails even when the two
calibrations depend on the complete preparation. A gravitating collar
would have to predict a different independently calibrated geometric
response.

A further finite source test keeps the original seam memories and a
reciprocal bridge-strain register active. On a 12-port interior,
the weak-detector census and the outward and inward probe currents
can be held simultaneously fixed while the entropy of the enclosed
heads, strain and spent-mobile modes increases. Nine first-arrival
time bins recover all nine fixed-marginal head-correlation changes
and predict two independently measured energy currents. Thus the
overlap response is measurable in this setup. Identifying it with the
source of an additional gravitational field requires its magnitude
and sign to predict a separately reconstructed metric at the
ordinary-matter coupling. This finite test supplies no new galaxy
normalization. The preparation and detector records are in
\path{rate_geometry/seam_strain/weak_ruler/}.

Enrolling all four actual radar terminals allows a direct test of the
entropy assignment. With the two outer stations included, the same
equal-work preparation increases the untagged regional entropy by
$[2.05099,2.06043]\times10^{-6}$ nats at fixed radar area and volume.
Another allowed preparation fixes the whole bridge state, its two energy
currents and the probabilities of all three four-terminal occupation
sectors. The four-terminal entropy derivative exceeds
$3.2465\times10^{-7}$ nats per unit change. This variation takes place
inside sectors, so a correction depending only on their probabilities
leaves the result unchanged. Retaining the two actual head memories adds a positive conditional
entropy slope above $5.63\times10^{-6}$ nats per unit on the same
control. Even the full closed flight has a positive entropy slope
while the surveyed area remains fixed. A proper-ball entropy mechanism would need a
separately measured compensating contribution from additional degrees of
freedom or from its proper boundary. None of these finite simulated
measurements determines the gravitational coupling to galaxies.

At the deliberately prepared entropy-plus-area maximum, the signed
responses can be read separately. Running an unread sharp
left-sign measurement increases ordinary exchange energy by 0.297563
per unit probability. The full-head entropy increases by 0.409453 nats
and the matched-volume area falls by 0.0177220. Replacing the heads
by independently drawn heads with the same one-head states instead
holds ordinary energy fixed. Entropy increases by 0.231106 nats and
area falls by 0.0100028. The volume adjuster is a right-coin rotation
which preserves each head marginal and has zero entropy and energy
cost on the parked source. Both differential responses give the
separately calibrated ratio 23.10424. The interval certificate proves
existence of a realizable local maximum at the actual finite gain
and volume. At the exact maximizer it also certifies a strictly
positive entropy change and a strictly negative matched area change
for each transfer. Interpreting this extra measured area response as gravity
requires the same metric to predict independent trajectories.

A fifth independently clocked station on this preparation gives four
positive centered Gram eigenvalues. The fourth is 0.6644 of the
first. These five distances fail the flat three-dimensional
reconstruction at the measured scale. A curved three-space requires
an independent curvature and refinement test.

Correlations do produce an observable optical effect on this source.
Across a bridge with five material emitters and five receivers,
the first nonzero transmitted angular response in one direction
is proportional to the connected correlation between the two
heads. Preparing independent heads with the same marginals removes
this response. The perpendicular component is zero at this order.
At four exchange times both components give a resolved image.
Using the same emitter ensemble and exposure, read the native
energy of the receiving seam on separate copies. Calibrating image
area on this energy with ordinary matter gives held
observed-to-predicted unpairing ratios of 0.85184--0.93919 for a
vacancy and \(-0.16242\) to \(-0.14329\) for an excess. Six independent radar
stations on the same source also exclude a single flat, spherical
or hyperbolic three-space chart for their measured distances.
These restrictions concern the specified instruments.

A directional test on these same preparations resolves both the
incident energy current and the work conjugate to stretching the
contact at a fixed material frequency ratio. In the interpretation
that uses the sharp image for curvature and the contact term for local
energy and normal pressure, ordinary calibration overpredicts the held
even focusing and underpredicts the held odd focusing for both charge
probes. A common positive rescaling of the collar response leaves at
least a 14.28 percent relative discrepancy in one of the four
comparisons. Varying only the receiving phases after the same flight
also changes the raw inferred focusing. These experiments identify a
finite correlation-dependent response and constrain its conversion
into a physical source term. They do not determine a galaxy's
gravitating mass. The assumptions, signed intervals and clock tests
are given in the spacetime paper~\cite{ophEinstein}.

A separate protected source permits a test over an entire family of
radii. Two written words have fixed marginals and a variable
correlation \(z\); their joint admission probability is
\(q_B=\Theta(\ell^4)\) in the routed clock radius. The clock area is
independent of \(z\). With reference correlation \(r\) the entropy
slope is \(q_B k(r)\), where \(k(0)=0\) and \(-k'(r)\) is bounded
above and below by strictly positive constants from the measured-word
law. The unique entropy maximum occurs for independent words. Any
sequence of references making the stationarity error \(o(\ell^4)\)
also makes the normalized linear collar signal vanish. Refining this
particular ruler therefore cannot retain the proposed leading source
and restore balance. A macroscopic gravitational interpretation needs
an independent geometric response to an extended matter preparation.

There is an independent scalar thermodynamic interpretation.
Allow cyclic unitary work extraction on many independent copies of
the parked source. At fixed entropy its asymptotic minimum energy
per copy is that of the corresponding Gibbs state, with
\(dE_{\min}=dS/\beta\) and \(\beta=1.87753\) in inverse exchange
units at this preparation. Twelve-copy energy sorting transfers
98.64\% of the ordinary calibration to the pairing area derivative.
The limiting relation is exact under the stated controls. The
operative energy is a work-extraction quantity, with an external
work store. Its identification with a conserved local stress and
with lensing on proper scales requires independent regional
measurements. The finite mechanism contains a visible propagation
effect of information missing from either head's individual
records. It supplies no observational exclusion of a separate
dark particle population.

The reciprocal-strain pump also permits a test of spatial averaging.
Choose uniformly distributed observer positions along the interior
hinges of its five-station radar star. More precisely, enroll a fixed
proper volume in thin tubes about the middle thirds of those hinges.
Each observer adjusts their ball radius to fix its proper volume. The
mean area deficit then scales exactly as the fourth power of the
volume-equivalent radius, as a smooth curvature contribution would.
After calibration on the ordinary rail, the measured response to
independent re-pairing exceeds the prediction from twelve-port regional
entropy by a factor greater than 59. Along a second preparation curve
the averaged area and enrolled number are exactly fixed while the
regional entropy increases. This identifies which source assignment
fails on the finite pump. Giving the collar charge a gravitational
interpretation requires a common proper-ball stress and geometric
response at the ordinary coupling. These data and the sampling law
are in \path{rate_geometry/seam_strain/weak_ruler/center_average/}
in the matched Einstein package. Two native lotteries also agree on
energy and number separately in each of four graph-distance shells, yet
have different first variations of the individually matched mean area.
A change of radial weights on these hardware measurements therefore
cannot restore the ordinary calibration.

There is an all-resolution collar measurement on a midpoint family
extending the finite Interval Echo boards.
Two distinct uniform words enter a shrinking clocked station annulus
together with probability asymptotic to $(729/256)\ell^4$ on the
declared midpoint family. Their addressed regional entropy has slope
$0.0094118671\,\ell^4+o(\ell^4)$ nats at the reversed-tag reference.
Independent tags on the original faces reduce that coefficient by a
factor of five. Neither variation changes the protected station-clock
ranges, even though the local word records resolve the signed change.
This supplies a collar contribution at the required order and rules
out its coupling to area inferred solely from these routes. A geometry
that responds to the term on proper balls remains necessary for a physical
halo source. See \path{collar_scaling/station_clock/} in the Einstein
station experiment.

Choosing the observer uniformly across the screen preserves a positive
entropy response of $0.0135530886$ nats per fourth power of the
clock-annulus radius. Each observer records the same routes under
the change in tags. In a separate preparation, change only the
correlation between two nonadjacent original face sectors. Comparing
the order of their measured seam energies detects the induced modular
variation. On a whole corner ball it contributes $0.00133857665$ nats
per fourth power of radius, while every sum of bounded-strength word
operators of sublinear range in station hops has zero leading response.
A physical halo interpretation would have to identify the effective
stress that carries this measured correlation and the independent
geometric response to it.

\subsection{Which stored records produce an attractive recoil?}

On an 80-cell material source let $z=-0.01437037$ be the measured
formed-minus-independent difference in the probability of a complete
return. A mark starts at one of eight central guide addresses and moves
until it drains. If it first reaches address $x$, admit the formed-source
comparison there. A miss gives a fair comparison word. The mean of the
resulting sign is $b_x=z h_x$, where $h_x$ is the first-passage
probability. Erasing this word relative to its fixed formed reference
has modular score $b_x\operatorname{atanh} b_x$.

Two independent such words can occupy the two load ports of one
native seam. A shared fair flip leaves each individual load fair.
With matching orientations the unlike-load probability is
$(1-b_x^2)/2$ before erasure and $1/2$ afterwards. Each unlike pair
has equal weights on energies 8 and 9. The complete diagonal pair state
commutes with the native exchange at every hold time. The energy
increment is therefore $17b_x^2/4$. Reversing the second physical port
gives its negative. The two orientations have the same input modular
score. Parking the two words on isolated filled memory heads gives zero
energy difference. In a conservative transfer on four native ports,
the reader's gain of $0.00087766$ at unit admission is the supply
seam's loss.

The independently calibrated energy reader releases pulses with
probability equal to mean energy divided by 512. The stationary guide
current obeys vertex balance. For any centered cube with $2k$ sites per
side, cancellation of the internal currents gives the mean inward
material recoil on its $24k^2$ boundary edges,
\[
 \bar d_{\rm in}(k)=\frac{8}{5}\frac{17}{2048}
    \frac{\sum_{x\,\mathrm{inside}}(zh_x)^2}{24 k^2}.
\]
Here $8/5$ comes from the ordinary material flight. A rational
calculation checks all 1,000 addresses of the smallest guide chart.
At one of its outer-directed faces the two material probes give signed
endpoint shifts of $-0.02365$ and $-0.02383$ per expected excess pulse.
The forecast is $-0.02375$ with a simultaneous Monte Carlo half-width
of $0.00107$ at 99\%. For $k=4,8,16$ on the largest chart,
$k\bar d_{\rm in}$ is respectively $1.504,1.504,1.392$ in units of
$10^{-7}$. It falls to $0.742$ at $k=64$ near the drain. This measures
a persistent interaction energy and a finite-time displacement on a
product-address instrument. Identifying it with astronomical mass
requires the source dynamics to populate similarly oriented physical
contacts, account for their energy supply and give their abundance and
length scale in ordinary spatial units.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source}{Protocol, exact guide calculation and material terminal records}.}


The two original head records also permit a direct test. After four
time units of free evolution on the 80-cell board, let $X,Y$ be the
two sharp native coin bits. Their positive covariance means the two
records agree more often than independent records with the same
single-head distributions. Integer evolution with a bounded unitary
Taylor remainder encloses $\operatorname{Cov}(X,Y)$ in
$[0.013449439,0.013449478]$ for a vacancy and
$[0.017401348,0.017401443]$ for a load excess. In the same physical
encoding, independent delivery with probabilities $h_L,h_R$
therefore gives an erasure energy
$\Delta E=17h_Lh_R\operatorname{Cov}(X,Y)$. More generally, let
$a_0,a_1$ be the response probabilities of the same local binary
reader on the two sharp input values. Independent copies multiply the
energy change by $(a_1-a_0)^2$, strictly positive for every sensitive
matched reader, including a common reversal. Full sharp admission gives
$+0.228641$ and $+0.295824$ respectively. Both fixed-reference
modular variations are positive. Both material probes move inward for
each original source. There are no duplicated return comparisons in
this measurement. The spatial prediction uses the two admission
routes and the ordinary calibrated receiver. The positive excess is
relative to the physically retained pair. If ordinary background
contacts have independent loads at the same marginals, full
erasure returns to their energy and the retained correlations lower
it. The source dynamics must therefore select the physical background
as well as the contact population and the energy supply.

\subsection{Can ordinary matter calibrate the modular charge?}

Consider the two exchange eigenstates of a binary contact with one
load. The symmetric state has energy 8 and the antisymmetric state has
energy 9. A mixture of equal empty and doubly occupied states has
energy zero. All three preparations give the same individual load
distributions. Compare the symmetric state with a mixture containing
$8/9$ antisymmetric preparations and $1/9$ balanced zero-energy
preparations. Both have mean energy 8. The independently calibrated
energy reader fires on $1/64$ of fresh preparations in either bank and
both subsequent material flights therefore have the same law.

For a sharp reference with unlike probability $w$ and a common fair
flip, the parity states have identical weight $w/2$. The masked
reference logarithm gives a modular difference of
$\log[(1-w)/w]/9$. This equals 0.23548 nats for the original vacancy
and 0.22746 nats for the original excess. Calibration on the ordinary
zero-to-eight transition would attribute an energy difference of
$8/9$ to these equal-energy banks. Thus a single scalar coupling to
this particular record logarithm fails an ordinary-matter control.

An independently defined microcanonical reference resolves the two
exchange parities. Give $M$ isolated native contacts total energy
$2M$ and total load $M$, and count all admitted eigenwords uniformly.
Let $\beta_8$ be the modular difference per energy unit from zero to
eight and $\beta_1$ that from eight to nine. For either original sharp
decorrelation the fractional error in the energy inferred using
$\beta_8$ equals $(\beta_1/\beta_8-1)/17$. The local log is singular
for $M=16,32,64$. The error is $-10.90\%$ at $M=128$ and
$+0.004449\%$ at $M=4096$. In the latter bank the two independently
calibrated modular predictions are 0.228651 and 0.295837 native
energy units, compared with 0.228641 and 0.295824. Both are
measurements along the same mismatch direction. The equal-energy
control retains a difference of $-0.00009298$ nats. The construction
supplies the equilibrium distribution. Its microscopic preparation
matters: conservation of additive energy, load and contact count
allows independent populations to change only in multiples of
18 symmetric states exchanged for 16 antisymmetric, one empty and
one double state.

A finite preparation makes the reference choice explicit. Draw
uniformly from the four native energy words. Use identically
prepared pilots to read the ordinary mismatch and odd exchange
components. For each active energy unit a three-bit shutter rejects
the untouched target with probability $1/8$. There are eight mismatch
units and one odd unit. Survival on level $e$ is $(7/8)^e$, so the
entire four-state reference has
$-\log\rho=(\log Z)I+\log(8/7)H$. Its calibrated modular variation
equals the native energy variation for every contact state, and the
equal-energy control gives exactly zero. Of 2,097,152 simulated
preparations 1,385,685 survive. Conditioning independent survivors
on total load $M$ and energy $2M$ gives the microcanonical reference
above, with selection probability $6.67\times10^{-6}$ at $M=4096$.
The shutters implement the ordinary energy weighting. An explanation
of cosmic gravity must identify an analogous physical reference
from the dynamics of astronomical regions.

The sign also depends on which physical records the source populates.
At exposure four, keeping the original minimum-neighbour arm rule
gives 144 positive and 96 negative directed placements on 80 cells,
and 656 positive and 304 negative placements on 320 cells for each
mobile charge. Independently preparing both local heads in the same
sharp state gives a positive population mean across the tested
20-, 80- and 320-cell ensembles. Individual placements can
have the opposite sign. An astronomical population law must supply
the preparation and its distribution.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station/mobile_collar_source/placement}{Native placement censuses, sign law and preparation controls}.}

On a closed native cell the two sharp records can be placed one hop
from the anchor but two hops from each other. No initial edge then
distinguishes them from independent records with the same marginals.
Subsequent free exchange redistributes energy. The exact long-time
mean enclosed energies per unit of covariance, for aperture radii
zero through three native hops, are $-1.02605,0,+1.02605,0$.
The exterior compensates the entire intermediate excess. Two cells
joined by their native seam also conserve the complete energy. Their
radius-two response averaged through time 65,536 is negative
while its numerical infinite-time projection is positive. Graph hops
and evolution times here are native units.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/closed_region}{Energy and modular pilots, exact finite projection and bath multiplicities}.}

\paragraph{Energy on the original written contact.}
A blank seam receives two sharp ternary loads and writes a memory while
redistributing those loads. Compare its sharp output with an ensemble
in which two independently prepared contacts exchange their memories.
Each contact keeps its full two-load distribution, its memory distribution
and its total load. On the native 99-state Hamiltonian at stiffness
$\kappa=8$, the mean energy increment is $17/8$ for odd input load
and zero for even input load. On an occupied--empty input the sharp
write gives energies $0,8,9$ with probabilities $(1/2,1/4,1/4)$.
Exchanging memories gives $(1/4,3/8,3/8)$. The swap supplies $17/4$
units to the two contacts together. Reversing it recovers that energy.
At fixed shutter-prepared reference the modular increment equals
$\log(8/7)$ times the ordinary energy increment on the full contact.
The positive sign follows from the native write and the specified physical
loss of pairing. Infrequent observation alone leaves the energy unchanged.

With the exchanged and retained preparations each freely evolving on the
same closed 80-owner board, the double commutator gives the exact initial
second derivative $-85/32$ for their excess energy on the two endpoints,
assigning half of each incident interaction to each endpoint. The adjacent
shell gains the same curvature. Total excess is $17/8$ at every time.
At time 64 the excess within two native hops is 2.1027. In a two-owner
assembly its infinite-time mean is 2.0804 within two hops. A second
experiment physically routes the records and converts their energy into
pulses at the rate calibrated with ordinary material. Both finite probes
give inward signed flight responses, $-0.02299$ and $-0.02329$ per
expected excess pulse. The forecast is $-0.02354$ and the simultaneous
99\% sampling half-width is 0.00192. This flight uses specified guides
and receivers. In the closed two-owner assembly, place an extra unit
load at the most distant port of either owner. Subtract the no-extra-load
occupation in each preparation and take the erased-minus-paired change
in its first distance moment from the written contact. The initial term
is $-1445t^6/2304$, compared with $-85t^6/576$ for an ordinary source
on a blank contact. Both initially draw the additional response inward.
At the registered time
four the response shifts \emph{outward} by 0.06910 hops on both sides.
An ordinary source on a blank contact shifts the corresponding response
inward by 0.15906 hops. The written response is inward at times one,
sixteen and sixty-four. This observable includes scattering between
indistinguishable loads and fails the proposed common inward-sign test.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary}{Native contact, closed propagation, finite energy budget and guided receiver records}.}

\paragraph{Can one layer supply the measured mass?}
On a federation with $N$ occupied binary owners and initially blank
records, write each inter-owner contact once. If $b$ edges cross from
an occupied to an empty owner, loss of pairing raises the total energy
by $17b/8$. All other local contributions agree between the prepared
ensembles. The entire ordinary input costs $72N+17b/2$, including the
degree contribution. Since $b\leq 3N$, the maximum ratio is
$17/260=0.06538$. A compact triangular patch of side $L$ has $N=L^2$
and $b=3L$. Other shapes of equal mass can have different boundaries.
Repeatedly restoring the sharp bulk profile also consumes material.
For a 32-edge boundary a reservoir supplies 16 inside units and takes
16 outside units per cycle on average in a finite repair experiment.

Identify the initial ordinary energy with the allowed baryonic mass
and apply the same mass conversion to the excess. Even allowing twice
each catalogue bin's upper baryonic mass, the four lensing stacks at
291.07 kpc require an excess of 5.41 to 8.06 times that allowance
under the velocity-error choices described below. They require at
least 83 to 124 maximal layer equivalents at perfect delivery. This
counts energy present simultaneously. A count of historical writes
does not measure it. Under the additional identification with the
homogeneous cold component of the Planck base-$\Lambda$CDM fit, the
ratio $0.11933/0.02242$ requires at least 82 such equivalents.

A common threshold on a resolved ordinary surface density predicts
a source proportional to its integrated boundary variation. In a
ten-galaxy Leroy--SPARC comparison, five galaxies fixed the overall
gain and five tested it. The median absolute speed residual on those
five was $35.0$ km/s. NGC~3521 and NGC~3198 were selected by their
baryonic profiles before using their speeds in this comparison.
With independent stellar distances, the radial source predicts a ratio
5.75 for their outer excess squared speeds; the measured ratio is 1.29.
Independent two-dimensional stellar, atomic and molecular maps give
4.09. Recomputing the ordinary disk force from those same maps gives
a measured excess ratio of 1.54. Scaling the atomic maps to catalogue
fluxes changes the two values to 3.57 and 1.60. Counting only the two
gas phases gives 1.56 on the original maps. Fixing a gas boundary gain
on NGC~3198 and applying it to the radial profiles of the other eight
galaxies gives a median absolute speed error of 34.9 km/s and an RMS
error of 68.7 km/s. With two-dimensional gas maps this transfer gives
18.6 and 53.1 km/s respectively, including a 99.0 km/s underprediction
for NGC~2841 and a 78.6 km/s overprediction for NGC~5055.
An astronomical source requires independently measured
record formation, survival and coupling across environments.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/boundary_source}{Galaxy enrollment, held speeds, direct distances and gas maps};
\href{https://doi.org/10.1088/0004-6256/136/6/2782}{Leroy et al.};
\href{https://doi.org/10.3847/0004-6256/152/6/157}{Lelli et al.};
\href{https://doi.org/10.3847/2041-8213/ad54b0}{Mistele et al.};
\href{https://doi.org/10.1051/0004-6361/201833910}{Planck Collaboration VI, Table 2}.}

The temperature spectrum tests the numerical size of this energy at
recombination. Give the maximal single layer the standard gravitational
transfer of an adiabatic, pressureless cold component in a flat cosmology.
Hold the Planck control's baryon density, tilt, initial amplitude,
neutrinos, optical depth and Hubble constant fixed. In CAMB the resulting
cold density $\omega_c=(17/260)\,0.02237$ puts matter--radiation equality
at redshift 569, compared with 3403 in the standard control. The ratio
of the third to second acoustic crest is 0.535. The observed Planck
temperature bins give 0.980, as does the control. Even matching the
first crest in both scale and amplitude leaves $947\,\mu{\rm K}^2$
at the third measured crest, compared with $2522\pm21\,\mu{\rm K}^2$.
These bin-centre comparisons specify the cold-fluid transfer; a different
metric perturbation law needs its own predicted spectrum.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/cold_dilution/native_boundary}{Transfer settings, complete bin predictions and rerun};
\href{https://irsa.ipac.caltech.edu/data/Planck/release_3/ancillary-data/cosmoparams/COM_PowerSpect_CMB-TT-binned_R3.01.txt}{Planck 2018 measured temperature spectrum}.}

\centering

The dashed curve assigns the maximum single-layer energy to cold matter.

\subsection{Equal regional charge and a material response of either sign}

On the same 24-port board, prepare one sharp native write and alter its
joint records in two ways. Exchange memories between independent copies,
or use a return-to-blank measurement and phase feedback. Both transfers
cost $17/8$ and preserve the ordinary load and memory marginals. Choose
the one-load equilibrium reference of the full interacting board at
$\beta=\log(8/7)$ and trace it to the two gateway sites. Both treatments
increase $\langle-\log\rho_R\rangle$ by $0.283224$ nats. In fact their
entire modular distributions agree on each mirror-symmetric ball,
at every positive temperature. Folding the two identical owners gives
four orthogonal channels distinguished by memory energy and membership in
the native write image. Each source occupies the gateway with the same
four probabilities $(1,3,1,3)/8$. Tracing a symmetric exterior preserves
the channels. An asymmetric seven-site cut resolves the two distributions.

Relative to the original write, at time four a freely launched material
probe shifts $+0.069105$ graph hops outward for memory exchange and
$-0.134267$ inward for feedback. A local pair of measurements predicts
both results. Let $u$ be the probability that the memory coin and outgoing
load agree with a native branch, and $v$ the probability of a return to
blank under a second echo. Conservation of left-owner load minus memory
coin and mirror exchange imply that the mirror-averaged radial read on
this four-dimensional source space is $a(t)+b(t)u+c(t)v$. Three physical
standards determine the coefficients. On the symmetric cut every spectral
probability is affine in $v$. Its calibrated modular mean can therefore
replace the return-to-blank read if $u$ is measured independently.
At fixed $v=1/4$ and fixed ordinary and memory marginals the allowed
time-four shifts span $-0.134267$ to $+0.170790$ hops. Integer time
evolution with a bounded remainder certifies opposite signs for the two
realized treatments. Four additional complex sources
satisfy the prediction in independent simulated port counts. A neutral
Ramsey clock installed near and far has an exactly source-independent gap.
It supplies no redshift for this material response.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/regional_reference}{Regional operators, measured source frequencies and calibrated material flights};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/matched_probe}{Matched preparations and native timing controls}.}

\subsection{Persistent stock, lensing pressure and acoustic polarization}

On 320 owners a single mobile load can write a path of 64 simultaneous
records if the apparatus supplies $531.25$ energy units. Once service
stops, its path retains at least 478 units above any one-load blank
control for all subsequent free times. This is a physical storage
mechanism with measured commissioning work. Its abundance and location
are set by the preparation. For a single service on an initially blank
federation the gain has an independent upper bound. Let $W$ be the native
write, $B$ the incident energy, and $G$ the energy observable on its
memory. The written generator is $G-WBW^\dagger$, with $GW=WB$.
Preserving the memory can therefore add at most the incident energy,
even on arbitrarily coherent preparations. On the external gateways of
the native federation, the internal and ordinary port energies tighten
the limit to $3/7$ of the board's ordinary input. Every ternary cell word
enters that bound. Fresh work in successive services can increase the stock.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock}{Persistent trail and work ledger};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/population}{Coherent and heterogeneous energy bounds}.}

Could relativistic pressure give this small stock a large lensing signal?
In spherical static Einstein gravity, use $G=c=1$, $m'=4\pi r^2\rho$,
lapse $e^\Phi$ and weak-field optical pseudo-mass
$L=(m+r^2\Phi')/2$. Integrating stress conservation,
$(r^2p_r)'=2rp_t-r^2(\rho+p_r)\Phi'$, bounds the positive
$2\pi r^3p_r(r)$ by $(1+\epsilon)[m_\infty-m(r)]$ if the outer surface
traction vanishes, $|p_r|,|p_t|\le\rho$ and $|s\Phi'(s)|\le\epsilon$
outside $r$. Thus $L\le(1+\epsilon)m_\infty/(1-2m/r)$, allowing
tangential tension. At 291.07 kpc in the four isolated-galaxy lens
stacks, setting both weak-field corrections to 0.001, doubling each
baryonic ceiling and shifting the measured accelerations down gives
minimum additional-to-baryon mass ratios of 6.34, 6.10, 8.04 and 5.40.
This uses total available mass-energy, including the exterior that
supports the pressure. External confinement contributes a separate
boundary term.%
\footnote{\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/astro-ph/0512213}{Faber and Visser, optical mass};
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09685}{Mistele et al., galaxy lensing};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/compensation/pressure_budget}{Observed inputs, two error allocations and boundary controls}.}

For the early Universe grant the entire $3/7$ stock a standard
pressureless adiabatic transfer and identify its incident energy with
the baryons. Fitting amplitude, angular dilation and tilt on 155 Planck
temperature bands yields a covariance-weighted conditional polarization
residual of 17,641 on 310 held TE and EE bands. Even a cold-to-baryon
ratio of one gives 11,410. The standard ratio of 5.36 gives 293. As a
separate check, Planck's published base-$\Lambda$CDM chains give 95\%
intervals of $4.99$--$5.48$ for TE plus low-multipole polarization and
$4.07$--$5.74$ for EE plus low-multipole polarization. These comparisons
quantify the shortfall of the tested energy supply under the specified
transfer. A cosmological prediction from OPH requires the formation
history, perturbations and geometric coupling of the same sources.%
\footnote{\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1907.12875}{Planck spectra and likelihoods};
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.06209}{Planck cosmological parameters};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/cold_acoustic_polarization}{Bandpowers, covariance, posterior samples and predictions}.}

\paragraph{Transferring three powered preparations.}
For the three actual freely retained trails (14, 32 and 64 records),
the measured whole-board excesses divided by the initial ordinary
energy are $425/202$, $1037/202$ and $2125/202$. Applying the same
standard cold-fluid transfer to all three gives held polarization scores
of 4612, 307 and 4272. The middle preparation supplies 259.25 units of
work on an ordinary input of 50.5. Its ratio of 5.134 falls inside
both central 68\% Planck polarization intervals. In the EE band window
at multipoles 270--330 it gives $7.90\,\mu{\rm K}^2$ against
$7.70\pm0.13$ measured. The standard control gives 7.74 and a total
held score of 293. The relative scores vary with baryon density.
This is a retrospective comparison on three supplied
sizes. The apparatus sets the path length and provides its energy.

If the same ratio of 5.134 is imposed on every galaxy, the maximum
weak-lensing equivalent speed at 291.07 kpc in the third mass bin is
151.2 km/s even with the doubled baryon allowance. The favorably
shifted observed value is 183.6 km/s. The middle trail falls below all
four such bounds, and below three when the four-bin simultaneous error
allocation is used. The longest trail can supply all four shifted
masses but misses the polarization spectrum. Predicting the amount
and distribution of surviving records, including a possible difference
between galactic and cosmic ratios, requires a formation mechanism.

\subsection{Does observing fewer records determine the extra pull?}

A controlled native preparation varies the joint records without changing
the amount of information lost to sparse observation. Begin with a
written source that returns to a blank seam under a second echo in one
quarter of trials. In the complementary branch route the ordinary
endpoint loads along a 17-edge bypass of the parent federation, apply a
partial native exchange through angle $\theta$, and undo the routing.
Each borrowed rail is restored. Release the same two-owner assembly and
retain all branches. The entropy, the separate memory and ordinary
distributions, and the symmetric regional modular distribution are
independent of $\theta$. The shared native branch has weight $1/4$.
The other branch continuously rotates between two states in the same
degenerate sector of the regional reference.

For a channel $\Phi$ that erases a memory reading with probability $p$,
the lost distinguishability is
$D(\rho\Vert\sigma)-D(\Phi\rho\Vert\Phi\sigma)$. At half erasure
it is 0.828866 nats with the missing flag retained and 1.255430 nats
when the missing memory is replaced by the reference marginal without a
flag. Each value is the same at both ends of the preparation range.
In fact the complete loss curves agree for every $p$. The source
rotation preserves the joint reference, its separate marginals, and
the common replacement state. Unitary covariance therefore preserves
the input and output relative entropies separately. The connected
linear modular expectation also has the same positive increment of
0.283224 nats throughout. The two quantities measure different aspects
of recovery.

On this family the actual time-four material response ranges from
$-0.134267$ to $+0.170790$ graph hops relative to the same sharp
baseline (Fig.~\ref{fig:loss-and-flight}). Integer bounds on the
propagators certify both signs. Two native source-sensitive clocks give
opposite near--far timing shifts at the second event at the two endpoints.
On the clock-equipped board an equal-energy ordinary standard delays
the first near--far tick by 0.01465 time units, whereas physical memory
exchange advances it by 0.03956. A scalar determined solely by the tested
charge or loss cannot identify a common attractive response of these
apparatuses. A tensor source, a different physical reference or a
macroscopic geometric observable would require its own transfer test.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/regional_reference/recovery}{Native routing, symmetry proof, finite counts and certified signs};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/matched_probe/signal_time}{Ordinary-matter timing calibration and independent clocks}.}

\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\IfFileExists{model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/regional_reference/recovery/recovery_flight.pdf}
 {\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/regional_reference/recovery/recovery_flight.pdf}}
 {\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{../model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/regional_reference/recovery/recovery_flight.pdf}}
