The points on the right are native model evaluations and the line is
the finite operator prediction.}
\label{fig:loss-and-flight}
\end{figure}

A measurable second quantity resolves this ambiguity on the routed family.
With the probability $u$ of native coin--load coincidence read on independent
copies, $2u-1$ times the response to ordinary orbital dephasing plus
$2(1-u)$ times the response to ordinary endpoint randomization predicts
both clock waveforms and the radial change. On two further angles the
predicted time-four radii from the sampled standards and source counts
were 2.2911 and 2.4432 hops; separate port reads gave 2.2914 and 2.4449.
Agreement follows from an identity of density differences on the conserved
sectors. The instruments retain different response functions.

Which source should count as extra ordinary matter to these observers?
The reference deposits a countable mobile unit at one of the two
source ports on $2/33$ of the eligible launches. Its calculated
full-unit energy cost on the instrumented board is $561/16$ in native
units. Independent interferometry on the same contact map with blank
memories measures its dispersion, $\Delta E=A\lambda$, where $\lambda$
is a spatial Laplacian eigenvalue. The clock and fringes give
$A=0.50002$ and a kinetic mass $1/(2A)$ between 0.988 and 1.013
in graph and clock units. This identifies a mobile excitation on a
specified background. A rest-energy assignment also needs a physical
energy reference. In a driven guide, halving the contrast leaves all
within-guide phase and position records fixed while halving one
branch's rate against a parked load. In a separate spherical source
with prescribed material coupling, three exterior clocks recover the
active mass parameter from pulse and echo intervals even when the
reader has no input radius labels. Their comparison with a free
release distinguishes a common gravitational source from an
instrument-specific coupling.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/record_masses}{Referenced mass, exterior clock inverse and free-release observations}.}
With the clock contacts installed on the mobile-unit board, eleven of
twelve held fringe frequencies miss the blank prediction. The first
return fraction is 0.83213 against a forecast of 0.99912. At each of
the six appointments the two fringe components exclude lossless
propagation by a single relative phase. The excitation's baryonic
identity and gravitational coupling are undetermined.

On a common mixture of paired and empty sources the two clock signs
and all occupied ports can be read together. The reader receives
the complete terminal records with the shutter exposure concealed.
Choose a further source for which a separately read memory coin
agrees with the occupied source endpoint on $68.00\%$ of preparations.
Both mirror launches then show an inward mean displacement, on the
bare board and on the clock-equipped board. The four changes range
from $-0.0041$ to $-0.0075$ hops and their simultaneous simulated
count intervals exclude zero. Yet a combination of clock disagreement
and one radial reading is positive for the reference load and negative
for every member of this routed family. The finite affine law transfers
the sign from its two endpoints. No positive multiple of this load's
measured response can explain all of these records, even when its
strength depends arbitrarily on the source coincidence. An effective
gravitational interpretation at larger scales requires a common
description of the macroscopic observers' own range and clock records.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/regional_reference/recovery}{Joint records, native kinetic calibration and held inward test}.}

There is a direct way to measure the proposed source without resolving
the pull of a microscopic mass. On the 80-cell equilibrium, two
observers each choose one of three local Pauli axes and record a sign.
They compare the sign pairs and their parity flag on copies of the
reference and of two prepared sources. Each individual head has the same
marginal on both sources. Denote the reference by $X$, its conditionally
independent product by $P$, and the endpoint difference by $D$.
The coincidence counts determine $q=\operatorname{Tr}D(\log P-\log X)$,
including the uncertainty in the reference. At $2^{38}$ simulated
trials per setting they give $0.00022951$ nats, with simultaneous
99\% interval $[0.00014802,0.00031100]$ rounded outward. The independent
product-reference control includes zero. This measures an increment
of correlation at fixed individual readouts and parked energy.

One finite consequence of that measurement can be proved on the same
preparation. The fixed marginals give $\operatorname{Tr}D\log P=0$,
so the entropy differential $dS_X(D)$ is exactly $q$. The ordinary
calibration and the stationarity bound give
$|q+\eta\,dA(D)|<1.26\times10^{-9}$ nats for the four-station
radar survey, including its residual volume change. Hence the measured
source implies a negative area derivative at the same confidence.
Independently enclosing the six reconstructed lengths gives an actual
endpoint area decrease of $9.93158\times10^{-6}$ squared range units,
to the precision shown. Both endpoint tetrahedra admit flat spatial
realizations. Adding two stations supplies an independent check on the
same source using 30 directed round trips. Seven of the fifteen
four-station areas decrease, five increase and three changes are
unresolved at simultaneous 99\% counting coverage. Even allowing
separate area and volume coefficients calibrated on ordinary matter
and a head rotation, ten of the fifteen surveys exclude a balance with
the same measured head entropy. A spatially distributed source needs a
regional prescription for which records contribute. The sign of the
pull inferred from a freely released
tracer therefore requires a further implication from the source to
the clock and trajectory records of the effective spacetime.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/openwave/interval_echo/row_19/selected_collar}{Coincidence counts, ordinary-calibrated survey and finite bounds}.}

The two clocks also test whether moving the comparison load can explain
the records. On each preparation allow a mobile unit with an independent
fair memory coin to start at any of the 24 ports on the empty half of
the common baseline. At supplied exposure twelve, with no launched tracer,
let $S=[8357\,\Delta P(++)-91643\,\Delta P(-+)]/100000$, with the near
clock sign first. An integer enclosure of the finite propagators gives
$S\ge0.0245310$ on both routed endpoints and
$S\le-2.4879\times10^{-6}$ on every port loading. Linearity in the routing
weight extends the first bound to the whole family. Every nonnegative
sum of the loading responses has the opposite sign or vanishes. Thus
even an arbitrary static incoherent spatial profile and free linear
strength fail this clock comparison. The score was selected on computed
probabilities. Its weakest loading sign is resolved by integer propagation.
Observers who retain only macroscopic averages can lose this distinction.

\subsection{From stored work to a spatial and cosmological source}

On a separate evacuated 32-write trail, two simulated readers calibrate
the regional modular charge from spectral frequencies. A folded route
places every write inside both 12 and 24 hops, giving the same positive
charge, about $36.3$ nats, at both apertures. The flat rest-ball kernel
$w_R(r)=\max(0,(R^2-r^2)/(2R))$ satisfies $w_{2R}(r)\geq2w_R(r)$.
The same inequality holds for sums weighted by any nonnegative shell
stress. Consequently a positive multiple of the raw enclosed reading
cannot supply the entire weighted stress integral at both radii.
An assignment of the local shell increments as mass density has a
different observable consequence, namely the enclosed orbital mass
as a function of radius.

On a paid trail, compare each written bond with a blank bond at the
same ordinary reduced state. For any subset containing $j$ of the
original heads the excess is $8.5j-D_j$, with $0\le D_j\le16.25$
for all later free times. Each deficit is positive and their sum is
fixed by the conserved full energy and the bounded one-load blank
Hamiltonian $48I+L/2$ for the graph Laplacian $L$. An outward shortest
path puts one record in each successive radial shell. Other routes of
the same length place their records at different enclosed radii.

Transferring the terminal load into a separate equal-energy detector
costs another 12.75 units. On the evacuated board the Hamiltonian is a
sum of independent memory operators. Its actual regional canonical
logarithm gives exactly $8.5\beta$ additional nats per enclosed head,
at every time. At 75\% retention a missing-data flag gives 0.8513 nats
per head; encoding a missing read as a blank gives 1.5224, above the
full-record 1.1350. Physical sign measurements can also change a head's
history without changing its 8.5 units of energy. The equilibrium
memory--ordinary connected logarithm vanishes throughout this vacuum
sector. No disjoint load-free receiver acquires a signal while the
stock is held. After an ordinary launch 35 edges from a 64-record path,
the time-four change of any ordinary outcome probability is below
$9.28\times10^{-24}$. This finite propagation bound uses the actual
blank generator and permits arbitrarily long source preparation age.

A simulated observer can also establish a local attraction by reading
which contacts were written. Let $m_x$ count the marked contacts at port
$x$, let $B=\{x:m_x>0\}$, and let $L_U$ be the positive Laplacian on the
unmarked contacts. An inserted mobile double load ($q=2$) has generator
$6\kappa q^2 I+L_U/2-\kappa q^2\operatorname{diag}(m)$ relative to the
empty board's evolving memories. On a written contact the native write
image at total load two has material word $(1,1)$, orthogonal to either
location of the double load. In the equal real wave on $N$ ports the
mean hop distance to $B$ therefore has initial acceleration
\[
 -\frac{\kappa q^2}{N}\sum_{\substack{i\in B,\ j\notin B\\i\sim j}}m_i .
\]
It is strictly inward whenever the source has a boundary and $\kappa>0$.
The outward and folded 960-port preparations give $-7.9333$ and $-4.9000$
hops per squared native time. Independent position counts resolve both
inward changes. For a separately localized insertion, conserved energy
below the free band places at least 96.8\% probability on the written
support at all times at the stated 99\% counting coverage.

The two stored magnitudes give about 36.3 and 138.9 nats on the outward
trail with identical double-load motion. A unit probe on the larger
magnitude has the same free dispersion and one quarter of the initial
acceleration. On a 240-port board a mobile ordinary unit and a written
seam increase the double-probe count on the source. The changes one
shell farther out have opposite signs at the same appointment. Preparing
both sources together gives exactly the ordinary-only initial shell
accelerations. Their measured changes fail addition in 18 shell and
time bins. These experiments identify attraction to the local incidence
of written contacts. Deriving universal free fall from a regional
source requires a different implication.

An ordinary unit can carry a response beyond the written contact itself.
On a 240-port board put one such unit in equilibrium at
$\beta=\log(8/7)$. Writing a single magnitude-two contact raises its
probability on the two endpoints from 0.794\% to 2.415\%.
Let $p_x$ be its measured position probability and $s_x$ the sum of
$p$ on the live neighbors of $x$. Insert the double load in the equal
real wave on the other $N-1$ ports, coherently conditional on the ordinary
address. Outside the written support the initial probability current
into $u$ from its neighbor $v$ obeys
\[
 (N-1)\dot J_{v\to u}=4\kappa\big[(s_u+p_u)-(s_v+p_v)\big]
              -\frac{d}{dt}\operatorname{Im}\rho_{uv}.
\]
The final derivative is a two-port interference reading on the ordinary
unit before insertion. At equilibrium it vanishes. The exchange and
contrast terms of the native generator give this relation without an
added force law. Source readings on separate copies consequently
predict an increase of $0.02301$ to $0.02307$ per squared native time
in the acceleration of the probability enclosed by the first exterior
cut. Independent position counts after release resolve the inward change.
At the second cut the source readings predict an outward change.
Giving the same ordinary position distribution a common preparation
phase changes the first acceleration by a resolved amount accounted
for by the interference term.

The corresponding insertion clock has a separately testable relation to
ordinary matter. Conditional on an empty insertion port, a written
contact and an adjacent ordinary unit each lower its initial frequency
by $4\kappa+1/2$. The clock slows at the first exterior station. A scalar
potential inferred from the clocks and calibrated on ordinary release
underestimates the first exterior probability acceleration after a sharp
address read by $0.0001019$ to $0.0001101$ per squared native time. Both
intervals use simultaneous 99\% counting bounds. The finite prediction
concerns the specified release in an equilibrated medium. A universal
metric also has to determine the response of other probes and lensing.

In a driven version, collect the ordinary unit seven surveyed hops from
the same written seam and return it to one endpoint. At fixed injected
current the change in its grounded spatial profile has equal and
opposite source terms at the two ends of the passive seam. Reversing
the supplying endpoint reverses the remote change at all 61 surveyed
interior addresses four to six hops away. At four hops the independent
position and injection meters resolve shifts of about $+2.33$ and
$-2.23$ occupied appointments per injection. The background stream
can carry the change farther. Its sign depends on how the ordinary
material is supplied.

A separate six-port converter transfers the 8 or 9 energy units of an
ordinary bond into one permanent written head. The initial and terminal
whole-module energy spectra are disjoint even where their means agree.
The pulse controller must participate in the energy exchange. On the
original unmodified blank board one ordinary load has total energy at
most 54. A separated retained load plus one emptied unit-contrast head,
with every other head blank, costs at least 56. This restricts the
conversion of a single closed supply.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture}{Regional energy, vacuum logarithm, observation density and distant-probe bounds};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/supply}{Conversion, controller work and full energy spectra}.}

The profile tests spatial shape as well as total supply. Under spherical
weak gravity, with compact baryons inside 62.71 kpc, the 32-record
outward trail permits an outer-to-inner equivalent speed ratio of at
most 0.855 at 291.07 kpc, over every common hop length and free age.
The four central lensing ratios are 1.023, 1.047, 1.070 and 1.007.
The largest discrepancy is 1.88 standard deviations after allowing both
systematic errors and any radial statistical covariance. With only
half the baryons inside the smaller radius the ceiling becomes 1.074.
Changing stack weights further limits this comparison. On individual
rotation curves the corresponding total-energy ceiling can be tested
against the resolved baryonic contribution without a common lens weight.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/compensation/trail_profile}{Two-aperture test, baryon sensitivities and source positions};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/individual_trail_budget}{Individual SPARC rotation curves, gas, stellar and distance uncertainties}.}

In 118 individual bulgeless SPARC galaxies, the catalogue values for
40 outer orbits exceed even a generous 32-record ceiling of 5.386
times the entire baryon inventory, with all the excess placed inside
the measured radius. At the paid ratio of 5.134 the count is 43.
Allowing 50\% more gas, a stellar mass-to-light ratio up to 0.8 and
favorable two-error shifts in the tabulated quantities leaves NGC~3741.
Its required speed floor of 43.35 km/s exceeds the 41.57 km/s ceiling.
Removing the observers' full uncertain pressure correction closes the
gap. These identified uncertainties matter for a claim about any one
system.%
\footnote{\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1606.09251}{Lelli et al., SPARC rotation curves};
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/astro-ph/0611355}{Gentile et al., NGC 3741 rotation and pressure correction}.}

An independent distance comparison uses DESI's full second-release
baryon acoustic oscillation covariance and a sound horizon computed for
each stock. Fit only $H_0$ to seven low-redshift distances and hold six
higher-redshift distances for comparison in flat cold-fluid cosmology.
The 14-, 32- and 64-record preparations give held quadratic residuals of
455.1, 1.01 and 226.2, with 1.95 for the standard abundance. The middle
preparation gives $H_0=68.58$ km s$^{-1}$ Mpc$^{-1}$; at $z=2.33$ its
radial distance divided by the sound horizon is 8.666 versus
$8.632\pm0.101$ measured. Keeping $H_0=70$ instead gives 28.63 on all
13 distances. Freeing both the expansion rate and the ruler makes all
three abundances compatible with these distances. The match therefore
depends on the calculated standard cold-fluid ruler.

Allow any universal baryon-weighted mixture of the three trail lengths.
Their generous local inventory ceilings each exceed their delivered
ratio $q$ by exactly $51/202$, so the same difference holds for every
mixture. Fitting all 13 distances with a physical baryon-density
calibration $0.02218\pm0.00055$ gives $q=5.263$ and
$H_0=68.53$ km s$^{-1}$ Mpc$^{-1}$. Profiling the expansion rate and
baryon density gives the nominal 95\% interval $4.842$--$5.723$ and a
maximum galaxy inventory of $5.9754$ times the baryonic mass.

At this ceiling every outermost eligible SPARC orbit fits the favorable
two-error allowances. Using all 1,621 eligible radii with one distance,
inclination and baryonic normalization per component per galaxy gives
117 feasible spherical mass profiles among 118 galaxies. Each profile
is free to place positive mass in any shell. At successive radii the
least possible enclosed mass is the running maximum of zero and the
lower bounds inferred from the speed intervals. Feasibility requires
this mass to respect every local upper bound and the inventory ceiling.
The 11 rings of F571-8 require a ceiling of at least $6.7850$, or
$q\simeq6.533$, where the distance and baryon-density likelihood costs
$\Delta\chi^2=25.33$. Removing its outermost ring suffices to admit
a profile. The inclination is $85\pm5$ degrees, and optical-depth and
projection systematics affect the rotation-curve comparison. The extreme
galaxy allowance admits all 118 fitted profiles. None of these fits
derives a radial source distribution from overlap records.%
\footnote{\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14738}{DESI DR2 distances and baryon calibration};
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/astro-ph/0202075}{Marchesini et al., optical and H~I rotation curves};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao}{Distance profiles, ring data and mass reconstructions}.}

\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\IfFileExists{code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/mixture.pdf}
  {\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/mixture.pdf}}
  {\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{../code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/mixture.pdf}}
\caption{Left, the distance likelihood with the Hubble constant and
baryon density profiled at each paid ratio. The diamond marks the ratio
required by the resolved F571-8 curve under the two-error allowances.
Right, measured speeds and two independently fitted spherical profiles.
At the cosmic ceiling the dashed curve minimizes the largest widening
of the allowed squared-speed intervals in units of the corresponding
measured squared speed. The higher inventory admits the solid curve
inside all the intervals. Both fits use baryonic and geometric nuisance
parameters shared across the galaxy's radii.}
\label{fig:universal-paid-mixture}
\end{figure}

We also predict a distribution before reading each target galaxy's
rotation curve. Use the measured shell fractions of the outward
32-write source, grant it a gravitating inventory of 5.386 times the
catalogued baryonic mass, and use ordinary Newtonian gravity for its
spherical monopole. Calibrate a single hop length in units of disk
scale length on the other galaxies. With a central launch on 118
bulgeless SPARC galaxies, the median absolute speed error over 1,621
rings is 17.99 km/s, compared with 42.37 km/s for baryons alone.
For ten galaxies with independently mapped stellar and gas surface
densities, distribute the launches in proportion to measured annular
mass. The median error across 369 rings becomes 16.03 km/s, compared
with 20.24 km/s for a central launch and 40.27 km/s for baryons alone.
At catalogue geometry, 87 of the 369 mapped predictions fall within
twice the quoted speed errors. Each mapped galaxy has misses.
The largest-radius predictions for NGC~2841 and NGC~5055 miss by
$-89.7$ and $+43.7$ km/s. These retrospective predictions test a
specified placement, abundance and conversion to gravitational mass.
Deriving the physical placement, abundance and coupling from the native
source is a separate requirement for the astronomical identification.%
\footnote{\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1606.09251}{Lelli et al., SPARC rotation curves},
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/0810.2556}{Leroy et al., resolved surface densities},
and \href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao}{shell transport, held calibrations and ring predictions}.}

The local potential measured by the cold probe gives a second astronomical
test. Grant a potential proportional to minus the density of written
contact ends, seed the measured trail on the same baryonic annuli and
average the radial work over a two-percent window on each side of each
measured radius. Fit one positive conversion and one hop length on the
other nine galaxies. For the outward and folded trails the held median
speed errors are 38.55 and 38.40 km/s. Only 39 and 41 of the 369 rings,
respectively, fall within twice the quoted speed errors. Even granting
DDO~154 its own length and positive normalization fails at radii 0.99
and 1.97 kpc for the outward trail and with the additional 3.46 kpc
radius for the folded trail. This comparison holds catalogue geometry
and baryonic contributions fixed. The finite contact mechanism binds a
nearby probe, but its local density dependence does not account for
the measured orbital profiles under this transfer.

The clock profile of the single written contact in the mobile ordinary
medium gives a median error of 40.27 km/s and 26 of 369 rings within
twice their quoted speed errors under the same held calibration. Nine
of the ten training sets select zero extra clock-potential amplitude.
Using the signed site and neighbor contributions to the exterior
release field after a sharp ordinary address read gives 39.89 km/s and
34 of 369 rings. With the ordinary source left in equilibrium, the
release field gives 39.88 km/s and 33 of 369. These comparisons grant
linear superposition of
isolated sources on the baryonic maps and a conversion from a release
observable to a circular-orbit potential.

Free evolution of a common uniform in-phase ordinary launch gives an
independent measurement of persistence. On the same 240-port chart an
observer samples times uniformly up to 65,536 native units. Position and
interference readings on separate copies imply changes of enclosed probe
probability acceleration of $[0.0110218,0.0110289]$ on the first exterior
cut and $[-0.0059604,-0.0059566]$ on the second, at joint 99\% counting
coverage in the executable model. A rational spectral enclosure on the
finite generator proves these signs for every sufficiently long uniform
age window. Sources with the same modular reading and opposite phases
on nonadjacent pairs give opposite changes at the first cut. Using the
measured in-phase source field as the local potential predicts 23 of 369
held speeds within twice their quoted errors, with median error
41.66 km/s. The common ordinary material preparation determines which
response observers measure.

How much freedom in the distribution would be needed to fit these
galaxies? Allow all baryonic annuli in a galaxy to deposit at any common
mixture of isotropic offsets. With the local density potential, separate
positive distributions and amplitudes fitted to each galaxy admit
all 369 observed speeds at twice their quoted errors. The joint vector
of responses is affine in inverse offset between annular corners, so
a finite convex hull covers every displacement. Rational witnesses
verify all the original intervals. A positive distribution learned on
the other nine galaxies fits 46 held speeds with median error 41.43 km/s.
Granting separate positive and negative deposits gives 28 held speeds
and 62.67 km/s. The fitted shapes require an independent production law
to connect them to the measured source.

Even with signed deposits, NGC 2841 needs at least
$5.33595\times10^7\,(\mathrm{km/s})^2\,\mathrm{kpc}^3/(10^9 M_\odot)$
as a common cap on the two local-potential strengths. Every stationary one-unit
medium on this degree-six chart has integrated field at most
$4\kappa(1+6)=224$ native units. If a baryonic proton mass supplies
one such source, the full 192-unit insertion gap supplies the probe
rest energy and fields superpose linearly, a cubic site must span at
least 0.2325 mm. Identifying the same probe with a proton through its
free kinetic curvature yields $2.914\times10^{-15}$ m per cubic hop.
These simultaneous microscopic identifications fall short by a factor
of $5.08\times10^{32}$ in amplitude. A separate test assigns deposits
positive gravitating mass at ordinary Newtonian $G$. At fixed catalogue
geometry, even arbitrary isotropic transportation from the measured
baryons fits only three complete curves at the 32-write inventory cap
or four at the conditional 95\% cosmic cap. The latter adds DDO 154.
These are constraints on specified source, geometry and coupling
assignments.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao}{Source distributions, numerical witnesses, rational bounds and held rotation curves}.}

A positive finite source-to-motion implication follows at weak contact
exposure. Write the blank kinetic operator as $D-a$. For its positive
normalized lowest mode $\phi$ the measured site probability is
$p_x=\phi_x^2$. The shared spatial coefficients are
$c_{xy}=a_{xy}\phi_x\phi_y$. For a perturbation $\epsilon V$ of the
native generator let $s_x=-\phi_x(V\phi)_x$. Differentiating its finite
eigen-equation gives
\[
     L_c u=s-\Big(\sum_xs_x\Big)p,\qquad
     \partial_\epsilon p_x=2p_xu_x,\qquad \sum_xp_xu_x=0 .
\]
The same weighted graph Poisson equation applies to a clamped ordinary
double load and to a different measured written contact. The native
exchange and an ordinary insertion-frequency measurement set the
coupling before observing the held write.

A finite preparation test starts a mobile unit in the lowest band of a
239-site active chart. A controller exposes the clamped load or written
contact for one part in 10,000 of each native cycle. The all-site
relative discrepancies between the first-order prediction and the
simulated occupation changes are 0.495\% on ordinary loading and
0.192\% on writing. Restore the blank contacts and release a separate
double probe without observing the ordinary unit on that copy.
Independent ordinary position and interference counts force extra
inward transfer on four exterior boundaries, at two, three, four and
six hops from the written seam. The same inference works on four
ordinary-source cuts. At two hops the source-only initial acceleration
interval is $[3.9637,4.1052]\times10^{-5}$ in native units. At time
$1/16$ the ordinary-calibrated first-order prediction is an additional
enclosed probability of $5.643\times10^{-8}$. Independently sampled
terminal records enclose it at joint 99\% counting coverage. The
controlled exposure, prepared band and material receiver specify the
domain of this result.

The timing of the written source is observable. On a four-owner
repair service the middle seam of the 2200 ordinary inventory carries
a magnitude-two record on a stationary fraction $424/1055$ of ticks.
The 1100 inventory never produces that record. A supplied relay on a
different chart applies a short pulse on each active tick. Its width
was fixed at $10^{-4}/(424/1055)$ of a tick before four new runs.
Across those runs, sampling ages uniformly through 8,192 or 32,768
ticks gives positive changes in initial enclosed probe acceleration
at all four cuts, inferred from the ordinary positions and two-port
interference alone. In the longer window the six-hop intervals range
from $6.1174\times10^{-6}$ to $1.0111\times10^{-5}$ in native units.
Keeping the same contact connected for the entire active tick
decreases the four- and six-hop responses in every one of these
eight ensembles. In the longer window its two-hop response increases
on every run. The source selects the arrival times. The duration
and relay are conditions of this implication.

Equal cumulative exposure does not determine this sign. For the
first 512 ticks of a separate service record, compare a short
pulse on every active copy with a full-tick pulse on a calibrated
fraction of copies. The expected integrated exposure agrees after
every tick. The two-hop acceleration changes are respectively
$[3.4120,3.5533]\times10^{-5}$ and
$[-1.6110,-1.6102]\times10^{-6}$. In the short-pulse preparation
the six-hop sign is negative over this early age window.
Both the measured timing and the ordinary material response matter
when extending the inference to a larger region.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\IfFileExists{code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/poisson_comparison.pdf}
  {\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/poisson_comparison.pdf}}
  {\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{../code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/poisson_comparison.pdf}}
\caption{Left, additional enclosed probability for a released double at
native time $1/16$. Each cross uses the blank detector and the independent
ordinary calibration. Right, spherical extra mass required by individual
NGC 2841 orbital speeds at fixed catalogue parameters. The highlighted
intervals at 18.48 and 63.64 kpc require a mass increase of at least
$6.50468\,M_b$ outside the Leroy radial map.}
\label{fig:poisson-contact-orbits}
\end{figure}

With a permanent write, min--max gives exactly two bound modes with
energies between $-8$ and $-11/2$. Their amplitudes decrease by at least
the factor $6/17$ per hop in the exterior maximum bound. Preparing the
ordinary unit at either endpoint gives about 97.5\% weight to these
modes on all three native sizes. Its complete local release field
predicts 32 of 369 held galaxy speeds within twice their quoted errors.
For weak local writes, granting a three-dimensional Poisson continuation
and a constant writing rate per baryonic mass gives 38 held speeds for
the disk baryon field or 65 for the independent spherical annular maps.
Even arbitrary positive spherical distributions confined to each
observed map can fit only three whole curves. In NGC 2841 the minimum
extra mass needed between two exterior radii exceeds the entire
conditional cosmic inventory, as shown in
Fig.~\ref{fig:poisson-contact-orbits}. The native experiment supplies a
finite Poisson susceptibility and a measured release prediction. A
galactic mechanism would also have to select the source outside these
mapped origins and determine the ordinary matter and probe calibration
in astronomical units.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao}{Held-transfer results, independent orbital bands and exterior mass certificates}.}

NGC 2841 has observed stars far outside the 17 kpc edge of the
Leroy profile. Dragonfly traces their light to about 70 kpc and
reports $(1.4\pm0.2)\times10^8\,M_\odot$ of stars beyond about
30 kpc. At the same distance of 14.1 Mpc, the frozen SPARC
baryonic assignment and speed intervals require at least
$4.8609\times10^{11}\,M_\odot$ of additional spherical gravitating
mass between 30.70 and 63.64 kpc. Assigning the entire measured
outer stellar inventory at its quoted central value plus twice
the error would require more than 2,700 additional gravitating
solar masses per solar mass of these stars. Including the whole
galaxy's detected atomic and molecular gas with helium reduces
the required ratio to at least 38.78. These are conditional
conversion requirements for the detected components.%
\footnote{J. Zhang et al., \emph{The Dragonfly Nearby Galaxies Survey.
IV. A Giant Stellar Disk in NGC 2841},
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.02583}{ApJ 855, 78 (2018)},
Secs.~3--4.}

A radial test uses the THINGS atomic-gas image and the approximate
stellar-to-gas surface-density ratio of three over 20--50 kpc.
Allow an arbitrary positive interior excess and a constant local
conversion. Eight inner rings allow conversions of 7.83--15.26.
All seven held rings separately intersect a prediction band, but the
43.08--49.19 kpc pair alone requires a shell conversion of at least
26.01. Its ratio to the allowed 20.57--34.88 kpc shell conversion is
at least 1.704 on these nominal reductions. A constant multiplier on
the gas profile cancels from that ratio. Applying the reported local
stellar-to-gas ratio to our fixed-orientation radio apertures also
assigns $1.142\times10^{10}\,M_\odot$ of stars to 30.70--49.19 kpc,
over sixty times the separately reported integrated outer stellar mass
even after adding two quoted errors. The two reductions therefore
cannot be combined into a calibrated stellar source. The warp and
aperture choices matter for any quantitative transfer. An OPH
prediction must determine the extra source profile independently of
the orbit used to test it.

\paragraph{A connected source read by ordinary pairs.}
Two equal ordinary units supply a physical contact exposure without a
pulse clock. On a stored magnitude-two seam the native generator agrees
with the vacuum head generator on every material word except 11.
When the endpoints each hold one unit, the coin-even head energies
shift by $-4\kappa$ and $-(4\kappa+1)$ at the two reversal parities.
The coin-odd shifts vanish. A written zero cuts the identical kinetic
link and gives zero shift. The commutation of the extra term with the
vacuum head generator makes this an exact decomposition for free
motion. The in-phase uniform two-unit launch on 240 ports gives the
measured pair address probability $1/28680$ before the material
evolves. A single ordinary unit has identical motion on these stored
head sectors. A mixed uniform two-unit preparation is invariant on
every sector and has vanishing response.

On the two physical repair seams compare the native sharp joint
reference $P$ with the independent pairing $Q=P_A\otimes P_B$.
Keeping written zero or two at both locations admits 448 of 2,110
stationary words. Their four multiplicities are $48,176,112,112$.
All 36 admitted sharp labels of the two six-level heads have positive
probability, and $P/Q$ is exactly constant within each of the four
magnitude blocks. The connected logarithm on this entire admitted
support gives
\[
 q=\operatorname{Tr}[(Q-P)(\log Q-\log P)]=\log(11/3)/14 .
\]
The simulated source bank encloses $q$ in $[0.09274,0.09287]$ nats.
For any conditional receiver $Y_{ab}$ the change from $P$ to $Q$ is
$-q(Y_{00}-Y_{01}-Y_{10}+Y_{11})/\log(3/11)$.
In particular an ordinary pair can travel coherently along two
parked alternatives, one at each seam. The individual head phase
factors determine their interference. The charge and conditional
ordinary measurements predict a bright-port probability increase of
$[0.0084412,0.0084461]$ at time $1/32$. Independent counts on the
joint and independently paired arms give $[0.0084333,0.0084540]$.
With the held head table withheld, the same calibration can be
inverted. One million simulated trials per setting give
$[0.0335,0.1508]$ nats for the full source. The null and half-strength
intervals both include zero. This establishes ideal ordinary-meter
sensitivity without a local gravitational measurement.

After uninterrupted source evolution for 64 native time units, erase
the two heads and insert a freely moving double uniformly among the
empty ports. The connected charge and four conditional ordinary
material readings predict its initial enclosed acceleration. It
decreases at two, three and four hops and increases at six. Independent
material readings on the two held arms resolve all four signs
(Fig.~\ref{fig:pair-contact-orbits}). The signs at two and six hops
are both negative at age 16. Thus a measured connected charge closes
the conditional implication to motion. The response of the ordinary
material and the observer's boundary enter into its sign.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\IfFileExists{code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/pair_comparison.pdf}
  {\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/pair_comparison.pdf}}
  {\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{../code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/pair_comparison.pdf}}
\caption{Left, change in initial enclosed probability acceleration
  of the double in native units after 64 time units. Each determination
  uses source or ordinary-material measurements. Right, NGC 2841
  orbital data and forecasts from surface-density products in neighboring
  image cells, with one gain calibrated on the other nine galaxies.}
\label{fig:pair-contact-orbits}
\end{figure}

For independent ordinary occupancies and a common physical depth,
a local pair abundance scales as the product of neighboring surface
densities. The gas image counts opportunities for a pair to sample the
source. Grant a common connected contribution per such opportunity and
a positive spherical gravitational source with that profile. In NGC 2841, integrating the actual atomic-gas image before
radial averaging requires 5.17--5.37 times as much conversion in
the outer 43.08--49.19 kpc shell as in the inner 20.57--34.88 kpc
shell, across six pair estimators and resolutions. Across ten galaxies,
using one gain fitted on the other nine for each withheld curve,
atomic pairs give 93 of 369 speeds within twice their quoted errors.
Adding measured molecular and stellar annuli gives 31, and the
catalogue baryons alone give 26. The respective median absolute errors
are 17.39, 39.70 and 40.27 km/s. No withheld curve passes in full.
A physical source prediction requires independent measurements of the
spatial correlations, depths and coupling.%
\footnote{F. Walter et al., \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/0810.2125}{THINGS};
F. Lelli et al., \href{https://astroweb.cwru.edu/SPARC/}{SPARC}.
The imaged source integrals and all withheld forecasts accompany
the finite pair measurements.}

\paragraph{Predicting the repair source from local records.}
A four-owner experiment takes independent ordinary fillings of two
units with probability $p$ and zero units otherwise. Keep the original
pair of written heads or physically re-pair them among copies with
the same available witness. For native joint masses $a,b,c,d$ in
one witness class of probability $n$, the change in its doubly
positive cell is $(bc-ad)/n$. Their sum $D$ includes every attempted
patch. The receiver counts the two head magnitudes and obtains a
connected change $q=-D\ell$, where
$\ell=\log(P_{00}P_{11}/P_{01}P_{10})$ is the pooled reference log
odds. A separate ordinary detector with measured mixed response $m$
then changes by $-qm/\ell$ for $\ell\ne0$.

At $p=1/16$, re-pairing without a witness gives
$+2.728\times10^{-6}$ nats per attempt and decreases the simulated
ordinary fringe's bright probability by about six parts per million.
Giving the repair access to the third seam reading, including its
blank outcome, yields $-2.315\times10^{-4}$ nats and increases that
probability by about 513 parts per million. Independent counts
resolve both changes. At $p=1/4$, reducing the frequency of blind
re-pairing can give exactly the same positive $q$ as at $p=1/16$.
The reference log odds have opposite signs, so the same material
fringe responds in opposite directions. The signed implication
takes the reference and detector calibration as physical premises.
A dwell of $2\pi/65$ puts the two measured nonzero ordinary phases
close to a half turn. On the fixed 2200 preparation the predicted
bright probability rises by 3.56--3.58 percentage points per
accepted copy. Conditional on the source and phase calibrations,
14,504 ideal independent receiver trials per arm suffice to
distinguish this signed alternative from zero with at most one
percent error in each direction. The physical realization and its
coherence and efficiency determine any laboratory exposure time.

The finite counts also give a source per patch at each specified
filling. Under a common depth and capacity, set $p$ from the
observed surface density of each 0.8 kpc cell. For each of seven
capacities we integrated the predicted source before comparing
orbits, keeping the previous baryonic speeds and spherical
continuation. With the atomic image and no witness, the highest
coverage occurs at eight times the sample peak density. It gives
127 of 369 speeds within twice their quoted errors, including all
34 in NGC 3521, with conversion fitted on the other nine galaxies.
This setting was one of seven tested on the same sample. Adding the
observed stellar and molecular annuli gives at most 45 of 369 over
those seven settings for blind repair.

In NGC 2841, no constant probability of access to the third head
fits all 15 orbits from 20.57 to 49.19 kpc at any tested capacity
on either tracer, even granting free interior excess and conversion.
Allowing access to vary freely by cell admits the 15 observations in
13 of 14 tracer and capacity combinations. To predict that variation,
we opened the shutter only when an independently prepared neighbor's
third seam recorded magnitude two. Its flag rate is fixed by the
same five repair inventories. In the dilute limit a positive
integrated source requires
$\sum_i p_i\bar p_i/\sum_i p_i^2 <570303/900970$, away from equality,
where $\bar p_i$ averages the four neighboring fillings. The ratio
in the observed apertures is 0.867--0.988 across both tracers.
This rule gives negative leading charge throughout the sample and
misses the NGC 2841 shell test at all seven finite settings. An
independent measurement of access and its coupling to macroscopic
geometry is needed.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao}{Measured maps, transfer tests and shell bounds}.}

\paragraph{Equilibrium energy measured on the connected source.}
Two ordinary units equilibrated on the frozen 2200 source give a
measurable energy increment. Keep the source weights fixed and
normalize the ordinary thermal distribution separately in each
conserved parity sector. At inverse temperature $\beta=\log(8/7)$,
spectroscopy on the ordinary occupied and empty contacts fixes their
conditional Hamiltonians. The measured connected source predicts an
internal-energy increase of $[0.00049352,0.00049438]$ native units
upon re-pairing. A separate simulated energy reading gives
$[0.00049222,0.00049531]$. The reversible isothermal free-energy
change is $[0.00004164,0.00004172]$; the remaining internal-energy
change is heat into the ordinary pair. These figures concern the
open material region and the specified thermostat.

Two gravitational identifications can be tested separately. If the
thermal positional work itself gives the central potential, its
change from four to six hops is at most $3.274\times10^{-9}$ of that
from two to four in absolute value. In NGC 2841, integrating the
observed excess squared speeds in log radius gives an outer-to-inner
work ratio of at least 0.5211 at 20.57, 40.99 and 61.56 kpc, with
linear interpolation in log radius and twice the quoted speed errors.
The assumed correspondence is about 10.25 kpc per hop. Reversing the
source comparison preserves the ratio.

Alternatively, a local change in energy could source an extended
geometric field. The measured mixed internal-energy response per unit
change $D$ of the doubly positive probability is 0.0069153.
Compare the ordinary double's uniform mode on the blank board with
a disconnected storage rail. Its insertion energy is 192 on the
native six-slot completion, with phase readings in $[191.9706,192.0302]$.
If this energy calibrates ordinary mass, four owners with mean
inventory $4p$ doubles provide an absolute abundance test. Only a
patch with a loaded owner can supply a magnitude-two head, so
matching the two marginals gives $|D|\leq 1-(1-p)^4\leq4p$ per
attempt. One equilibrated pair per patch therefore supplies at most
$3.603\times10^{-5}$ times its ordinary inventory in extra
energy-equivalent mass. The Planck base-model cold-to-baryon ratio
is about 5.36. With ordinary Newtonian coupling all 28 measured
map, capacity and recovery combinations give 26 of 369 orbital
speeds within two quoted errors, the same count as baryons alone.
The largest speed change is below 0.000079 km/s. Counting the
ordinary material of arbitrarily many independent reader pairs
also bounds their fractional energy increase below 0.00004440.
These limits concern the thermal energy and its stated mass conversion.
A proposed additional geometric stress requires a measured source
and its own coarse-graining implication.

\paragraph{A local measurement of the source and a finite timing consequence.}
The two written contacts also transmit ordinary messages. At each contact a
receiver splits one ordinary unit between its port and a local reference
rail. A single at the sending endpoint shifts the relative phase. A second
half swap gives a dark-port receipt with probability
$c=(1+\cos(\pi/65))/4$ on a positive head and zero on a magnitude-zero
head. Let $x,y$ be the time each of two such routes saves when it beats a
blank bypass, with zero assigned to a slower route. At fixed single-head
frequencies, re-pairing increases both the joint success and joint miss
probabilities by $Dc^2$, where $D$ is the measured change in the doubly
positive head probability. Thus the mean earliest receipt changes by
$Dc^2\min(x,y)$. For the most sensitive pair of stations the source and
ordinary calibration predict a delay of $0.15982$--$0.16013$ blank-hop times.
Independent simulated receiver counts give $0.15323$--$0.16523$.
A freely released single or double on the evacuated chart has exactly the
same position probabilities on both arms at every time. Its local words
on a written seam always have at least one vacant endpoint, so the written
generator acts only on the head. The timing implication closes on these
records. A gravitational interpretation requires a common geometry that
also governs free motion.

On the thermal preparation, observers counted the live boundary links of
balls containing exactly 165, 192 or 219 stations on each copy. All three
enclose both complete source contacts. At volume 165, the change in entropy
of the local head and position records, conditioned on ball membership,
is $0.092806$--$0.092831$ nats to first order. With increasing address
to split tied ranges, ordinary calibration requires a mean loss of
$0.10560$--$0.10569$ links. The independent boundary count instead gives
an increase of $0.0000127$--$0.0000170$. All three volumes exclude this
stationarity law under both tie orders. A second survey includes the two
local phase receipts as shortcuts in its recorded ranges. At volume 192,
the measured charge predicts a boundary increase of about 0.00426 links,
confirmed by independent counts. The ordinary entropy calibration
requires a decrease of about 0.105 links for the same range ordering.
A source contribution to the proper-volume stationarity theorem remains
to be identified.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/THERMAL_RESULTS.md}{Source measurements, timed messages and fixed-volume area tests}.}

\paragraph{An overlap source read through free motion.}
Fill each port on the 240-station chart with one ordinary unit and
release a delocalized excess or vacancy on the two frozen contacts.
Let $K$ be the measured ordinary generator and $V$ the additive frozen
contact energies. For a diagonal position record $M$, write
$C=[K,M]$. Its acceleration is $CK-KC+CV-VC$. Matching the one-head
laws gives zero change in initial acceleration whenever the initial
material preparation is common to the source arms. Once the material
has moved, the measured contact and material records can be correlated.
An observer who measures ordinary site and interference terms on an
exterior cut at triangularly distributed ages can infer the later
terminal occupation from their integral.

At 64 native ticks the source counts and independent material calibration
predict an increase of 129.62--131.94 parts per million in the probability
that the excess is within one live hop of the source. Held position counts
give 130.69--130.89. The vacancy decreases by 120.68--120.87 parts per
million. On ten of 400 intact triangular routes for each probe, the
inferred increments in scalar potential drop fail to sum to zero.
Direct time slopes of the ordinary bond currents confirm the strongest
loop for each probe. These measurements establish the signed changes
in flight probability on this preparation.

In equilibrium the ratios of four-to-six to two-to-four hop work for the
vacancy and the excess are each below $1.336\times10^{-8}$. The outer
NGC 2841 orbit intervals require a ratio above 0.5211 under the preceding
radial identification. Allowing the observer to pool both tracers at
any fixed fraction before taking shell probability ratios also excludes
a common work conversion on 40.99--51.27 and 51.27--61.56 kpc.
An extended gravitational field sourced by the measured energy gives a
separate test. On the same inactive written chart the lowest excess-load
insertion energy is $42.40978$ and the vacancy costs $32.50038$, each
confirmed by a clock comparison with a disconnected rail. Activating
the written contacts adds positive removal costs to either excitation.
Using each ordinary insertion energy as its mass standard, the bounded
re-pairing increment $|D|\le 1/4$ gives at most $0.00019045$ extra mass
per unit of counted ordinary mass in independent such populations.
The Planck base-model cold-to-baryon ratio is about 5.36. For the ten
imaged galaxies all 112 defect, orientation, tracer, capacity and access
choices give 26 of 369 circular speeds within two quoted errors. The
largest speed change is below 0.00166 km/s. The mass conversion and
independent populations are hypotheses of this bound.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/OCCUPIED_RESULTS.md}{Free release, ordinary interference, current circulation and galactic work tests}.}

\paragraph{What a local source reading establishes.}
Observers can measure the overlap contribution without resolving microscopic
gravity. Re-pairing the two source readings at fixed marginals changes the
expectation of a calibrated regional score by the measured displacement
$D$ times the mixed difference of its four sharp-source means. In the
occupied 192-marker survey this predicts an entropy derivative of
$0.10573$--$0.10604$ nats for the vacancy. Independent regional
interference and source-label records give $0.10582$--$0.10596$ nats.
The ordinary load calibration would require the boundary to lose
$3.37$--$3.40$ crossing links. Direct enumeration gives 52 links for
an empty clamp and 44 for an occupied clamp in every phase-receipt
class. The held source keeps that clamp distribution fixed, so the change
is exactly zero. The corresponding excess-load prediction also fails.
Most of the recorded entropy change comes from the source labels and the
identity of the surveyed ball. A second observer receives only its marker
membership and the ordinary material records. On the excess arm the
local source measurement then predicts a change of $0.0008989$--$0.0009028$
nats in a fixed material score. Independent detector outcomes give
$0.0008928$--$0.0009090$ nats. This closes an inference from the source to
a material reading even when its observer never receives the source labels.
A gravitational interpretation requires a finite implication to the common
proper geometry inferred from clock and freely moving material records.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/COHERENT_RESULTS.md}{Regional source calibration, interference measurements and exact area control}.}

\paragraph{A positive response outside the written source.}
Take an ordinary reference oscillator below the inactive material band and
compare its phase with the return of a defect launched at station \(x\).
The narrow-line response measures the diagonal of
\(R_{ab}=(K+aP_A+bP_B-\omega I)^{-1}\) on the two conserved written
contacts. Nonpositive hopping on a connected chart makes every entry of
this inverse positive below the band. Its two-contact mixed difference
is the sum of two positive products visiting both contacts. Hence the
local measurement \(D>0\) entails an increase in the response at every
station. It also entails a finite detector work. For a reference rail
coupled by \(1/16384\) at detuning \(1/16\), moving from one to two hops
costs an additional \(8.57\)--\(9.41\times10^{-14}\) native energy units
on the vacancy chart. This sign follows even where a direct reading of
the level difference is too weak to resolve.

Two ordinary clocks also reconstruct the joint removal frequencies on
the retained pair. Their covariance increases by \(4225/224\) squared
native frequency units. Calibrating a common fluctuating potential on
an additional ordinary load would give opposite signs for a mixed
vacancy--excess clock pair. At 64 ticks the clock law predicts the
excess-load flight, while the ordinary-gain transfer gives about
\(5.74\) parts per million of additional one-hop occupancy against the
measured \(129.33\). On the central-source radial interpretation, every
nonnegative mixture of the tested dispersive profiles gives less than
\(0.00001894\) outer work per inner work throughout the continuous
detuning interval \(1/1024\) to \(32\) below each rounded band edge.
The NGC 2841 orbits require at least \(0.52118\) at the adopted scale.
Distributing the sources across observed atomic or baryonic annuli gives
a second comparison. Choosing the profile and calibrating its amplitude
on nine other disks gives 59 of 369 held speeds within two quoted errors
and no complete rotation curve. All 56 map and orientation choices
exclude a common fit even with arbitrary nonnegative mixtures of the
six measured kernels. This calculation assumes thin-disk superposition
and a common physical length. An extended gravitating stress and common
observer geometry would need a further physical identification.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/CLOCK_SOURCE_RESULTS.md}{Paired clocks, positive exterior responses, finite detector work and orbital comparisons}.}

\paragraph{Pricing the measured correlation with an ordinary thermometer.}
Two observers on separate written contacts can distinguish the overlap
source by recording their local magnitudes on the same preparation.
The native counts give cross-product odds \(R=3/11\). On each of 1,024
new accepted simulated preparations the observers compared just these
two receipts. Conditional on their measured one-contact totals, the
one-sided probability of a table at least this anticorrelated is
\(1.91\times10^{-7}\) under the most favorable member of the enlarged
comparison \(R\geq 0.59\).

This comparison tests an energetic interpretation independently.
For two magnitudes \(i,j\), let \(Z_{ij}\) sum the Boltzmann weights of
the complete written contact energies and ordinary material levels at
\(\beta=\log(8/7)\). Independent fields that price either magnitude
cancel from \(Z_{00}Z_{22}/(Z_{02}Z_{20})\). Ordinary energy-filter
counts give odds near \(0.99999\) for one vacancy and \(0.99496\) for
one excess load. For every fixed inventory of independent copies of
either material sharing the same two contacts, the finite lower bound
is \(0.59460\). The head lines are summed only after multiplying the
traces of all copies on each line. An equilibrium preparation of this
family would require at least \(5.83\) further native energy units
in the joint free-energy difference to reproduce the recorded odds.
This price is inferred from the hypothesis of equilibrium. The protected
record population has its own preparation law.

A distinct astronomical test grants that the connected logarithm at each
local filling supplies gravitating energy \(q/\beta\). Two independently
measured ordinary insertion energies fix its mass conversion. For the
four-owner inventory, every filling and every repair preserving the two
marginals supplies at most \(0.01214\) times the ordinary mass in absolute
value. The Planck cold-to-baryon benchmark is \(5.36\). On the spherical
weak-field transfer, all 56 forecasts from the observed gas or baryonic
maps give 26 of 369 speeds within two quoted errors, equal to the
baryon-only count. The largest speed change is \(0.181\ {\rm km\,s^{-1}}\)
or less. The local measurement establishes the correlation.
A conserved stress with a measured spatial distribution and the ordinary
gravitational coupling remains the physical premise.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/EQUILIBRIUM_RESULTS.md}{Local measurements, finite inventory bound and mass conversion}.}

\paragraph{An ordinary local measurement of the same modular displacement.}
Retain all four original loads and all three memories in the closed
2200 preparation. Across its 448 accepted sharp words the middle load
\(n\) is zero or two exactly when both selected heads have magnitude
zero. This projector identity makes the source accessible to a single
ordinary material reader. At fixed one-head marginals the change \(D\)
of the double-two probability equals \(\delta\langle(n-1)^2\rangle\).
On each of five blank bonds from that home port to a vacant neighbor
the native sharp energy obeys
\[
 \delta E={31\over4}D+{65\over4}\delta\langle n\rangle .
\]
For independent head frequencies at fixed mean load this gives
\(31/56\) native units per bond. A spectrometer gives \(0.5518\)--\(0.5554\).
A neighboring observer whose return protocol parks an occupied target
in a side rail must also see the half round trip shorten by \(1/28\)
of a hop. Independent receipts give \(0.03566\)--\(0.03578\).
On 32,768 simulated boards a feedback preparation reached all selected
targets by native sharp appointments without exchanging material
between boards. The address service and its supply of work are part
of the preparation.

For an ordinary load inserted next to the middle port the response
depends on the load. A single has an added insertion cost \(3D/4\);
a double's cost is unchanged at fixed mean middle load. A common scalar
calibrated on an independently added ordinary unit would predict
\(0.1014\)--\(0.1034\) units for the double, while its measured shift
lies within \(0.00050\) of zero.

The same local source need not increase the total conserved energy.
Retaining the native conditional word law in each two-head cell gives
\(-1993/1848\) native units on the whole returned board. In two
preparations matching all four mean loads and the three original
path energies, the same \(D\) permits changes of \(-16.69\) and \(+17.34\)
units to the precision shown. On the common transparent echo chart even
an arbitrary signed port density inferred from all radius-two surveys
fails to reproduce the radius-four regional entropy slopes. In the
four-owner image population the absolute gravitating energy obtained
from any compatible returned preparation, including coherent ones,
is below \(0.00868\) times the ordinary mass at the blank-double
calibration. Granting either sign and varying that energy from patch
to patch gives an envelope containing at most 29 of 369 speeds
within two quoted errors on the spherical transfer. This local energy
and clock implication therefore closes on the measured charge;
a universal gravitating stress with the observed abundance remains
open.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/CLOSED_SOURCE_RESULTS.md}{Closed-source interventions, energy spectroscopy, regional density tests and galaxy forecasts}.}

A third local record predicts a distant material reading. Park the four
original loads and time the first passage and return of a double load
through randomly addressed ordinary contacts. The third head passes it
exactly when blank. In the minimal repair its blank fraction increases
from \(3/14\) to \(101/462\). Between exterior ports 25 and 36 this
shortens the mean round trip by 19.04 appointment ticks. The predicted
increase in three-tick arrivals is 20.04 per million and an independent
simulated receiver measures 12.91 to 25.63 per million. A single load
resolves a further orientation of a magnitude-one head. At identical
two-head charge and returned mean energy, stationary preparations permit
both signs of the distant delay. The third-head read closes the
double-load timing premise.

Interpreting that clock as a gravitational potential gives a separate
test. Outside a compact source the time relative to a uniform partner
depends on the square of a harmonic contact voltage. With the sign of
an attractive well, its enclosed flux can only decrease outward.
In 91 of 108 eligible bulgeless SPARC galaxies the inferred extra mass
must grow between two outer radii at the fixed baryonic reduction.
In 63 the growth survives the tested distance, inclination and baryonic
normalization ranges. Alternatively, placing independent compensated
clock profiles throughout ten galaxies according to the measured images
and granting one fitted gravitational gain accommodates at most 143
of 369 speeds within two quoted errors across the 140 tested settings.
The missing physical implication is from these shared records to the
stress and radial force at the ordinary gravitational calibration.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/CLOSED_SOURCE_RESULTS.md}{Source measurements, first-passage experiment and finite clock theorem};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/PASSAGE_MASS.md}{Compact and spatially distributed galaxy tests};
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1606.09251}{SPARC mass models}.}

Ordinary photons are one possible source of the commissioning work.
If a fraction $f_i$ of the final pressureless stock forms at scale
factor $a_i$ after nucleosynthesis and before recombination from photon
energy alone, the past excess of comoving photon energy above its final
value is $R=(q\omega_b/\omega_{\gamma,0})\sum_i f_i a_i$ in units of
that final value. With baryon number and decoupled neutrinos preserved,
exact blackbody restoration increases the baryon-to-photon ratio by
$(1+R)^{3/4}$ and the neutrino-to-photon energy ratio by $1+R$.
Forming the whole middle stock at $z=10{,}000$ would consume 31.7\% of
the original photons' energy and give $N_{\rm eff}=4.46$. Separate
two-error sensitivity checks on the published baryon densities and
$N_{\rm eff}$ favor a burst above redshift about $48{,}000$ for this
specific supply. They use conventional background fits. A cosmological
formation law must also predict perturbations through the transfer.%
\footnote{\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14738}{DESI DR2 distances and nucleosynthesis prior};
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.06209}{Planck density and radiation benchmarks};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao}{Independent distance predictions};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/record_abundance/photon_supply}{Photon-energy accounting and arbitrary formation histories}.}

\section{The Deep-Regime Profile}
\label{sec:deep}

Around a baryonic mass \(M_b\), the proposed collar-count interpretation
assigns a number of settled cuts proportional to the enclosing radius
\(r\). The precise deep-regime characterization uses the following two
premises, each independently falsifiable.

\begin{premise}[Deep-regime scale covariance]
\label{pre:cov}
In the deep regime the enclosed anomalous mass is degree-one homogeneous in
the radius: \(M_A(M_b,\lambda r)=\lambda\,M_A(M_b,r)\) for every
\(\lambda>0\). A sparsely recorded deep cut retains no length scale, so
rescaling the radius rescales the enclosed mass by the same factor.
\end{premise}

\begin{premise}[Quadrature composition]
\label{pre:quad}
The anomalous amplitudes of independent baryonic sources compose in
quadrature:
\(M_A(m_1+m_2,r)^2=M_A(m_1,r)^2+M_A(m_2,r)^2\). The collar defects of
distinct sources are independent, their variances add, and the gravitating
anomalous charge is the amplitude of the unrecovered record fluctuation.
\end{premise}

\begin{theorem}[Profile characterization]
\label{thm:char}
Let \(M_A(M_b,r)\) be nonnegative, monotone in the source, somewhere
nonzero, and satisfy Premises~\ref{pre:cov} and \ref{pre:quad}. Then there
is a unique constant \(a_0>0\) with
\begin{equation}
M_A(r)=r\sqrt{\frac{M_ba_0}{G}},
\qquad
\rho_A(r)=\frac{1}{4\pi r^2}\sqrt{\frac{M_ba_0}{G}}.
\label{eq:profile}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Scale covariance factorizes \(M_A(M_b,r)=r\,f(M_b)\). Quadrature
composition makes \(f^2\) additive on positive sources, monotonicity makes
it monotone, and a function additive and monotone on the positive reals is
linear: \(f^2=\lambda M_b\) with \(\lambda>0\) by nondegeneracy. Setting
\(a_0=\lambda G\) gives \eqref{eq:profile}; uniqueness follows by
evaluating at \(M_b=r=1\). Each premise is load-bearing: an additive law
\(M_A\propto rM_b\) satisfies everything except quadrature composition, and
an \(r^2\) law everything except scale covariance.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Linear enclosed mass]
\label{pre:linear}
The deep-regime density is the shell derivative of the enclosed mass,
\(M_A'(r)=4\pi r^2\rho_A(r)\), with \(M_A\) linear in \(r\) and the
coefficient \(\sqrt{M_ba_0/G}\) forced by
Theorem~\ref{thm:char}.
\end{corollary}

Deep-limit scale invariance is the known symmetry of the modified-dynamics
limit~\cite{milgrom2009}, and square-root mass laws appear in
entropic-gravity proposals~\cite{verlinde2016}. The resulting law is the
deep limit of modified Newtonian dynamics, \(a_A=\sqrt{a_ba_0}\), with its
baryonic Tully--Fisher corollary; the characterization adds that the two
premises force it jointly, and it reads the square root as the variance
additivity of independent collar defects under source composition. It does
not fix \(a_0\).

Premise~\ref{pre:quad} itself follows from a more primitive per-cut model.
Suppose the \(nr\) nested cuts within radius \(r\) each carry anomalous
amplitude \(\sqrt{V(M_b)}\), the standard deviation of the collar defect
sourced by the baryons, with the variances of independent sources adding
and the variance monotone in the source. Then the variance function is
forced linear, \(V(M_b)=cM_b\) with a unique \(c>0\), quadrature
composition is a theorem rather than a premise, the induced law satisfies
Theorem~\ref{thm:char}, and its constant is exactly
\begin{equation}
a_0=G\,n^2c .
\label{eq:dictionary}
\end{equation}
The dictionary is exact but determines only the product \(n^2c\): two
models with different cut densities and equal \(n^2c\) induce the same
enclosed-mass law, machine-checked. The magnitude question is therefore
the value of the collar density and of the per-cut variance constant;
neither is derived here.

One sufficient realization of both premises rests one level deeper. Variance
additivity follows from probabilistic independence: for atomic defect
contributions with finite second moments, pairwise independence, and a
common per-atom variance on one probability space, the variance of the
compound defect of every finite atom family is the sum of the per-atom
variances, so disjoint-union composition is additive and \(V(m)=cm\) on
the modeled masses. Independence is stronger than necessary; pairwise zero
covariance and other dependent but uncorrelated constructions are viable.
An exact linear-asymptotic cut count follows from one rigid geometry: a
cut family with a positive first cut, consecutive gaps of at least \(s\),
and every length-\(s\) interval meeting a cut is exactly the arithmetic
progression with spacing \(s\), its count obeys
\(r/s-1\le N(r)\le r/s+1\), and the counted enclosed mass deviates from
the linear law by at most one per-cut amplitude at every radius, with
\(n=1/s\). A perfectly correlated pair and a one-cut family show only that
the conclusions do not follow after deleting every decorrelation or density
condition. They do not make independence or exact spacing necessary.
Irregular or ergodic cut families with asymptotic density and sublinear count
error are possible. The exact \(nr\) per-cut law is the continuum or
mean extension; the discrete count above differs from it by a bounded error.

\paragraph{A material measurement of the unrecovered amplitude.}
On the closed four-home preparation, a single native write between
the middle load \(n\) and an ordinary reference of one stores the
signed contrast \(X=n-1\). Its mean is zero. Its variance changes from
\(3/28\) to \(5/28\), so the measured modular increment is precisely
\(\log(11/3)\,\delta\operatorname{Var}(X)\). An ordinary single launched
from a fair side crosses the written contact with probability \(1/4\)
on magnitude one. An equal-load write blocks the probe. In a second
instrument the terminal record compares this probe's first crossing
to a fair walker's first return on a ring of \(2L\) ports. When the
appointments independently sample written contacts with single-trial
crossing fraction \(h\), the two-step first-return recurrence gives
\[
 P_L=\sqrt h\,\tanh\!\big(L\operatorname{atanh}\sqrt h\big).
\]
Thus an observer can read an amplitude from ordinary material arrivals;
the apparatus supplies the ring, the clock and the sampling law. At
\(L=23\), using \(h=\operatorname{Var}(X)/4\), the predicted rates
are 16.350\% and 21.127\%; independent simulated records give
16.341\% and 21.023\%. A second calibrated two-attempt receiver also
resolves the change. Repeated visits to one persistent head instead
give a probability linear in its population fraction. A forward
courier can recover square-root scaling through local visits to
regularly spaced marks. The motion of the observer and the spatial
extent of the sampled source are measurable premises.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\IfFileExists{model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/fluctuation_return.pdf}

four at the same radius. At the final radius they enclose identical

\end{figure}

The four centered home loads sum to zero. Their covariance matrix
\(C\) therefore gives regional variance
\(V(B)=-\sum_{i\in B,j\notin B}C_{ij}\).
The whole increase in middle variance comes from its anticorrelation
with home 8. A station equidistant from the four homes assigns zero
RMS to every cut. At the middle station the repair increases the
sum of RMS over cuts by 0.73669 in load units times hop distance,
even though both observers eventually enclose the same region.
This prescription fails to give a common total mass on the chart.
There is a unique additive extension preserving the measured one-port
variances: include the internal covariance term
\(-2\sum_{i<j\in B}C_{ij}\) as well as the cut variance.
The whole-source change then equals \(-46/1617\) in unit-donor
variance units. Preserving the one-port RMS instead gives an additive
increase of 0.01165 unit donors. At the ordinary single-to-double rest
calibration this increment gives at most 0.000138 of the ordinary mass
over the specified four-owner populations.
If positive per-cut amplitudes instead accumulate only about a
designated galactic center, the weaker condition of nondecreasing
convex enclosed mass is compatible with all 108 eligible outer
bulgeless SPARC curves. Directly predicting those amplitudes from
independently sampled imaged patches is more restrictive. For ten
mapped galaxies the direct variance increment accommodates at most
157 of 369 speeds with one fitted gain; calibration on the other
nine gives at best 109 held speeds within two quoted errors across
the tested settings. On 22 independent SLACS lenses the disk-calibrated
direct increment also predicts an outer shear contrast. Across the
twenty eligible stellar-mass and receiver settings, the predictions
lie inside the box obtained by moving each of the two published bins
by two quoted errors. That same box includes zero. This comparison
assumes compact stellar sources, a de Vaucouleurs mass profile and the
same exterior potential for stars and light. A common stress for light and orbital motion requires an independent
calibration on the same source.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/FLUCTUATION_RESULTS.md}{Source, receiver, spatial correlations and finite proofs};

\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/astro-ph/0701589}{SLACS shear};
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/0911.2471}{SLACS stellar masses}.}

\paragraph{A source field shared by two ordinary probes.}
A blank-contact spectrometer resolves \(8(n-t)^2\) and its exchange
parity separately. Subtracting the contrast readings for each isolated
load gives \(-16nt\). Dividing by the test load \(t=1,2\) gives the
same signed field, \(-16n\), on every sharp record. Transport the two
correlated home loads \(n,m\) by reversible blank exchanges to the
ends of a four-port path. The middle variance \(f=\langle(m-1)^2\rangle\)
is the two-zero head frequency. On its nonunit events the partner
load has the opposite deviation. Hence the mean-square field difference
is \(256(v+3f)\), where \(v=\langle(n-1)^2\rangle\). Its mean vanishes.
At fixed single-head frequencies, three compact energies and unit mean
loads, every sharp mixture stationary on the compact source that realizes
the specified \(1/14\) displacement has \(v\ge905/4508\).
The increment in the mean square must exceed five squared field units.
For the canonical repair its exact value is \(4608/77=59.8442\).
Independent simulated single and double spectrometers give simultaneous
99\% intervals of \(51.79\)--\(66.43\) and \(52.32\)--\(66.96\).

The complete energy of bringing the pair one hop closer on a clean
mount changes by an additional \(181/154\) native units in the canonical
repair. A measured increase in \(f\) fixes the sign even when the
partner variance is free, since the mean compression work is
\(-17+65f/4+3v/4\) and \(v\ge f\). Both preparations release energy
on approach. The repaired pair releases less. At the original homes
all 25 two-stroke comparisons of single blank hops have zero mixed work.
