The two lower mobile insertion levels can give the same apparent
increment of 0.01023184 ordinary donors if \(v=0.33999775\) to the
precision shown. An arrival experiment distinguishes this effect from
an equivalent addition of sparse ordinary donors. The distinct
arrival limits in Fig.~\ref{fig:pair-contact-field} hold over every
independent static diagonal field on the original two-port mount.
On the original chart 232 clean ports admit the calibrated field
readout. All four home means are fixed, so the mean field change is
zero throughout that survey. Summing squared differences over its
587 edges decreases the field score by \(173/539\) in squared
single-field units under the canonical repair. Granting a mass
proportional to this score and the full matched range at each imaged
patch covers at most 28 of 369 rotation speeds within two quoted errors.

A pressure contribution to the total conserved energy of these patches
is also bounded. For a separately conserved homogeneous component
with ordinary gravitational coupling, put \(s=1+z,\ e=\rho_x/\rho_b\)
and \(A=(\rho_x+3p_x)/\rho_b\) in units \(c=1\). Conservation gives
\(d(se)/ds=A\). Even allowing arbitrary intermediate pressures,
\(|e|\le0.00868\) bounds the mean active source over the radial DESI
interval \(0.51\le z\le2.33\) by 0.02309 baryon densities.
Independently, the change in \(H^2\) between those endpoints requires
\(|e|>2.82\) at one endpoint for a flat background with constant vacuum
density, drag ruler 125--165 Mpc and the stated ordinary density ranges.
The two radial measurements use a simultaneous 99\% Gaussian rectangle
conditional on these calibrations. A cosmological source with energy
exchange or an additional contribution to the field equations requires
its own transfer law.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/PAIR_FIELD_RESULTS.md}{Contact records, finite bounds and spatial forecasts};
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14738}{DESI DR2};
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17643}{Low-redshift ruler calibration}.}

\paragraph{When a source fluctuation produces a lasting clock delay.}
An ordinary load interrogates five blank bonds beside the middle home.
After monitoring the neighboring loads for exchange, a material
interferometer records a tick on a dark-port arrival. Failed attempts
consume full slots. Let \(p_{tn}\) be the calibrated flag probability
at test load \(t\) and source load \(n\). If the source persists until
the tick, the mean slots per tick at unit mean home load are
\[
 T_t(f)=\frac{1}{p_{t1}}+
 f\left[\frac{1/p_{t0}+1/p_{t2}}{2}-\frac{1}{p_{t1}}\right].
\]
Here \(f\) is the measured two-zero head frequency. Native pulse
amplitudes give a positive coefficient for both instruments.
The specified \(1/14\) increment increases the single's mean waiting
time from 6.03120 to 6.81468 slots and the double's from 2.95158 to
2.98954. Independent simulated banks of 4,194,304 ticks per arm
resolve both delays. With a fresh source at every attempt, the
mean waiting time is \(1/\mathbb{E}p\) and decreases for both instruments.

The same-board free release tests whether the clock delay gives a
potential felt by ordinary material. A standing even-parity probe
starts one and two hops from the middle home. The two placements
have zero initial current. An ordinary unit donor accelerates the
load contrast toward the nearer site. The canonical repair changes
this contrast acceleration by \(-3/56\) per squared native time for
the single and zero for the double. The double's leading extra near-port
contrast is fourth order, with fourth derivative \(-821909/112\).
Among 587 surveyed clean edges the clock contrast changes on 44.
For the single, 36 changes in release acceleration point opposite
to that clock contrast and eight vanish. For the double all 44 vanish.
These are finite-resolution measurements and implications on the native chart.

An independent observational test follows from the residence-time
distribution. If \(u\) is the local rate with event law \(\pi\), the
renewal rate is \(R=1/\mathbb{E}_\pi(1/u)\).
A random-time survey samples \(\mu_i=\pi_i R/u_i\) and has fractional
variance \(s^2=\mathbb{E}_\pi u/R-1\). If the event-mean rate follows
the ordinary lapse and the clock predicts circular motion, the extra
potential difference from the inner to the outer radius is at most
\(c^2\log(1+s_{\rm in}^2)\). Across ten matched disks the required
widths are 33.8--199.6 km/s, each exceeding its own detected
21 cm width. This comparison assumes that individual emissions
sample persistent clock offsets with uncorrelated Doppler scatter.
For rapid modulation the corresponding random-time rates would need
about 11--108 independent samples per emission to reduce the width
to the observed values. No native-to-emission time conversion is imposed.

The same clock admits an image-based forecast. The imaged filling fixes
all five ordinary inventory weights, and the native source distributions
give the mean tick time before and after the repair. Calibrate the
ordinary gain on the bare-load profile and the visible-matter potential
before comparing with the excess. Across two imaged tracers, seven
capacities and both clocks, zero of ten outer potential differences
lie inside their two-error boxes, including calibration on the other
nine galaxies. A fitted common dark gain covers at most four.
The measured source entails a clock effect whose spatial and material
transfer can be tested independently.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/RENEWAL_RESULTS.md}{Native time, release and source-service tests};
\href{https://www2.mpia-hd.mpg.de/THINGS/Data.html}{THINGS data};
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/0903.0183}{H\,\textsc{i} line-width analysis}.}

\paragraph{Can observers measure the gravitating precursor locally?}
On the closed four-owner preparation a joint source count predicts
a positive increment of $31/56$ in the native energy of the ordinary
13--20 bond. A separate observer assigns the line to a random endpoint
and records whether it lies within the later of two independent integer
deadlines. At six choices of center and radius the measured weighted
energy increases as predicted. The same readings also measure the
additional entropy of the spectral line given the middle load. At
zero load the line is unique, while either occupied load gives two
equiprobable parities. The repair increases the zero-load probability
by $1/28$, so the extra conditional entropy tangent is
$-\alpha\log(2)/28$ at gate acceptance $\alpha$. Local work and
regional entropy are experimentally distinguishable on this source.

The middle record has an entropy tangent of $0.200958$ nats on all
integer radii from two through eleven hops. The positive rest-ball
weight requires a gain of at least $0.90431$ nats between those radii
if the radius-two reading sets the source scale. Equal-record
comparisons at 240 centers also exclude every signed port density
representing all these sharp regional readings on the transparent
chart. An ordinary control with blank heads raises the chance of a
double at the middle port from one quarter to three quarters. Its
mean energy rises by 97.5 units, while the sharp regional entropy
tangent stays at half the logarithm of three over the same radii.
The regional measurement thus fails its ordinary-matter calibration.

The five reachable ordinary inventories supply a separate bound.
The empty inventory has zero energy on all 27 reachable records.
Across the other four inventories the full returned Hamiltonian,
compressed to each reachable support, has energy between 117 and 213
units per initial ordinary double. At fixed total fuel, arbitrary
correlations and coherent preparations therefore allow an energy change
of at most 96 units per double. At the measured ordinary conversion this
is at most $0.50008$ additional units of mass per unit of fuel.
Even moving the entire imaged fuel to the center, the minimum integrated
extra mass required between the two resolved radii exceeds this budget
by factors of 1.75--17.33 in the ten enrolled galaxies. This calculation
uses additive closed patches, the fixed baryonic reduction and a
spherical excess-force law. An interaction between patches would require
a separate contribution in the regional energy and geometry.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/NESTED_RESULTS.md}{Local spectral measurement, finite regional tests and galaxy mass bounds}.}

\paragraph{A local branching measurement and a held boundary forecast.}
When ordinary material moves and observers register its history, a
specific overlap contribution survives equilibration. On 12 of the
99 sharp contact inputs a full exchange gives four equiprobable
outcomes. Two copies with identical preparatory readings yield
different noninitial outputs with probability $3/8$ on these inputs
and zero on all others. This is a local test of the proposed precursor.

Let $S$ be the additional joint entropy in bits acquired in one sweep
of interior half exchanges. Send a binary message by pulsing or
waiting. Let $A$ count the distinguishabilities of crossing messages
and $V$ their incidences at enrolled markers. The native transition
matrix and incidence counting give
\[
 S+\tfrac12 A-\tfrac12 V=\gamma F,\qquad
 \gamma=\tfrac94-\tfrac58\log_2 5 ,
\]
where $F$ is the sum of the measured four-way frequencies on interior
seams. Two ordinary aperture measurements fix both coefficients.
The repair raises the summed term by 0.000240109 bits per sweep.
The local composition and written-head readings matter: repairs
matching the original head-table change and the returned energy
can lower the four-way frequency.

In the first surveyed ball enclosing the third written seam, compare
the repair with an ordinary addition mixed to the same communication
volume. Independent history, branching and volume readings predict
a boundary change between $-0.002101$ and $-0.000625$ channel units.
The held boundary gives $-0.001357$ to $-0.001336$. A separate
comparison uses ordinary single and double controls to match volume
exactly and history to within $1.5\times10^{-13}$ bits. Increasing the
measured four-way source then predicts a boundary gain of
0.0007777--0.0007976, confirmed by the held interval
0.0006676--0.0008778. Interior history supplies the larger contribution
in the contracting comparison. At eight hops
19 regions have positive branching increments. At matched volume
12 boundaries contract and seven expand. The observed signs depend
on both the source and the motion records. The operational volume
and boundary in this experiment require an independent connection
to orbital geometry.

Applied to the ten enrolled disks, the accepted-patch abundance and
the ordinary price of the complete history rate cover 26 of 369 speeds
within two quoted errors. Giving the branching term its own common
positive conversion covers at most 141 of 369 across the registered
image tracers and capacities. The repair frequency, patch geometry
and time scale enter this astronomical comparison.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/MOVING_RESULTS.md}{Paired source measurements, fixed-volume records and galaxy comparisons}.}

The same preparations admit a direct test with ordinary moving loads.
Measure the signed transfer on the third written contact at each of
two visits, with a single or double released from either end. These
two readings determine the complete terminal mean load on all 240
stations through the calibrated blank exchanges. Averaging the
two launch directions cancels the excess exactly at every station,
in both service orders. Under indefinitely repeated monitored sweeps,
blank transpositions make every material placement equiprobable at
fixed inventory within a reachable class. The late load increment is
therefore the same at every station even for a one-sided release. The variance of
the signed first transfer per unit load, with the two directions
equally likely, nevertheless increases under the repair by \(19/12936\)
and \(1/462\) for the two loads. An ordinary double addition changes
neither variance. Measuring this fluctuation alone thus leaves the
gravitating-amplitude premise of Theorem~\ref{thm:char} open.
A galaxy continuation that sums this
orientation-balanced material response linearly consequently predicts
only the baryonic speeds at every radius. With the reference SPARC
mass-to-light ratios this accounts for 452 of 3,391 measured speeds
within two quoted errors and four complete curves among 175 galaxies.
Allowing distance, inclination and mass conversions to vary within
the sensitivity ranges in the accompanying computation admits 29
complete curves. This comparison tests the specified linear continuation.

Observers also read a transverse change in their signal geometry.
Two pulses in opposite message orders give a bit with a heralded
success or an erasure. The expected travel time on the written contact
follows from the local transfer to an empty target and a measured
occupied-target correction. Its endpoints are equidistant from the
central observer, so this contact leaves all central radial distances
unchanged. At the same seven-hop radius the repair shortens the mean
transverse range by 0.005095 and 0.011151 pairs of pulses on the two
alphabets. Isolating the positive branching change at matched interior
history and communication volume lengthens those ranges by 0.002923
and 0.005593. The ordinary double addition leaves both means unchanged.
These results identify the measured precursor of a perceived geometry
change. An inference to a common attractive gravitational stress
requires coupling calibration on the same regions.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/MOVING_RESULTS.md}{Finite release, transverse measurements and SPARC comparison}.}

\paragraph{A locally measured precursor of exterior tidal motion.}
On the monitored stationary source an observer can measure the fraction
\(a\) of blank heads and the fraction \(b\) of heads belonging to the
four partially transmitting labels. Let \(c\) count the latter in
coincidence with a single target load. With two full pulses, arbitrary
two-slot words from the three loads, a shared random bit mask and
unambiguous endpoint decoding, the fastest acceptance probability
is \(p=a+(b+c)/4\). The conserved inventory bounds \(c\le b/60\).
Measurements of \(a,b\) alone separate the canonical repair from
its original reference. The expected time on the third written
contact falls from 3.28285 to 3.25072 attempts. A different
preparation at the same repaired modular score and mean ordinary
energies gives an acceptance of 0.26495, below the original 0.30461.
Thus the extra head readings carry information the scalar charge misses.

Calibrate the small-exposure clock \(s\) by setting \(s^2\) equal to
the probability that a sharp ordinary single reaches a blank neighbor.
Release two exterior probes at equal radius from station 219.
Use their squared ranges to the launch stations to project their
relative motion along the initial chord.
At fixed initial chord length the change in its normalized initial
acceleration is a sum of changes in squared neighbor ranges divided
by the squared chord. The cheaper link can only shorten these
ranges. A single strict shortening forces an inward change for both
probe loads. On the 991 registered exterior chords with matching
initial lengths and at least two hops of clearance, 25 have an
inward change and 966 are silent. At the four-hop witness the
independent relay-time readings give the interval
\([-0.016775,-0.016457]\) per squared ordinary clock unit.
This inference uses local clock and communication measurements as
the premises of a finite implication. It does not require a
microscopic gravitational force detector.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/TIDAL_RESULTS.md}{Native source measurements, proof, matched-charge control and galaxy forecasts}.}

The mean inward response on the active registered shells is equivalent
to 8.01--8.02 ordinary single insertions on the same ruler. The scale
dependence of the ordinary response leaves its Newtonian mass
identification open. Giving each independently imaged eligible patch
the measured radial field, one stellar disk scale length per hop and
the ordinary single-to-double energy ratio as a mass conversion
reproduces at most 28 of 369 observed speeds within two quoted
errors across the tested ordinary calibrations and image maps.
No complete curve passes. Even allowing a separate positive mass
normalization for each galaxy fails to recover a whole curve with
this profile and length conversion.

\paragraph{A bound for every physical patch size.}
One can let each imaged source annulus distribute its fixed inventory
over arbitrary positive physical hop lengths. Each mass element acts on
every orbit through the measured, linearly interpolated compact tide.
An annulus is the difference of two disks. At observer radius \(x\)
and source separation \(\rho\), an edge \(R\) has inward angular moment
\(\sqrt{((x+R)^2-\rho^2)(\rho^2-(x-R)^2)}/(x\rho)\) between its
two tangencies, and zero outside. With \(u=\rho/L\), its radial
integral has weight \(u^2K(u)\,du\). Enclosing the edge moments over
all \(L>0\) bounds the acceleration of every mass-preserving mixture.
At 4.94 kpc in DDO~154 the imaged patches supply at most
\(99.08\,(\mathrm{km/s})^2/\mathrm{kpc}\), while the two-error lower
limit exceeds \(384.33\) in the same units.

Of the 700 galaxy, ordinary-calibration and image combinations, 696
violate an individual speed band. In the other four, a positive
weighted sum of the required accelerations exceeds the sum of the
largest attainable weighted responses of the constituent annuli.
Thus none of the ten complete curves follows under the assumed
ordinary mass conversion, compact kernel and imaged annular abundances,
even when the scale distribution varies by source annulus. A separate
experiment sets the length by the distance to the first other enrolled
patch on the radio or baryonic image. One or six native hops across that
distance give at most 33 or 36 of the 369 speeds at the ordinary
calibrations. The atomic-image shape can cover NGC~7793 with an amplitude
inferred from its orbits, at least 43 times the strongest ordinary
calibration. These restrictions concern the astronomical continuation
of the measured finite effect. The reconstruction of its source
abundance or a collective long-range response from consensus records
is an independent physical premise.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/tidal_scales.py}{Continuous-scale calculation},
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/tidal_mixture.py}{joint-radius certificate verifier}
and \href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/code/cosmology/paid_trail_bao/tidal_neighbors.py}{image-derived predictions}.}

\paragraph{Several measured repairs and an ordinary comparison.}
On the 80-owner carrier, prepare eight disjoint four-owner sources and
enrol a 121-contact message circuit. One route to a remote station
takes 65 blank units. The alternative has 52 blank contacts and four
heralded relays. With all repairs active, the four locally inferred
relay times sum to less than 13. Every individual repair stays above
13. Two exterior material probes have initial chord 64, with all
their native incident contacts blank. The shorter cross-range therefore
forces a more inward projected acceleration. Of the four sources on
the route, any three suffice and any two are silent, including when
all four spectators are repaired. All 256 subsets were checked.
With all eight repairs the change is \(-0.00228134\) per squared
blank clock unit. An independent simulated route-time acquisition
gives \([-0.002305,-0.002263]\). With unrestricted routing the same
fully repaired source and the same probe pair give zero change.

A uniformly added ordinary single raises the original contact's
message probability by \(b_0/(4V)\), where \(b_0\) is the observed
partially transmitting share and \(V\) the number of material positions.
The local source measurements fix the ordinary preparation reproducing
the repaired ruler. Here the first inward response occurs at 23 added
singles. Preparing 31 or 32 singles on fresh attempts, with probability
\(60631/88935\) for 32, gives exactly the repaired probabilities and
the same exterior acceleration. For \(m>0\) identical independently
enrolled chains the matched count is
\[
n_{\rm eq}=(38/1155)V-(1780/124509)m+131792/622545 .
\]
On a pool without a return the excess is zero. This is a measured
equivalence of message rulers and initial release records. The volume
term reflects the dilution of the uniformly placed ordinary control.

With unrestricted routing a single source on 320 owners affects
chords at up to 18 blank hops of clearance from its written endpoints
in the registered survey. Using measured profiles of one to eight
sources and matching their native median neighbor distance to the
galaxy image gives at most 45 of 369 speeds within two quoted errors.
A separate continuation grants Newtonian gravity to the predicted
ordinary-equivalent count, allocates it uniformly over each communicating
pool, and infers the occupancy probabilities from the image alone.
Tile, annular and whole-disk pools yield at most 32, 40 and 77
speeds respectively across the spherical and softened-disk tests.
No complete galaxy curve is recovered. For homogeneous 0.8 kpc cubes
at the empirical deuterium baryon density, the 3840-port native pool
gives an extra-to-baryonic ratio at most \(2.40\times10^{-6}\) in the
tested image capacities. Recovering the Planck ratio of about 5.36
requires an independent determination of the communicating volume.
Granting the mean-matched volume yields a further prediction if
independent active cubes contribute a uniform linear gravitating
contrast with smooth remaining matter. The source count equals
\(D\,1_{N>0}+\beta N\) for \(D=(38/1155)V+131792/622545\) and
\(\beta=-1780/124509\). Its variance and the spherical top-hat overlap
then fix the total-matter rms on \(8\,h^{-1}\) Mpc. The least variable
of the fourteen central forecasts gives \(\sigma_8=0.8697\), compared
with \(0.8111\pm0.0060\) for the Planck base model. At twice the quoted
input errors the conservative bounds leave one image setting unresolved.
This calculation assumes the linear contrast and its spatial
independence. A prediction of their evolution requires additional
dynamics.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/COLLECTIVE_RESULTS.md}{Native circuit, ordinary titration and astronomical tests},
\href{https://doi.org/10.3847/1538-4357/aaab53}{primordial deuterium and BBN density},
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.06209}{Planck cosmological parameters}.}

\paragraph{First arrival at fixed ordinary energies.}
In a 240-port exchange experiment a local unit comparison measures the
variance of the signed middle load. A repair moves this variance from
\(3/28\) to \(5/28\) while keeping the two head marginals, four mean
home loads, three compact energies and full returned energy fixed. A
single and a double probe distinguish three response classes on the third
contact. Blank contacts pass both loads, selective contacts can pass the
single, and the other contacts block both. Hold each selective word at
its original frequency. The entropy maximum on the accessible exchange
components increases the blank frequency by \(0.01565555\), with an
absolute enclosure narrower than \(1.5\times10^{-8}\). A positive
rational sampler satisfies every constraint exactly. A Gibbs dual
certificate and Pinsker's inequality bound its total-variation distance
from the ideal maximum by \(2^{-27}\).

The sign also holds between the original and product tables. If \(s\)
is the fraction of this measurable displacement, a rational certificate
gives a blank-frequency increase exceeding \(s/512\) throughout
\(0<s\le1\). Near zero the linear response dominates an explicit
quadratic remainder. The rest of the interval is covered by convex
divergence bounds on 115 closed subintervals. The product table
uniquely maximizes the two-head entropy at fixed head marginals.
Any positive weight on that entropy added to the sharp-word entropy
selects a positive displacement. The original uniform population
maximizes the sharp-word entropy alone. The sign follows for this
family of repair rules with the measured ordinary records fixed.
An unweighted first return through the native write-or-exchange
process preserves the uniform distribution on the 448 enrolled words.
All 262,144 simulated returns gave counts consistent with the
unchanged blank fraction and joint table. This waiting protocol
therefore supplies no positive repair weight.

There is an operational way to generate the modular signal. Two
observers each read one head of a separate native return. A material
observer draws fresh returns until one word matches both reports. If
\(u_j\) is the probability of the report pair and \(t_j\) its joint
frequency on a single ordinary return, a word \(w\) in cell \(j\)
is released with probability \(u_j\pi_w/t_j\). In 262,144 executable
trials every pair obtained a match, using 1,172,168 material returns.
This reproduces the canonical repair and changes three ordinary
energy readings. Merely exchanging the two reports leaves material
probe statistics at their ordinary values.

A peer comparison also admits ordinary-matched preparations. Hold
the individual head marginals, the exchange-component mean home and
energy readings, and the selective-word frequencies at their ordinary
values. A peer randomly re-pairs the right-hand readings. Accept the
original register when its joint tally agrees with that of the peer.
The conditional weight is the native sequence multiplicity times the
hypergeometric probability of the observed tally. This rule selects
no target blank count. At 56 returns the modular variance increases
and the blank change vanishes. At 448 the blank change is negative.
At 896 it is \(+0.01263575\). The 896-return size for native
acquisition was chosen after comparing the finite register sizes.

An efficient conditional sampler assembled 4,096 such 896-word
registers using native first returns. Source observers counted
832,480 blank contacts among 3,670,016 prepared members and 225,370
among 1,048,576 ordinary returns. These counts and the independently
calibrated open-minus-blocked transfer imply 3.59--11.17 fewer
arrivals at station 36 per 10,000 launches from 104, for each load
(at least 99.1\% joint coverage). The local counts discharge the
premise of this finite trajectory implication. The passive joint
incidence of the ordinary guard and peer agreement is only
\(6.71\times10^{-28}\) even with the selective composition supplied.
Under the ordinary-equivalent mass transfer the ten imaged galaxies
give at best 26 of 369 rotation speeds at this rate, or 92 with a
success at every eligible site, within twice the quoted errors.
Neither transfer reproduces a complete curve. At up to 3,840 ports,
the latter transfer gives a cosmic extra-to-baryon ratio at most
\(1.03\times10^{-5}\). Identifying the measured effect with the
gravitational discrepancy requires its exterior geometry and source
abundance to account for the orbital and cosmological records.
See \texttt{peer\_register\_lab.json} and \texttt{peer\_abundance.json}.

For any position-history event let \(O,C\) denote its probabilities on
open and blocked words. The selective contribution cancels between the
two preparations. A measured increase \(\delta a\) in blank frequency
thus gives \(\delta a(O-C)\) for either probe, at every observation time
and for eventual first arrival. From station 104, the probability of
reaching 36 before 25 decreases by \(0.00090747\) for both loads.
Independent source counts and open/blocked receiver measurements bound
the decrease between \(0.0006032\) and \(0.0013057\) at joint coverage
of at least 99.4 percent. The inference uses measurements of the
precursor and of reference transport. At the same modular and energy
readings, other stationary preparations give zero and the opposite sign
when entropy maximization is relaxed. Reopening the three write services
reduces the total-variation difference below \(3.41\times10^{-9}\)
after 4096 sweeps.

For the canonical modular repair without these additional constraints,
the single and double generally have unequal first-arrival changes.
The selective single-load gate reduces to two copies of the bypass
joined by a single edge of conductance \(1/2\). Among all 28,059
sensitive beacon pairs, the ratio of single to double changes ranges
from 0.4567 to 0.6075. A separate unrestricted round-trip ruler gives
a more inward initial mean range at 206 of 208 exterior launches for
one canonical source. At each launch all 240 landmarks see the same
change. The initial change in their range difference is exactly zero.

Applying the energy-matched source to a common material pool yields
42.08 and 673.31 equivalent ordinary singles at 240 and 3840 ports for
one chain. In ten imaged disks, the fixed ordinary conversion covers
at most 94 of 369 measured speeds with one local preparation proposal
or 100 with renewal until acceptance. Neither rule reproduces a full
curve. Even at a mixing volume fitted to the cosmological mean, the
lowest central \(\sigma_8\) under independent cubic pools is 1.1614 or
0.8732 respectively. These results establish common material transport
in the finite experiment. Identifying the physical mixing volume and
the geometry that governs astronomical orbits requires additional
observer records.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/blob/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_shell_flux/product_address/green_response/material_recoil/overlap_source/native_boundary/stock/aperture/LANDMARK_RESULTS.md}{Entropy certificate, all-route release, two-beacon experiments and astronomical comparisons}.}

\paragraph{A locally measured modular excess and its radial prediction.}
The three native exchange contacts partition 448 returned words into 59
communicating components. An ordinary thermal reference that retains
their observed frequencies \(\pi_g=d_g/448\) reads
\(K_{\rm ret}=\beta H+\log(Z_g/\pi_g)\) on component \(g\), where
\(H\) is the ordinary exchange Hamiltonian, \(d_g\) is the component size
and \(Z_g=\operatorname{Tr}_g e^{-\beta H}\). For preparations uniform
within components, fixing the ordinary means, work readings, protected
selective frequencies and individual head marginals leaves an exact
calorimetric identity. The complete modular increment is
\((8/4225)\log(z_2/z_3)\,\Delta\langle V_g\rangle\), where \(z_2,z_3\)
are normalized centered thermal traces of two native spectral classes
and \(V_g\) is the within-component energy variance. The coefficient is
strictly positive at every positive temperature. Observers can read \(V_g\)
as half the mean squared difference of two independent ordinary energies
within the same component. At the ordinary thermometer the measured
variance implies \([-0.001042,-0.000253]\) nats in 4,096 prepared
registers, covering the exact increment \(-0.00061220\). A reference
that thermalizes across components gives exactly zero at the matched heat.

Restore native writing and let an observer report each subsequent
return independently with probability \(p\). Keep the thermometer and
the reference fixed. The component excess after subtracting ordinary heat
satisfies \(q(p)\geq0.01065356p\) nats for every \(0<p\leq1\).
Independent native returns resolve positive excesses at both \(p=1/8\)
and \(1/64\). Thinning changes the sampled age of the prepared records.
Using a tile's ordinary baryonic filling as its reporting probability
predicts the radial source without using the orbital speeds. At the
independent ordinary-double calibration only 26 of 369 SPARC speeds
in ten imaged galaxies lie within twice their quoted errors. No
complete curve agrees. Even granting successful preparation to every
eligible site and arbitrary word-independent observation ages, the
extra-to-baryon ratio is at most \(2.186\times10^{-5}\) on this
inventory. The finite theorem closes the implication from laboratory
records to a modular contribution. The physical identification requires
the regional stress that sources the ordinary metric and a population
that supplies the measured abundance. The counts, finite bounds and radial forecasts
are in \texttt{sector\_calorimetry.json}, \texttt{sector\_retention.json}
and \texttt{sector\_mass.json}.

\paragraph{A local source predicts a boundary contraction.}
An ordinary observer in the executable model can test a geometric
consequence of the source without resolving a microscopic force.
Let \(f\) be the reported frequency of an empty material home at
station 20. Emptiness is constant within each ordinary exchange
component and occurs on 24 of the 448 returned words. Its retained
reference probability is \(3/56\). In the prepared-minus-ordinary
comparison, the finite return kernel gives
\(\Delta f\ge 0.01339400p\) at every reporting probability
\(0<p\le1\). The three local mean contact energies, measured on
copies with the recorded local inputs, identify the full
heat-subtracted component price through fifteen possible triples.
The local regional charge depends on the records admitted by the survey.

From station 13 an observer protects occupied homes by parking and
restoring their loads on adjacent ordinary links during a round trip.
Rank the markers by the minimum acknowledged round-trip time and take
the first six, resolving ties in increasing address order. The recorded
membership and local readings have two possible outcomes. Their
boundaries have ten links when the home is empty and fourteen when it
is occupied. Relative to the equilibrated reference the modular
increment is \(\delta Q_B=c\Delta f\), where ordinary spectroscopy
gives \(0.44435<c<0.44850\) nats. Consequently
\[
  \Delta A=-4\Delta f=-4\delta Q_B/c,\qquad
  \delta S_B=\log(53/3)\Delta f .
\]
At \(p=1/8\) the source predicts \(-0.04559\) links. An independent
register acquisition measured \(-0.04249\) links in 524,288 trials
per arm. At \(p=1/64\), 8,192 fresh registers gave \(-0.02026\)
links with a 99.6\% interval \([-0.03517,-0.00532]\), covering the
prediction \(-0.02146\). Transporting an ordinary double from station 8 to station 20
increases the boundary by four links at fixed marker count. This
ordinary transfer fixes \(\eta_6=\log(53/3)/4\) nats per link and
\(\delta S_B+\eta_6\Delta A=0\) throughout the binary preparation
family. The change has the sign of removing a double from the home.
Both calibration arms have zero ordinary home load inside the
respective six-marker sets.

At eleven and thirteen markers the observed regional entropy rises
while the boundary and its total measured communication rate are fixed.
This excludes a common fixed-volume stationarity law on these surveys.
Under the signed spherical mass transfer, 334 of 369 SPARC speeds
require an excess of the opposite sign at twice their quoted errors.
A separate ordinary material-relay calibration covers all 34 NGC~3521
points at \(p=1/8\) for a whole-disk pool, mapped baryons at capacity
16 and spherical continuation. That choice, selected from the declared
comparison grid, covers 68 of the 369 speeds overall. The finite
result establishes a local geometric response at one volume. A
regional stress with common ordinary normalization and the observed
radial abundance requires further physical input.
See \texttt{sector\_balls.json} and \texttt{sector\_geometry.json}.

\paragraph{Two local modular readings determine the ordinary rest inventory.}
Let \(x=\Delta P(0)\) and \(y=\Delta P(2)\) at home 20. The
six-marker source gives \(q_6=cx\). A second observer distinguishes
all three loads and obtains \(q_h=ax+by\) at the same retained and
exchanged thermal references. The ordinary reference spectra give
\(c\in[0.44435,0.44850]\) and \(a,b\in[0.47134,0.49021]\).
The calibrated single and double carry 48 and 192 native energy
units. The two source measurements therefore imply
\[
 \Delta R=48(-x+3y)=48\,\frac{3c q_h-(b+3a)q_6}{bc}.
\]
The finite return kernel gives \(\Delta R\ge 1.28424p\) for every
positive reporting probability. At \(p=1/8\) the measured modular
readings imply \(0.6924<\Delta R<1.5079\). An independent clean-bond
spectrum and protected round-trip clock measure the same quantity
through \(\Delta R=6\Delta E-\tfrac32\Delta T\). In 2,097,152
simulated returns per arm this gives \(+1.01172\) units, with a
simultaneous 99\% interval \([0.37736,1.64609]\). An ordinary load
delivered from outside an eleven-marker region adds 48 or 192 rest
units inside and lengthens the local echo by two ticks. The overlap
preparation raises the frequencies of both empty and double reports.
Its mean rest inventory rises while its echo shortens.

Summing the gains and losses at the four homes and assigning that
increment to spherical gravity at the ordinary coupling covers 26 of
369 measured speeds in ten imaged galaxies within twice their quoted
errors. A separate bound allows the entire adopted baryon budget
of each galaxy to fund successful disjoint sources. Even if each
orbital radius can enclose the entire excess independently, the
measured preparation lacks the mass required by 330 speeds across
all ten galaxies, for any mixture of positive reporting probabilities.
For any population on the 448 returned records the four-home rest
inventory is at most eight single units, compared with \(37/7\) in
the ordinary reference. The corresponding excess fraction is at most
\(19/37\). Even this bound fails at 197 speeds. Including the full
native energy of an arbitrary supported preparation gives a maximum
excess of \(10919/28416\) of the ordinary rest inventory and fails at
219 speeds. These comparisons use the adopted distances, inclinations
and baryonic mass calibration. A macroscopic gravitational effect
from these records requires a geometric source law beyond the
tested assignment of the packet's excess energy to spherical mass.
The energy measurements and radial budgets are in
\texttt{sector\_home.json} and \texttt{sector\_home\_mass.json}.

\paragraph{A regional modular reading determines a distant clock contribution.}
Let \(X,Y\) indicate occupation at homes 8 and 44 on the same reported
trial. Comparing their joint readings with independent pairings of the
same marginals gives \(C=\langle XY\rangle-\langle X\rangle\langle
Y\rangle\). On the binary pair algebra, a thermal reference with
four-cell odds \(O=P_{11}P_{00}/(P_{10}P_{01})\) gives a
paired-minus-independent modular reading \(-C\log O\).
The retained-minus-exchanged reading therefore changes by
\(q_{\rm conn}=\lambda\Delta C\), where the two independently
calibrated log odds give \(-3.06161<\lambda<-3.02258\) nats.
At reporting probability \(1/8\), 2,097,152 simulated returns per
arm give \(\Delta C=0.002482\), with interval \([0.000810,0.004178]\).
The finite source gives \(0.002652\) and
\(\Delta C\ge0.0034818p\) at every positive reporting probability.
The sharp ordinary reference instead has odds \(67/483\) and gives
a positive paired modular change. The reference fixes the sign of
the modular reading.

In the fixed-duration service experiment a local acknowledgement has
period \(1+o_i+o_j\) in empty-link units, with \(o_i\) the occupancy
of the station. A randomly phased appointment forwards a message at
the completion phase. On each retained preparation the mean commute
between two stations is \(6N\) times its effective resistance \(R\).
Two of the four homes lie in separate blocks joined by articulation
points, while 8 and 44 share a block. Series addition of block
resistances gives the exact ordinary calibration
\(R=R_0+\sum_i A_i o_i+DXY\). Thus the term missed by independently
pairing the records is \(Dq_{\rm conn}/\lambda\). Its mean over all
19,701 exterior pairs is positive, with mean mixed coefficient
\(D=2.49023\times10^{-6}\) to within \(8.51\times10^{-10}\).
Individual baselines have both signs.

An intervention holding all four single-home load histograms, the
three mean native exchange energies and the mean total board energy
fixed changes the joint occupancy by \(1/2\). The predicted
resistance change at stations 82 and 104 is
\(-3.8560\times10^{-5}\). A separate exterior excursion experiment
gives \([-4.074,-3.561]\times10^{-5}\). Five exterior baselines
reconstruct the joint change as \(0.4860\), with simultaneous
99.6\% interval \([0.3577,0.6143]\). At the native \(1/8\)
reporting rate the connected contribution on the averaged clock
is equivalent to \(5.89\times10^{-5}\) ordinary deposits at home 20.

Two observers can also release ordinary probes from rest and compare
their terminal positions on that ruler. On a clean exterior pair,
each probe contributes half the sum of neighbor-minus-launch
distances to the initial acceleration of their mean separation.
Linearity carries the measured covariance into this acceleration.
At stations 48 and 82 the local source interval at \(p=1/8\) implies
an inward contribution of \(6.29\times10^{-9}\) to
\(3.27\times10^{-8}\) in normalized commute units per native time squared.
The finite kernel forces the same sign at this pair for every \(p>0\).
On the square root of the mean commute, the matched intervention
also predicts a more inward change at stations 50 and 82.
Independent trajectory samples for both ordinary probe loads
resolve it a quarter tick after release.

On the commute ruler the acceleration change equals half the sum of
the changes in the incident edge resistances at both launches.
This follows from harmonicity of the Green difference at unmodified
launches. Every positive ordinary deposit away from those contacts
increases or preserves each incident resistance. The inward change
therefore distinguishes the joint source from that ordinary calibration.
The comparison concerns what observers infer with this specific ruler.
A single and a double have equal responses on this clock despite
their different rest energies. At either conversion the spherical
mass assigned to the connected signal covers 26 of 369 imaged SPARC
speeds within twice their quoted errors. At 334 speeds the required spherical mass exceeds the
entire budget for disjoint sources funded by the galaxy's adopted
baryons, for every radial placement.
This closes the inference from a measured connected modular
contribution to exterior clocks and to a change in the measured
relative acceleration. Its identification with a common
attractive metric of galactic strength requires a further physical
implication. See \texttt{sector\_passage.json},
\texttt{sector\_passage\_tide.json} and
\texttt{sector\_passage\_sky.json}.

\paragraph{Correlations between distinct reports and their gravitational price.}
Two prescribed routes from station 72 to 97 take twelve
acknowledgement periods on the empty chart. Their respective times
are \(12+2X\) and \(12+2Y\), where \(X,Y\) are the reported
occupancies at homes 8 and 44. Native exchanges verify both readings
on all 448 eligible words for either ordinary probe load.
Summing each clock over \(N=896\) distinct members gives the two
occupancy totals \(S_X,S_Y\) from exterior records alone.
At reporting probability \(p=1/8\), the simulated peer and ordinary
samples give an increase in \(\operatorname{Cov}(S_X,S_Y)/N\)
of \(0.02681\), with estimated sampling standard error \(0.00466\).
The finite return calculation gives \(0.0279454\) within
\(0.0000055\), or 10.54 times the single-member gain.

For type counts \(V\) in the accepted register, conditional single
probabilities \(h_x,h_y\) and joint probability \(h_j\),
the two contributions to the covariance are
\(\sum_c E[V_c](h_{j,c}-h_{x,c}h_{y,c})\) and
\(h_x^\top\operatorname{Cov}(V)h_y\). Integer multiplicities for
31,603 families determine the count covariance. Each member reports
on its own return clock. A finite return-age enclosure gives an increase
of at least \(0.02884p\) per member for every \(p>0\), including at
least \(0.02705p^2\) from comparisons between distinct members.
The ordinary covariance per member is \(-13/392\). The increase
reduces the magnitude of the anticorrelation at \(1/8\).

Grant the collective reading the same ordinary mass price per unit
of covariance as the mean exterior commute. Assign to each imaged
tile its measured baryonic filling as the reporting probability.
These forward profiles cover 26 of 369 orbital speeds within twice
their quoted errors and zero of ten complete curves. Even granting
every baryon to disjoint successful preparations, with arbitrary
radial placement, the upper extra-to-baryon ratio is \(0.000634\)
over all reporting rates and all subgroups of this register.
The sparse limit explains the direction of the discrepancy. For
a tile of filling \(0<p\leq2^{-24}\), the collective covariance gain
per member is between \(0.04912p\) and \(0.04952p\). The enrolled
four-home source contributes an additional factor
\(6p^2(1-p)^2(448/2110)\) per tile, whose ordinary mass is proportional
to \(p\). The mapped extra-to-ordinary mass ratio is thus between
\(0.0003476p^2\) and \(0.0003507p^2\). In this test, sparse observation
means a low independent probability of reporting an eligible return,
set by the mapped baryonic filling.
At 291.07 kpc four isolated weak-lensing stacks require ratios of
5.41--8.07 under the spherical deprojection and doubled baryon
allowance used below. Even arbitrary dependence among 896 members
permits less than 3.77 at the same price, since
\(\operatorname{Cov}(S_X/N,S_Y/N)\leq1/4\).
The timing enhancement follows from the joint records. Assigning it
gravitating stress is an additional hypothesis with a measurable
shortfall at this price. See \texttt{sector\_fronts\_collective.json}
and \texttt{sector\_fronts\_lensing.json}.

\paragraph{Measuring the source at fixed survey volume.}
Inside a 197-marker region, observers time ordinary protected pulses on
four home links and add the readings across two members. A balanced
re-pairing preserves the complete one-member histograms. In one arm
the four-clock tuples are \((2,4,4,2)\) and \((3,4,4,3)\).
In the other they are \((3,4,4,2)\) and \((2,4,4,3)\).
The second arm increases the mean log ratio of exchanging to retained
thermal reference probabilities by \(0.02250\)--\(0.05111\) nats.
Independently, at reporting rate \(1/8\), two distinct members of the
native peer register yield a calculated \(0.0001036\)--\(0.0001254\)
nat excess over two independent members with the same marginals.
These are simulated records and finite implications about their
measured modular prices. All 36 boundary links of this region return
one acknowledgement period on every preparation. A monotone bound on
the shortest times certifies the same 197 members for any number of
reports. Thus this fixed-volume boundary instrument cannot balance
the observed source. A microscopic force measurement is unnecessary
for a source-to-gravity proof. The remaining premise is the physical
identification of the measured charge with stress on a common geometry.

The same chart distinguishes propagation through the interior.
Launch uniformly inside and count first exits by 256 ticks of the
ordinary slot walk with link acceptance equal to inverse mean period.
From a common baseline, either of two mixtures raises the four-home
modular charge by exactly \(0.00005\) nats at fixed one-member
histograms. A fractional switch between the controlled arms raises
the exit probability. A fractional switch to distinct native members
lowers it. The killed transition matrix for exterior launches is
identical in both arms, so all exterior first-entry records have the
same law. The equality of charges is a finite consequence of mixture
linearity at the calibrated prices. See
\texttt{sector\_register\_escape.json}.

At the thermal conversion \(\Delta E=\Delta Q/\beta\), pairwise charges
predicted from the mapped baryon filling cover 26 of 369 orbital speeds
within two quoted errors and zero of ten complete curves. There is
also a size-independent mass bound. In each native sector \(j\), the
two thermal references have the same conditional operator and sector
weight ratio \(r_j\). The probability ratio of any common outcome on
\(N\) product-reference members is a positive weighted mean of the
products \(\prod_i r_{j_i}\). Therefore any change in mean log ratio
is bounded by \(N(\max_j\log r_j-\min_j\log r_j)\) in absolute value.
This holds for joint instruments and arbitrarily correlated source
preparations. The calibrated span is below \(8.0001\) nats and the
ordinary rest inventory exceeds \(191.879N\) native energy units.
At \(\beta=\log(8/7)\) the extra-to-ordinary mass ratio is below
\(0.313\) for any register size, or \(0.625\) for a difference of two
connected comparisons. All four outer lens stacks exceed even the
latter allowance. The bounds use disjoint paid inventories and the
specified product thermal references. See
\texttt{sector\_register\_balls.json},
\texttt{sector\_register\_source.json} and
\texttt{sector\_register\_sky.json}.

\paragraph{A measured slowdown and its predicted radial distribution.}
Four local ordinary-pulse links read two distinct members from one
896-member peer register. At reporting probability \(1/8\), 4,096
simulated registers yield a relative thermal modular excess of
\(0.0001192\pm0.0000066\) nats per pair, with one sampling standard
error at nominal calibration. A separate ordinary control is consistent
with zero. At home 8, the frequency of two occupied reports falls by
\(139.7\pm0.9\) parts per million at fixed single-member law.
The protected mean link period gives departure probabilities \(1,2/3,1/2\)
for zero, one or two occupied reports. At fixed marginals, a sixth
of the change in the two-occupied frequency is exactly the change
in departure probability. The four-home mean slows by
\(1.21160\times10^{-5}\) of its blank frequency at \(1/8\). The
finite return bound gives a strictly negative change for every
positive reporting probability, proportional to \(p^2\) in the
sparse limit. Measuring the coincidence closes the premise for this
clock slowdown in the executable model. The inclusive visit counter
at the same station has constant rate. Their changing ratio therefore
cannot be accounted for by a common change of proper time alone.

To test its gravitational interpretation, assign the observed tile
filling to \(p\), use the enrolled pair availability, pool the
departure counts in each annulus and identify the log frequency ratio
with the extra static lapse. From the measured circular speeds,
a nonnegative spherical extra mass satisfies
\(\Delta\log N\ge GM_{\min}(<r_i)(1/r_i-1/r_{i+1})/c^2\)
on each annulus. In DDO~154 the clock rises by about
\(2.15\times10^{-12}\) from 1.48 to 5.92 kpc. The orbits require at
least \(1.79\times10^{-8}\) under the adopted baryon model.
Across all 14 tracer and capacity settings each of the ten imaged
galaxies misses an orbital interval, even with a free positive gain
per galaxy. Seven have globally consistent spherical speed boxes at
the two-error allowance. In the broader bulgeless sample, changing the
stellar, gas, distance and inclination assumptions and allowing 4.33
quoted speed errors leaves 74 of 114 galaxies with a strictly positive
extra integrated frequency gradient. These are frequency requirements
inferred from orbits. See \texttt{sector\_return\_redshift.json} and
\texttt{sector\_return\_lapse.json}.
