\paragraph{What fixes the ordinary calibration?}
On the stationary 80-cell tetrahedral survey the two ordinary
radar probes each resolve a smaller boundary area at fixed
survey volume under an increase of parked exchange energy.
Independent energy and entropy readings with the equilibrium
area coefficient predict about \(-0.05956\) squared range units
per unit ordinary energy. On a separate 240-marker carrier, a
single or double with standing energy 48 or 192 crosses a recorded
boundary link and comes to rest inside the original eleven-marker
region. Its six boundary links recover their original timing even
when the entropy of the local arrival record increases. With both
endpoints occupied, an exchange across a complete twelve-marker
shell transfers 120 native energy units inward including interactions.
The regional bond calorimeter resolves that gain within 0.046 units
while all 627 protected link periods stay fixed. These experiments
establish a conditional geometric response and an independently
measurable transported energy. The Einstein and
dark-matter claims require ordinary stress and the proposed overlap
correction on one physical family of observer regions. See
\texttt{ordinary\_stationarity.json} and
\texttt{ordinary\_mass\_crossing.json}.

\paragraph{A finite source experiment on the ordinary-calibrated survey.}
Observers vary the correlations of the two heads while keeping each
parity-resolved marginal and the parked ordinary energy fixed. With
\(P\) the independent pairing of the reference marginals, the entropy
gain equals the decrease in relative entropy to \(P\). On a finite
held preparation the observers measure a connected modular charge
increase of \(7.0204\)--\(7.1043\) micro-nats and a net entropy gain
of \(5.2603\)--\(5.7010\) micro-nats. A mixture of independent pairing
and a second correlation write brackets the reference volume. At an
exact match the ordinary-calibrated finite equilibrium bound forces a
decrease of at least \(8.15\times10^{-8}\) squared range units in the
four-station projected area. Radar counts independently enclose the
decrease in \([1.6768,4.4257]\times10^{-7}\). The experiment
establishes a finite response to the joint records on its stated
geometry. Identifying their charge with the physical collar stress in
Eq.~\eqref{eq:tanom} requires an independently calibrated bulk boost
and the same physical small-ball family. See
\texttt{finite\_pairing\_dose.json}.

\paragraph{Can a local observer identify the source from material transfer?}
A blinded decoder receives the transfer counted on one internal link
of the same preparation. It knows the three possible input stocks.
The source probabilities, held head counts and radar area are
withheld. Positivity of the probabilities and affinity of the
transfer bound every mixture compatible with the measurement.
Entropy concavity then gives a gain of at least
\(1.8089\times10^{-7}\) nats. With both head marginals fixed the
connected modular increment is at least this large. The ordinary
equilibrium inequality, including the measured uncertainty in volume,
forces an area contraction of at least \(6.5922\times10^{-9}\)
squared range units. Independent radar acquisition gives a contraction
between \(3.0410\times10^{-7}\) and \(3.0660\times10^{-7}\).
This closes the local source premise on the calibrated three-stock
family. See \texttt{link\_source\_contraction.json}.

\paragraph{Does observing the source less often produce an attractive contribution?}
For six senders, two probe signs and eleven flight times, the terminal
position histograms identify all 31 varying coordinates of the
parity-resolved source. The observer knows the native flight calibration
and receives neither a preparation recipe nor the held radar. On the
paired write the inferred connected charge rises by \(4.6164\) to
\(9.5061\) micro-nats. The inferred entropy and volume force an area
decrease of at least \(2.3446\times10^{-7}\) squared range units at the
ordinary equilibrium. Independent radar reads a decrease in
\([3.0410,3.0660]\times10^{-7}\). This measures a local precursor
and checks its finite geometric consequence on the same chart.
Writing \(P_X\) for the parity-conditioned product of the observed
single-head marginals, the exact balance is
\(\Delta S=\Delta Q_X-\mathrm{Tr}[(Y-X)\log P_X]-D(Y\Vert X)\).
The middle term vanishes on the paired write. Independent head counts
of the charge and position counts of the entropy put the finite
relative-entropy cost in \([1.5213,1.6443]\) micro-nats.

We next physically omitted the midpoint position interrogation on the
same native flight. At half-windows of one, two and four half-ticks,
respectively, the four-anchor area expanded, contracted and expanded.
Keeping the interrogation and subsequently erasing its report gave the
dense-arm response. Interference between histories reaching and missing
the detector lets the source observer predict all nine held area signs
across three pointer overlaps and three flight times, including the
volume contribution, before any held clock is read. See
\texttt{position\_source.json} and \texttt{source\_interference.json}.

When the same memory survives repeated flights, all four tested
schedules yield a persistent positive sparse-minus-dense connected
charge at the dense stationary reference, between \(0.0006345\) and
\(0.02931\) nats. At the independently fixed ordinary reference the
charge difference is negative on every schedule. All four sparse
endpoints expand the ordinary-calibrated survey, including after a
one-in-256 pulse into its equilibrium. At each of the eight dynamical
endpoints an accessible preparation increases entropy with twelve
ranges and two marked-force means fixed. Thus these equilibria of
the servicing dynamics fail the fixed-volume entropy premise on this
survey. A gravitating collar source requires a physical regional weight
and ordinary-matter calibration. See
\texttt{persistent\_density.json} and \texttt{stationary\_source\_test.json}.

\paragraph{What does the freely released material measure on the contracting flight?}
Each probe measures its change in momentum over the full four-tick
flight and the change on a matched positive ordinary write. On the
contracting two-half-tick window the negative probe infers an ordinary
energy equivalent in \([5.81,36.30]\times10^{-6}\) native energy units
and the positive probe in \([-14.15,-9.56]\times10^{-6}\).
The independent classical preparation coin permits separate sampling of
the active one-in-256 copies. The common inactive contribution cancels
exactly from the difference of material expectations. The sign repeat
used \(2^{76}\) simulated starts per tagged setting.

The ordinary source can be assigned in nonnegative amounts to any of
twelve native sender and flight-sign histories. Allow a further unknown
momentum increment that reverses with probe charge. Averaging the two
raw probe readings cancels that increment. All twelve ordinary
charge-averaged impulses exceed \(5.6493\times10^{-10}\), whereas
the measured sparse average lies below \(-6.8106\times10^{-12}\) in
the same orientation. No positive allocation in this family can
produce the material response. The independently sensed overlap source
predicts these impulse intervals as well as the geometric contraction.
A gravitational interpretation on larger proper regions requires a
physical stress with the ordinary coupling there. See
\texttt{density\_material\_impulse.json} and
\texttt{charge\_even\_material.json}.

\paragraph{Can servicing alone maintain the reference used in the area proof?}
For six senders, two launch signs and twelve half-windows, a signed
Pauli reading increases by at least \(8.4109\times10^{-9}\) per
flight at the independently localized ordinary equilibrium. Four
pointer-phase calibrations, a measured Heisenberg drift and an
integer propagation enclosure cover the full unit disk of pointer
overlaps. Every state-independent probability mixture of those
settings inherits the sign. When a controller uses the actual midpoint
click or miss to choose one of thirteen remaining flight durations, a
separate reading increases by at least \(5.5368\times10^{-7}\) for
all 24,336 rules that serve both parity sectors alike. Allowing a
different rule in each incoming parity sector remains unresolved in
that comparison. Thus maintenance of the equilibrium needed for the
area implication is a physical premise on this preparation. See
\texttt{certified\_pointer\_flight.json} and
\texttt{equilibrium\_feedback.json}.

\paragraph{Can a measured overlap charge imitate ordinary matter on six material probes?}
Two probe signs each give a contact force, an instantaneous momentum rate
and a four-tick impulse. On an equal mixture of three flight times the
six real pointer controls can reproduce all six ordinary changes at
\(1/8192\) of the calibrated ordinary write. Interval inversion of the
measured response matrix proves that the controls lie in the physical
pointer disks. The corresponding ordinary-energy equivalent is
\([1.4188856,1.4188976]\,10^{-7}\) in native units. The inferred
connected charge lies in \([0.95649,1.08253]\,10^{-7}\) nats.
Fresh joint readings at the programmed central settings give
\([1.01930,1.02009]\,10^{-7}\) nats. The material responses set the
controls, so their agreement is a calibration.

Independent observers timed every return and timeout on thirty routes.
They tagged the ordinary and overlap preparations before probing and
recombined the route means at the calibrated dose before reconstructing
area. The overlap contraction at the programmed settings is
\(37.85\)--\(38.72\) percent of the equal ordinary contraction.
Fourteen log edge ratios remove the six sender clock gains and the
overall length scale. On this chart their joint equality is excluded
for every physical pointer at each of the three times, every common
time mixture and every positive fraction up to the full ordinary
write. The measured charge thus has a finite geometric consequence.
Identifying it with the source in Eq.~\eqref{eq:tanom} requires
predicting the regional shape and motion from the same stress. See
\texttt{six\_meter\_pointer.json}, \texttt{six\_meter\_clocks.json}
and \texttt{pointer\_shape.json}.

The same comparison distinguishes two possible radius dependences
in Eq.~\eqref{eq:tanom}. On a fixed piecewise-flat filling the
clock-measured change of solid angle gives
\(dA|_V=r^2\,d\Omega/3\) at a vertex. All thirty-six angular
derivatives exclude zero. Dividing this uncompensated geometric
response by \(r^4\) grows as \(1/r^2\). Identifying the measured
area response with a finite smooth anomalous density at a constant
conversion therefore requires additional regional records or a
physical refinement. See \texttt{conical\_ball\_radii.json}.

\paragraph{Can a distributed ordinary source explain the clock change?}
Address-resolved head counts give positive total connected charge and a
negative local contribution at station 38. All 36 ordinary sender, probe
sign and time histories deposit positive excess energy at that station.
The ordinary conversion of total modular increment to excess energy
predicts a positive slope of \(3.27525\)--\(3.49849\) in units of
\(10^{-9}\) for the log edge ratio \((1,2)/(0,1)\), allowing every
nonnegative distribution of the inferred connected source among these
histories. Independent clocks measure a negative slope between
\(-3.38619\) and \(-3.34606\) in the same units.

Ordinary preparations can also agree on energy, connected and marginal
modular scores at each of seven receiver outcomes while their spatial
responses differ. Interval minors on the equal-source directions require
at least six more independent scalar readings across the survey for a
differentiable law to recover both clocks and material responses. On
the programmed comparison the full two-head readings predict all
fourteen measured clock-ratio slopes. Including the net energy inflow
at every receiver leaves at least four independent joint
responses on directions that preserve all these readings. See
\texttt{regional\_transport.json}. These independent joint responses
specify which shared records a stress assignment in Eq.~\eqref{eq:tanom}
must account for. See
\texttt{distributed\_source.json}, \texttt{regional\_rank.json} and
\texttt{matched\_regional\_flight.json}.

\paragraph{What survives when the observations do zero flight work?}
A less distinguishing observation can supply energy to the flight.
Observers read both quadratures of the hopping terms at the switch.
An independent short flight against a bypass reads the total energy
after propagation. The two instruments agree on all 72 tested
history--intervention pairs. Bare omission supplies a positive mean
energy at each duration. From the work quadratures one can instead
set a pointer phase giving exactly zero mean work at that duration.
At three such settings the measured full modular increments and the
ordinary stationary entropy balance force a smaller four-station
boundary area. Independent radar resolves all three decreases.
The orientation was selected using the connected modular reading.

There is a stronger equal-volume comparison. Mix the duration-two and
duration-four ensembles equally. Reverse the first phase and adjust the
amplitude of the second. The independently reconstructed volume changes
with opposite signs at the two ends of the measured amplitude bracket
\(0.20090306\)--\(0.20096022\). By continuity a setting in this bracket
has exactly the baseline volume and zero mean work. Its connected
increment lies in \([2.3450,2.3553]\,10^{-6}\) nats. The full increment
exceeds \(1.1385\,10^{-6}\) nats throughout the bracket, and the finite
stationarity error is below \(9.374\,10^{-9}\) nats. With the ordinary
gain this bounds the area change by \([-5.127,-4.887]\,10^{-8}\)
squared range units. Fresh clock records at the programmed center give
\([-8.825,-1.054]\,10^{-8}\) and enclose zero volume change. The two
material probes give disjoint ordinary-energy equivalents on the same
input. See \texttt{fixed\_volume\_flight.json} and
\texttt{zero\_flight\_work.json}.

Equation~\eqref{eq:tanom} asks a different question of the local
receiver. At one half range unit, the full cap entropy derivative
less the centered boost of its measured flight energy is positive at
all six receivers in the bypass convention. When the interior retains
one central receiver as radius \(r\) shrinks, this remainder has
the form \(q+c r\). The measured intercept is positive at four
stations and negative at two, each resolved away from zero. Thus
\((q+c r)/r^4\) cannot have a finite limit on this fixed chart at
any of the six stations for any fixed finite \(c\). A distributed
source in a refining family of proper balls would need its own
measurements. A finite test uses \(F(r)=rE(r)\). Each positive shell
contributes a nonnegative multiple of \((r^2-s^2)_+\) to \(F\), so its
secants in \(r^2\) are nondecreasing. At radii \(1/8,1/4,1/2\) the
receiver gives \(F(1/2)-5F(1/4)+4F(1/8)=-q/4\). Four receivers fail
the positive-shell condition independently of the centered boost.
See \texttt{cap\_stress\_radii.json}.

A contrasting measurement is available on the protected refining screen.
At the original corner, enroll every station within the clock radius
and read the two word registers, including all misses. On four
successive tested resolutions, closed-ball word-count experiments verify
the forecast from the number of eligible distinct pairs and the modular
price at the reversed-tag reference. On the mesh of whole clock hops
the radial secants admit an explicit nonnegative shell distribution on
every board. In the shrinking, many-hop limit the closed-ball entropy
increment per fourth power of relative radius tends to
\((81/16)\kappa=0.0167322\) nats, where \(\kappa\) is the conditional
word entropy derivative. Substituting this coefficient into
Eq.~\eqref{eq:tanom} gives \(0.00317874\) in the normalized routed
units. The protected journeys avoid the word slots, so their return
times are exactly independent of the intervention. Identifying this
coefficient with a stress requires one geometry for the clock and
material motion. An independent instrument reads the same source.
Local phase standards interrogate the two recorded seam energies in
each word. At all four resolutions \(q f/\kappa\), formed from the
independent modular reading and conditional fringe response \(f\),
predicts the sign and size of the observed paired fringe. Equal
exposures have zero contrast. Doubling the second exposure resolves
both quadratures, but they imply disjoint changes of elapsed time.
See \texttt{resolved\_shells.json} and \texttt{word\_phase\_read.json}.

\paragraph{What does a material survey of the recorded source actually see?}
Encode each ordered energy word in a distinguishable matching of six
written contacts in its cell. Every port retains the same number of
blank contacts. This choice preserves the regional word spectra and lets
both mobile loads encounter the records. Their calibrated single-swap
maps give the same blank graph. Each freely released load propagates
with one half its graph Laplacian up to a constant. On the twenty-cell
screen the measured modular increment is
\(0.00010435\)--\(0.00010440\) nats. Independent sharp routing standards
and this reading predict a decrease of \(1.2183\,10^{-5}\) swap
appointments in one of the 190 mean station ranges. The other 189 have
zero population contrast. After four native time units, more probes
released from cell six enter the source, yet their mean squared radial
distance also increases. Reversing which contacts encode the second
word reverses both measured signs at the same modular price.

There is a sign that follows from the shared writer tag. Each station
times round trips to its eleven other ports and the observers sum their
durations over the enrolled ball. Let \(d\) be the signed one-word
frequency direction and \(f\) the nine sharp clock costs in swap
appointments, normalized so that the conditional pair contrast is
\(d d^{\mathsf T}/1296\). The one-word marginals agree. The change in
variance of the total duration is therefore
\(2P(d^{\mathsf T} f)^2/1296=P/162\), where \(P\) is the probability
of admitting both words under the same code. The mean is unchanged.
The independently measured charge \(q=P\kappa\) predicts this timing
effect at all four resolutions. The reversed code in the second slot
gives the opposite change. Arm a separate deadline after the common
baseline, surviving each two extra appointments with probability \(s\).
The sharp standards give the survival contrast
\(P[s(1-s)]^2/1296>0\) for every \(0<s<1\) under the same encoding.
Thus these observers infer a shorter elapsed duration. On the 80-station
screen the half-survival and quarter-survival clocks resolve shortenings
of \(2.124\)--\(2.150\) and \(0.615\)--\(0.632\) millionths of an
appointment. The disagreement persists on every positive aperture.
Writing \(u,v\) for the fresh half- and quarter-survival fractions and
\(x=P/20736\), one has \(u\le1\) and \(v\ge3025/9216>9/32\).
Their survival ratios obey
\(1+9x/(16v)<(1+x/u)^2\). A common time displacement would give
equality. As the admitted fraction tends to zero, the ratio of
inferred shortenings tends to \(32/9\). Removing the survival change
predicted from the independent modular reading leaves a common additive
delay within \(9.3\,10^{-9}\) appointments of zero on the 80-station
quarter-survival meter. Adding a known two-appointment center round
trip recovers two to within \(2.8\,10^{-8}\) on that meter.
See \texttt{word\_routes.json} and
\texttt{regional\_travel\_times.json}.

A common positive rest-frame density entails a further comparison.
Average the measured moment \(F=rE\) over all centers. For any positive
measure on the clock stations the ratio at \(2r,r\) cannot exceed the
largest ratio for a unit source at a single station, since the
contributions add with positive weights. The homogeneous two-word
preparation exceeds this bound on all four screens. On the finest
screen the count interval is \(24.45\)--\(32.57\). The maximum is
\(3076/193\) for station masses and is at most \(214408/10587\) even
for mass within half a hop of a station. These tests concern the flat
rest-ball kernel on the measured distances. Independently fitting
shells at each center loses this consistency condition.

A counted ordinary unit at an inside contact supplies a separate force
control. It gives a positive initial change of the phase-sensitive
inward occupation acceleration for both probes. The correlated source
gives exactly zero initial change for every diagonal position score
of either single probe on this routing preparation. Its two distinct
word contacts act in disjoint cells, so their mixed term in the
initial acceleration vanishes. The finite-time changes above survive.
These records identify how a regional overlap can affect two freely
moving loads. Establishing an anomalous stress in
Eq.~\eqref{eq:tanom} requires an ordinary-calibrated gravitational
response on the reconstructed macroscopic geometry and a source
distribution determined independently of galactic motions.
See \texttt{overlapping\_shells.json} and
\texttt{initial\_acceleration.json}.

\paragraph{A common three-dimensional source inferred from pair records.}
Let \(a,b\) be two endpoint directions in the independently calibrated
round angular chart with screen radius one. Their inferred midpoint
\(m=(a+b)/2\) satisfies \(|m|^2=1-|a-b|^2/4\) for each recorded pair.
For independent isotropic endpoints this gives \(P(|m|\le r)=r^2\) and
the common normalized density \(1/(2\pi r)\) within the screen.
A boundary cap of chord radius \(R\) admits both words with probability
\(R^4/16\). For every boundary center \(x\) and \(0<R\le1\), direct
angular integration gives
\[
 \mathbb E[(1-|m-x|^2/R^2)_+]
 =\frac{1}{4R^2}\int_0^R(R^2-u^2)^2\,du=\frac{2R^3}{15}.
\]
Thus the positive density \(15\kappa/(64\pi^2 r)\) has precisely the
measured-limit endpoint charge \(\kappa R^4/16\) through the rest-ball
kernel \(\pi(R^2-d^2)_+/R\). For a radial density, differentiating its
boundary moment twice with respect to \(R^2\) fixes the integral of
\((1-u)\rho(1-u)\) up to depth \(R\). A fifth-power moment therefore
fixes the \(1/r\) radial form almost everywhere in the open ball.
An atom at the origin is invisible to these short boundary apertures.
At \(R=5/4,3/2\) the two additional central masses required by the
endpoint observations are incompatible.

This construction distinguishes a boundary cap read from a bulk read
of the joint endpoints. Independent separation counts on the
5,120-station angular mesh put the fraction of midpoints inside radius
\(1/2\) between \(0.2422\) and \(0.2584\). The direct finite fraction
is \(0.24981\). At nine sub-unit boundary settings, area-weighted
endpoint and converted midpoint charges differ by at most \(2.45\%\)
on this mesh. Uniform station counting gives differences up to
\(9.94\%\). Angular refinement controls the area-weighted error.
The angular chart and the physical association of a pair to its
midpoint are explicit hypotheses. See \texttt{midpoint\_bulk.json}.

Three smaller regions distinguish the raw endpoint charge from any
common positive rest source on this chart. At centers \(n,n/2,0\)
take radii \(B=\sqrt{11}/4,\ R=3/4,\ S=\sqrt{15}/4\), respectively.
For any source location \(x\) the quantity \(2(R^2-|x-n/2|^2)\)
equals \((B^2-|x-n|^2)+(S^2-|x|^2)\). Taking positive parts
and integrating gives \(2R Q(n/2,R)-B Q(n,B)-S Q(0,S)\le0\).
This includes exterior matter, anisotropic matter and atoms.
The measured endpoint charge violates the bound on all four meshes
and both writers. At 5,120 area-weighted stations the residual is
\([3.9667,3.9762]\,10^{-5}\) in unit-screen coordinates. The
midpoint-weighted record passes the same comparison on all meshes.
It implements one consistent positive-source instrument. Its physical
interpretation requires a comparison with entropy stationarity. See
\texttt{three\_regions.json}. If an extra term supplements a positive
rest source to recover all three raw readings, at least one such
term must exceed \(1.2029\,10^{-5}\) nats in magnitude on the finest
area-weighted screen. If the midpoint integral supplies the entire
fixed-volume entropy source, the predicted \(\eta\,dA|_V\) at half
depth is \([-2.337,-2.336]\,10^{-4}\) nats, with \(\eta>0\)
to be calibrated on ordinary matter in the same reconstruction.
The edge of the screen also tests signed stress.
The central raw charge changes by \([0.00330501,0.00330527]\) nats
between radii \(1\pm1/4096\). The rest-ball kernel is
\(2\pi\)-Lipschitz in radius at every mass element, so this jump needs
at least 2,184 times the total midpoint mass in positive and negative
magnitudes combined. The exact thin-screen address rule retains its
jump as the interval shrinks. A locally finite signed stress cannot
supply that full family. A finite spread of physical source radii would
limit the conclusion to the resolved width.

On the same twenty-cell preparation a native ordinary load of measured
energy \(42\) can stay in the home cell indefinitely with every
intercell contact open and the absence matching written. Its gateway amplitudes vanish and the full generator
preserves its measured one-cell support. The sharp overlap source is
also stationary before release. On a selected exterior crossing, a
positive localized midpoint mass would give a potential rise of
\(0.28686\)--\(0.28747\) per unit \(GM\) from the measured pair
weights. Adding that potential to the probe generator must change
the phase-sensitive initial occupation acceleration. The actual
native increment is exactly zero for either probe, even after
arbitrarily long source preparation. A neighboring ordinary unit
gives resolved increments of \(8.5\) and \(16.25\) on this receiver.
This tests the scalar force identification on the finite chart. See
\texttt{settled\_midpoint\_force.json}.

An ordinary exterior observer can also count ticks of one load in a
superposition of localized energy levels 42 and 43. Its port occupations
oscillate at the level difference. On the same two exterior stations
the gap is exactly unchanged in all 486 sharp configurations of the
localized pair source and with the compact ordinary load present.
Hence these material clocks register zero source redshift at any delay.
Independent port counts through time 64 put the shared-minus-fresh
change of the far-minus-near frequency within
\([-2.177,2.223]\,10^{-4}\) in native frequency units. Writing a word
at the clock itself changes a measured port probability by about
\(0.481\). A universal lapse in this chart would have changed the
frequency difference. See \texttt{midpoint\_clocks.json}.

For a spherical screen of size \(L\) the inferred enclosed fraction
is \(\min(r^2/L^2,1)\). If it gravitates as rest mass, the extra
acceleration is constant inside and Keplerian outside. On 1,288
outer SPARC rings in 136 galaxies, 109 admit an interior-only profile
and 117 admit one freely placed outer edge under the stated nuisance
and doubled speed-error boxes. A positive mixture of any number
of concentric screens gives enclosed mass nondecreasing and concave
in \(r^2\). On a finite mesh every such curve is a positive sum of
the cut-off profiles, allowing zero weights. Even this larger family
fails on three rings each in NGC 5907, UGC 00128 and UGC 09133. The
additional uniform speed-error half-width needed in UGC 09133 lies
between \(2.390\) and \(2.407\) km/s. On the four isolated lensing
stacks out to \(291.07\) kpc a single freely normalized edge fits
three under doubled sums of the statistical and systematic errors.
Mixtures admit all four effective stack profiles. The shape tests
leave normalization and screen sizes free for each galaxy or stack.
See \texttt{midpoint\_halo.json}.

Direct shear gives a test independent of the spherical deprojection.
For density \(A/r\) inside \(L\), the thin-lens excess surface density
is \(S(R)=2A/(1+\sqrt{1-R^2/L^2})\) for \(R<L\) and
\(2AL^2/R^2\) outside. Consequently \(g_{\rm extra}/2\le\pi G
S_{\rm extra}\le g_{\rm extra}\) for any positive concentric mixture.
For two radii with \(s=(R_{\rm near}/R_{\rm far})^2\), the same
family requires \(s S_{\rm near}\le S_{\rm far}\le
(1+\sqrt{1-s})S_{\rm near}\). The bounds are attained with an edge
inside the near radius and at the far radius respectively. In the
six enrolled isolation bins of Brouwer et al., all fifteen
pairs permit these inequalities in each of KiDS-bright and GAMA.
Using their released covariances, the largest signed departures at
bin centres are \(-1.901\) and \(+0.161\) standard errors. These
are shape tests of a common population with compact ordinary baryons,
free of any fitted screen parameters. See \texttt{midpoint\_shear.json}.

\paragraph{Measuring the full overlap source with ordinary material.}
On the twenty-station coded preparation each source observer sums the energies
of the two stored seam labels, starts a material tag at port 3 and opens
only contact $(3,6)$ for one swap period. Arrival or retention together
with the energy resolves every degeneracy of the nine-word energy spectrum. The two local
readings therefore give a bijection of all 81 joint records. Applying the
reference cost to independent shared and fresh counts gives
$0.00329828$--$0.00331199$ nats per unit intervention at simultaneous 99\%
coverage for the two word orders. Independent counts also resolve a finite
entropy increase of $0.00159369$--$0.00162974$ nats. The code is calibrated
on sharp preparations. The observers can recover the entire source on this
preparation without a gravitational measurement. At 162 of the 190
placements the complete interstation timing distribution has exactly zero
contrast. Comparing station energies with those times resolves the
intervention at all 190 placements.

Encode a point by the midpoint $x$ of its two recorded directions. With a
surface clock proportional to angular distance, the shortest time for both
parts to reach a second encoded point is the angular distance between the
lifts $(x,\sqrt{1-|x|^2})$ on a hemisphere of length radius $L$. For a
central ball of angular radius $R$, retain each pair at depth $d_i$ with
probability $(\cos d_i-\cos R)_+/\sin R$. Let $Q_R$ be the measured modular
increment of the gated record including misses. Define $e_i=\kappa w_i/(2\pi
L)$ for measured shell fraction $w_i$ and full price $\kappa$. In a gap
between shells the two numbers
\[
Y(c)=\frac{\sin R}{2\pi L}Q_R=m-cM,\qquad
m=\frac{c_1Y(c_2)-c_2Y(c_1)}{c_1-c_2},\quad c=\cos R
\]
recover the active mass $m=\sum_{d_i<R}\cos d_i\,e_i$. This identification
requires spherical proper-volume entropy stationarity at the ordinary
coupling. In units of $\kappa/(2\pi L)$ the third-gap reading is
$0.457740$--$0.458199$. A second acquisition of the full clock-and-energy
alphabet at both apertures gives $0.450844$--$0.465107$. The direct address
census gives $0.457974$. Under spherical dust evolution this mass gives the
additional inward acceleration $Gm/r^2$, with $r=L\sin R$.

Independently define each of eight regions by a material delivery deadline.
Measure its volume by the count of reachable markers and its boundary by
crossing links. A public mixture of two deadlines restores the mean volume
exactly at finite dose. All eight regional entropies increase. Six
boundaries expand and two contract. Reordering the second word reverses all
eight boundary changes. Even arbitrary constant coefficients for the
boundary and two separately measured radial ordinary-energy terms leave
at least $0.00091683$ nats of residual at one of four centers. These
measurements exclude this identification of the regional stress and proper
geometry. The angular gate, dynamical law and conversion from entropy to
ordinary mass remain physical hypotheses for the dark source.

\paragraph{An agreed source record produces inward flight at exterior stations.}
One unit messenger passes the two calibrated observer contacts in succession.
Across all 81 sharp word pairs its final arrival is the product of the
two local pass bits. Sharing a fair source tag raises the arrival probability
from $25/36$ to $101/144$ at equal one-station marginals. The excess $1/144$
is the variance of the conditional pass rate. This one comparison and the
full modular tangent are proportional along the fresh-to-shared preparation
line. The receiver reads a single projection of the joint words.

A courier transports the arrival record from the dispatch cell. It takes
a native intercell swap from a uniformly chosen port, logs the destination
and repeats with probability $31/32$. After the journey, each accepted visit
toggles six disjoint internal memories at its cell. The stored contact
pattern persists after writing ceases. For transition matrix $P$ and
$G=(I-31P/32)^{-1}$, summing odd returns gives the excess written
probability $u_v/144$ with $u_v=G_{0v}/(2G_{vv}-1)$.
The dispatch, stopping rule and receiver operations are specified apparatus.
At two and three hops from dispatch the near-to-far difference is
$d=(0.1379585-0.0869008)/144$. A free in-phase probe transfers occupation
inward from zero initial current. The two loads give additional release
accelerations $(17d/4,65d/4)$ and the near-minus-far material clock
slows by $24d$ in native frequency units. A separate no-report control
has zero initial acceleration. The local arrival counts predict both
position readings and the clock at both enrolled durations within their
independently acquired count intervals.

An ordinary deposit at the end of the same courier route provides a mass
standard. Both ordinary deposit types and every positive mixture give
$A_2-2A_1\leq0$ for the two probe accelerations. The additional record
gives $A_2-2A_1=31d/4>0$. Changing the distribution of passive internal
marks preserves this response ratio on each open crossing of this type.
In the nearer cell, a survey of two face-sharing tetrahedra compensates
their mean volume using a public choice. At the independent-tag endpoint
the area derivative exceeds $0.0006767$ in squared range units and the
entropy derivative of the local latch and public choice exceeds $0.0021501$
nats per intervention. Both positive signs exclude first-order entropy
stationarity with a positive area coefficient on this material survey.
The measured source entails a persistent exterior clock field on the
specified receiver. Its ordinary mass and regional entropy tests constrain
the identification of that field with the dark contribution to gravity.

A single coherent launch covering an entire hop shell tests the scale
dependence of the response. Every additional written contact joins one
gateway to a private port. The Hamiltonian change projected on the gateway
launch is diagonal, while the commutator with the enclosed occupancy
has support on the crossing gateways. The measured coefficient is therefore
the same $(1/4+4q^2)$ times the mean written-degree gradient on the cut.
Over 78 complete shell and stopping-time combinations at 20, 80 and 320
cells the source gives extra inward release on all 78. On 77 the terminal
ordinary controls give an inward response satisfying $A_2\le2A_1$.
On the other shell both ordinary deposits give an outward response.
None admits a common positive ordinary mass for the record source.

\paragraph{An ordinary source persists through erasure of the agreement contacts.}
One ordinary load initially spread uniformly over 240 ports measures
the response of existing material to the agreement field.
Only two receiving cells on adjacent exterior shells write the courier's
report. Every intercell bridge permits exchange during exposure and
restoration.
Restore the contacts and wait again before locating the ordinary load.
Score its position by the difference between availability next to the near
and far gateways, retaining blocked launches. Write $b$ for the change of
the mean score from independent to shared tags. Ordinary two-load exchange
fixes the changes of the initial probe curvatures and clock frequency to
\[
       (\Delta A_1,\Delta A_2,\Delta\omega)=(17b/2,65b/4,-31b/2).
\]
The independently sampled positions give $b$ between $0.600$ and $1.243$
millionths. Dispatching the courier from the opposite station gives $b$
between $-2.122$ and $-1.479$ millionths. The finite spectral calculation
gives $0.922$ and $-1.812$ millionths, with error below $0.015$ millionths
including the finite waits. Fresh probes and clocks read the guarded crossing
after the position measurement. At duration $1/8$, all three detect the
predicted signs in both directions (Fig.~\ref{fig:ordinary_polarization}).
Using just the source position histograms and ordinary calibration counts
conditional on each location gives twelve finite-time predictions across
both durations and dispatch directions. Each interval overlaps the
independently sampled probe or clock interval. For the forward clock at
$t=1/8$ the prediction is $[-9.104,-4.396]$ millionths and the held read
gives $[-7.003,-6.477]$ millionths.
On this preparation family the measured change in the serial overlap
record causes a persistent, independently measurable redistribution with
the ordinary coupling of these instruments. The source contains the same
one unit of material throughout. Universality for other preparations at
the same modular charge would require an additional implication.

In the mirror-symmetric guarded pair the corresponding stationary
expectations are exactly opposite. A fair choice of calibrated delays
suppresses interference between distinct energies while preserving each
degenerate block. The bound on the difference from the stationary ensemble
holds uniformly in subsequent free delay. Repeated complete localization
between independent energy averages drives the guarded material toward
uniform occupation. Persistence here concerns freely evolving preparations
followed by one terminal read. For the primary written-contact effect an
additional swap read distinguishes a written seam from a signed ordinary
redistribution that would give the same two initial accelerations and beat.
It records vacancy and the identity of the returned load.

A further experiment compares local phases on fresh copies before recording
a position. The reached algebra distinguishes one-load modes inside the ball
and a single outside vacuum outcome. Both interference quadratures for
every local mode pair bound the fresh regional logarithm. At the same
twelve fixed-volume comparisons the resulting von Neumann entropy
derivative requires a negative area coefficient on every overlap trial.
At the first forward near pair both the entropy and the area grow, giving
$-\dot S/\dot A$ in $[-13.49,-12.79]$ for the induced source and
$[8.2959,8.2961]$ for the ordinary deposit. The local calibrated probe
response therefore coexists with a measured failure of positive
stationarity for this regional material algebra.

At that first pair an additional entropy $X$ at the same measured geometry
would have to give $X'_r-(A'_r/A'_m)X'_m$ between $-145.69$ and $-142.68$
millionths of a nat per intervention, where $r$ and $m$ denote the record
and ordinary settings. Appending the erased seam labels gives zero
because their value is certain for each chosen ball. This supplies a

\paragraph{Transmitting the modular charge by comparing reference records.}
The original serial instrument can give zero or outward change on a
positive modular intervention. Keep the exact one-station word histograms.
Moving probability only among passing pairs gives $0.00156505$ nats of
opposite-reference modular change and exactly the same serial arrival
law. A second joint preparation gives $0.00113231$ nats and decreases
serial admission by $1/144$. Any clock or material read conditioned
solely on that admission inherits this ambiguity.

An observer can transmit the full charge without assigning guessed
weights to individual words. Let $p$ be the reference frequency of the
joint energy and swap record actually observed. Compare it with fresh,
independent draws of the reference. Among the comparisons preceding the
first match, count the new minima in an independent exchangeable
ordering. Comparison $k$ contributes with probability $(1-p)^k/k$.
The expected count is exactly $-\log p$. A binary read uses one
public prefix length $k\le L$ of probability $1/(kH_L)$, where $H_L$
is the harmonic sum. A miss throughout the prefix dispatches one
courier. Its success probability $a(p)$ satisfies
\[
 0\le -\log p-H_La(p)\le
 \frac{(1-p)^{L+1}}{(L+1)p}.
\]
At $L=12{,}288$ the error for any contrast on all 81 calibrated symbols
is below $1.51\times10^{-14}$ nats. This is the truncation error.
The separate counts of modular charge have uncertainty about
$1.5\times10^{-8}$ nats. Ordinary material slots on a seven-swap route
transmit the second station's measured energy and arrival to the first.
All 81 symbol pairs are recoverable there.

On the 240-port board, a receiving event of conditional probabilities
$Y_1,Y_0$ has change $\Delta a(Y_1-Y_0)$ for any such input preparation.
Independent source measurements and a separate receiver calibration
predict the ordinary neighbor count, both probe flights and
the clock. All 24 finite-time comparisons across three changes of
correlation and two dispatch directions overlap their held intervals.
Figure~\ref{fig:reference_charge_transfer} shows the exterior clock
at $t=1/8$ after erasure. This is a finite causal implication for a
receiver implementing the comparison. Establishing which comparison
a freely reconstructed gravitational field implements is an independent
physical question.

\paragraph{Keeping the actual write history.}
Preserve each gateway's sharp write parity before erasing the contacts.
Which bits are accessible is fixed by the reached ball. Each deterministic
summary of two bits is a partition of four possible histories. Testing
all such summaries independently for the two radii gives 1,920 choices
in the twelve fixed-volume comparisons. Separate conditional material
phase and position measurements and both entropy secants at the fresh
reference give $-S'/A'<-7.28$ on every choice. Full retention makes the
ordinary calibration singular on ten comparisons. The no-write evolution
of the uniform launch stays in a four-dimensional span. On the first
six-port ball a local mode orthogonal to it receives the ordinary
localized load with probability $7/24$ conditional on no writes.
An infinitesimal admixture of that deposit opens an outcome of zero
reference probability. The entropy derivative diverges while the area
derivative remains finite. The charge-transmission result supplies a
source at ordinary material coupling on the receiving probes. This
archive cannot supply the required positive stationarity on the same
regions.

\paragraph{Ordinary mass and overlap charge in the same measured region.}
Install the complete reference comparison on the guarded 24-port material
instrument. Supply kinetic work to the single ordinary load by changing
its phase at one gateway. Five local phase-sensitive seam measurements
give $0.434665$ native units of work. Allow another independent free
wait. A public choice of two adjacent radii holds mean proper volume
fixed. On separate trials observers record the public radius, center
clock scale and whether the ordinary load lies inside. With work on a
quarter of preparations, all twelve pairs show a smaller boundary and
more enclosed ordinary material. At the smallest forward gateway pair
the enclosed fraction rises by $0.007518$ and the area falls by $0.001043$
in squared clock-length units. Regional material entropy increases to
first order. The ordinary reference load remains one throughout.

On that same pair, stationarity of material entropy plus $\eta A$ would
require $\eta$ between $79.23$ and $79.25$ for the kinetic intervention,
and between $35.93$ and $35.94$ for a localized ordinary deposit after
the same wait. Changing only the shared overlap preparation requires
$\eta$ between $-16.10$ and $-14.76$. All twelve full-reader comparisons
give positive first variations of both area and entropy for the overlap
source. Observers resolve ordinary contraction and modular charge in this
model. Identifying a regional stress that enters the Einstein equation
at a common ordinary coupling remains an independent premise.

Holding the localized ordinary calibration fixed specifies an independent
target for an additional measured entropy term $X$. At the first
full-reader gateway pair $X'=-S'-\eta A'$ lies in
$[-0.18092,-0.18087]$ nats for work and
$[-1.682,-1.608]\times10^{-5}$ nats for the overlap variation.
Both ordinary controls admit the same fresh source-word histogram.
A function only of that histogram has zero derivative for both.
Equation~\eqref{eq:tanom} further requires the measured regional
remainder to scale with the fourth power of proper radius at the
stress inferred from clock and orbit records.

At the smallest opposite-gateway pair a mixture of ordinary work and
localization holds both mean enclosed load and mean proper volume fixed.
The source histogram is also fixed. Its measured first variations give
positive changes in both area and entropy. With independent trials at a
finite quarter intervention, forward dispatch and the complete reader,
the area increases by $[4.4763,4.4771]\times10^{-5}$ squared clock-length
units. This excludes a repair to regional stationarity depending only
on the mean enclosed rest load and marginal source histogram on this survey.

\paragraph{Does the same charge produce the ordinary curvature and force?}
Two further clock stations observe the same finite intervention. From
thirty directed timings one can build six closed tetrahedral interiors
with the same measured edges. An ordinary parity write is diluted to
match the increase in head entropy before reading any of the clocks.
In five interiors the change of integrated spatial curvature, divided
by the cube root of volume, has opposite signs for the two sources.
In the sixth the joint write gives only 17--23 percent of the ordinary
response. Matching the entropy derivatives at zero dose also leaves
a resolved discrepancy in each interior. Two material probes
independently resolve their four-tick force changes. Ordinary
calibration gives disjoint energy intervals of
\(0.00046\)--\(0.00170\) and \(0.00984\)--\(0.01133\) in native units.

There is an exact ambiguity at the prepared reference. All six
curvatures, the anchor volume and eight material means can stay

reference modular charge varies. At the exact stationary point a

The physical identification in Eq.~\eqref{eq:tanom} therefore requires

\paragraph{Erasing a measured correlation on a fixed-volume ball.}
A local source experiment can distinguish two explanations for the
entropy term. Let \(X\) denote the joint record reconstructed from
parity and two-head Pauli counts, and \(P\) the independently paired
heads at the same parity and with the same separate marginals. Send
both through the same observer channel \(\Phi\), with a distinct
timeout outcome. Along \(X_t=(1-t)X+tP\), the negative derivative at
zero of the relative entropy lost in transmission is
\[
 q_\Phi=J(X,P)-J(\Phi X,\Phi P),\qquad
 J(X,P)=D(X\Vert P)+D(P\Vert X).
\]
Data processing in both directions gives \(0\le q_\Phi\le J(X,P)\)
for every fixed channel. The separate-head ordinary entropy slope
vanishes on this intervention. Thus a positive area coefficient in
fixed-volume stationarity requires a nonpositive area slope if this
lost score supplies the anomalous term.

Seven preparations on the 80-cell source give
\(0.2311048<J(X,P)<0.2311051\) nats from independent simulated
readings. At station 38 the source score from the five outgoing
routes, including both returned heads, lies between 0.2170001
and 0.2170004 nats per unit erasure fraction.
Thirty directed clocks independently determine the edges used in
six piecewise Euclidean spatial reconstructions. Consider the ball
at each of six vertices below the opposite faces. With
\(\Omega\) its solid angle, \(V=\Omega r^3/3\) and \(A=\Omega r^2\)
give \(\delta A|_V=r^2\delta\Omega/3\). At equilibrium radius
\(1/2\), fourteen of the 36 areas increase and twenty-two decrease.
Station 38 expands in all six reconstructions, even though an
ordinary parity intervention there calibrates a strictly positive
area coefficient. Holding the individual-head and parity energies
fixed removes a possible ordinary entropy contribution on the
erasure intervention. The sign conflict arises on a finite ball.

The observation aperture may itself respond to the reconstructed
geometry. Since \(Ar=3V\), any differentiable radius-only term
cancels from the erasure response after calibrating on the ordinary
intervention. Let the observer select an incident tetrahedron with
probability proportional to its measured solid angle, and then apply
a channel in that tetrahedron. Bounds on the input log likelihood
ratio control the variation even when the tetrahedron label is
hidden. At station 38 the ordinary-calibrated stationarity residual
exceeds \(0.1734\) nats per unit erasure for every such receiver in
all six reconstructions. The specific returned-head instrument gives
a residual between \(1.2722\) and \(1.3358\) nats with public angular
selection. These enclosures use simultaneous 99\% counting coverage
under the simulator's sampling law. A gravitational interpretation
requires an additional measured response or a different physical
regional geometry. The six triangulations supply examples of proper
balls compatible with the radar data. Selecting the physical filling
requires further observations. See \texttt{transmission\_balance.json} and
\texttt{erasure\_balls.json}.

\paragraph{A clock prediction from the source alone.}
A station chooses one of its neighbors uniformly, sends an ordinary
carrier, and records the first return after the carrier has visited
that neighbor. An independently prepared source is held for each
cycle. Let \(G=L^+\) for the measured network Laplacian,
\(R_{ij}=G_{ii}+G_{jj}-2G_{ij}\), \(d_i\) be the degree, and \(n\)
the number of stations. The round-trip mean on a link is \(6nR_{ij}\).
Thus the stationary period of the clock is
\(T_i=(6n/d_i)\sum_{j\sim i} E R_{ij}\).
At clean launch sites, harmonicity of the changed Green columns
and the ordinary single- and double-load hopping amplitudes give
\[
  12n\,\Delta\ddot R_{ik}=d_i\Delta T_i+d_k\Delta T_k .
\]
The local four-pulse readings determine the joint source weights in
these expectations. At station 48 the measured weights give a period
increase of \((2.425\pm0.016)\times10^{-5}\) slots (one sampling
standard error). All 18,502 enrolled clean pairs have a positive
change in initial separation acceleration on this ruler. This sign
describes relative motion on the specified finite chart. A source
measurement and the finite implication suffice to establish this
prediction without a microscopic gravitational force reading.

\paragraph{Spatial reach of this clock.}
For each link \(e\) write \(c_e\le 1\) for its conductance and
\(R_e=b_e^\top L^+b_e\) for its measured resistance. The trace
\(\sum_e c_eR_e=n-1\) and monotonicity give
\(\sum_e|R_e-R_e^0|=\sum_e(1-c_e)R_e
 \le\sum_e(c_e^{-1}-1)\) relative to the blank network.
At each station \(\sum_{e\ni i}R_e\ge1\) by the unit-current exit
bound. For a signed contrast \(D_z\) of preparations, it follows that
\[
  \sum_i|\Delta\log(1/T_i)|
  \le 2\sum_z |D_z|\sum_e(c_e(z)^{-1}-1).
\]
With a fixed source budget this bound is independent of the exterior
size. A nonzero Newtonian monopole would give octave clock differences
proportional to \(1/k\) on shells of proper radii \(k\ell\) and
\(2k\ell\) at fixed \(\ell\). Each shell enters at most twice. The
harmonic sum eventually exceeds the bound. This excludes the
asymptotic monopole for compact passive changes and this clock law.

On finite disks, distribute the measured response over independently
imaged tiles, retain its finite radial support, and add the responses
to first order in source abundance. Select hop length and positive
amplitude on the baryonic potentials of nine galaxies, then predict
the excess in the tenth. With squared circular speeds interpolated
linearly in log radius and two quoted speed errors at the endpoints,
30 of 359 annuli agree and no whole galaxy agrees at any of the 14
tracer and capacity settings. With separately adjustable gains and
an atomic-gas map, as many as eight galaxies pass the interval-shape
test at the largest tested scale. The ordinary normalization and
spatial mapping therefore matter. The empirical comparison and the
finite inference have different domains. See
\texttt{sector\_neighbor\_ticks.json} and
\texttt{sector\_neighbor\_galaxies.json}.

\begin{theorem}[Deep radial-acceleration relation]
\label{thm:rar}
The anomalous acceleration of the profile \eqref{eq:profile} is
\begin{equation}
a_A(r)=\frac{GM_A(r)}{r^2}=\frac{\sqrt{GM_ba_0}}{r}=\sqrt{a_b(r)\,a_0},
\qquad a_b=\frac{GM_b}{r^2}.
\label{eq:deep}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Flat rotation curve and baryonic Tully--Fisher relation]
\label{thm:btfr}
The circular speed supported by \(a_A\) satisfies
\(v^2=a_A r=\sqrt{GM_ba_0}\), independent of \(r\), and hence
\begin{equation}
v^4=GM_b\,a_0 .
\label{eq:btfr}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proofs]
\(GM_A/r^2=G\sqrt{M_ba_0/G}/r=\sqrt{GM_ba_0}/r\), and
\(\sqrt{GM_ba_0}/r=\sqrt{(GM_b/r^2)a_0}\) for \(r>0\). Multiplying by \(r\)
removes the radius; squaring gives \eqref{eq:btfr}.
\end{proof}

The effect of orbital radius has a separate explanation. Compare two
orbits at $r$ and $2r$ in this smooth deep exterior. Both enclose the
same baryonic mass, so the baryonic acceleration at the outer orbit
is one quarter as large. The enclosed anomalous mass doubles between
the two orbits, so its acceleration is one half as large. Both
accelerations decrease, while their ratio doubles. Explicitly
$a_A/a_b=r/r_M$ with $r_M=\sqrt{GM_b/a_0}$. This radius is measured
from the galaxy's mass centre. Identifying a sparse physical collar
and establishing that its charge gravitates are additional source
measurements. A rotation curve alone cannot infer the local density of
retained records.

The exponents \(1/2\) in \eqref{eq:deep} and \(4\) in \eqref{eq:btfr} are
the parameter-free content of the profile. The constant \(a_0\) collects
\(C_C\), the collar density, and the small-ball normalization; none of these
is fixed by the source, so \(a_0\) enters every comparison as a fitted
value. The deep regime is the range \(a_b\ll a_0\), equivalently
\(r\gg r_M=\sqrt{GM_b/a_0}\). If the deep expression is algebraically
extended to the matching radius, both accelerations equal \(a_0\) there and
the anomalous term dominates outside \(r_M\); those algebraic statements are
machine-checked. The premises are asserted only in the deep regime, so they
do not determine the physical transition or the response inside \(r_M\).

\subsection{A forward prediction in Andromeda}

M31 lies outside the SPARC calibration. Distribute a coherent source over
its measured stellar and gas profile and propagate it with the spherical
kernel $\exp(-\mu r)/r$. Taking its squared amplitude as the excess
density gives an aperture mass and circular speed. The SPARC scale fixes
the dimensional gain. Allocating the cosmic cold-to-baryon mass ratio to
this galaxy fixes $\mu=0.0058661\,{\rm kpc}^{-1}$ before comparing the
velocities. These are additional astrophysical assumptions. Five disk
scale lengths set the inner radius of the test. The outer rise and warp
were known when the target was chosen.

The finite-range prediction is within one published uncertainty on eight
of nine outer rings in the reduction of Corbelli et al.\ and zero of 27 in
the reduction of Chemin et al. At 38.09 kpc the latter gives
$275.1\pm4.8$ km/s. Baryons alone give 131.1 km/s, the transported
source gives 209.6 km/s and infinite survival gives 226.9 km/s.
At 36.5 kpc Corbelli et al.\ give $240.1\pm38.2$ km/s against 211.6 km/s.

A subsequent shape test eliminates the overall conversion from squared
source amplitude to mass. From 28.17 to 38.09 kpc the nominal Chemin
velocities require the enclosed excess mass to grow by a factor of 2.727.
Across all tested baryon variations and two favorable quoted errors this
factor is at least 1.879. On the declared positive spherical source shells
it is at most 1.476 across those variations for every survival length.
This excludes repairing that reading by changing the gain or lifetime.
The two surveys observe the same warped disk with different outer
coverage and uncertainty models. The wider Corbelli uncertainties
permit the construction. Independent spatial tracers are needed to
decide whether the discrepancy lies in the source prescription or in
the circular speeds inferred from the warped gas.%
\footnote{\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/0909.3846}{Chemin, Carignan and Foster};
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/0912.4133}{Corbelli et al.};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/m31_transport}{source tables, velocity forecasts, aperture inequalities and survey uncertainties}.}

An independent test uses hot halo red giants observed by SPLASH.
The bin covering the disputed gas radii gives a line-of-sight velocity
dispersion of $98.0^{+7.2}_{-6.6}$ km/s. At its median radius of
33.29 kpc the isotropic stellar Jeans projection gives 105.5 km/s
with the fixed finite transport, 121.6 km/s at infinite survival and
58.9 km/s from baryons. With radial anisotropy $\beta=0.5$ the
finite-range prediction is 98.4 km/s. In the isotropic finite model
84.4\% of the projected dispersion variance comes from forces beyond
the last Chemin gas ring. These stars constrain the extended potential;
the data do not independently resolve the steep local rise.%
\footnote{\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1711.02700}{Gilbert et al., SPLASH
velocity dispersions} and
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/m31_transport}{frozen force profiles, tracer selection and projection}.}

Varying the finite contact reference with radius provides a direct
test of one possible correction to the M31 forecast. Across the six
faithful microcanonical banks from 128 to 4096 contacts, the ratio
of outer to inner response per unit of sharp decorrelation energy
can reach at most 1.124, even allowing the ordinary calibration to
vary with the bank. If the original aperture mass ratio is bounded
by $A$, the reweighted mass ratio is at most $1+1.124(A-1)$.
The nominal Chemin endpoints require a shell-to-interior gain ratio
of at least 3.90. Pairing the baryon variations and two favorable
quoted errors requires at least 1.888. The Corbelli errors
permit a common gain. This is a retrospective comparison at the same galaxy endpoints.

\section{Lensing and the Relativistic Source}
\label{sec:lensing}

The anomalous charge must enter \eqref{eq:einstein} through one stress tensor;
Premise~\ref{pre:universal} supplies no readout-dependent coupling. That fact
does not imply equality of masses inferred from null geodesics and timelike
orbits. Those observables contract different components of the metric response
and can differ in the presence of pressure, anisotropic stress, or gravitational
slip. A static spherical exterior makes the extra stress assumption and its
observable consequences explicit. Write $m(r)=b+hr$ on an annulus,
$b=GM_b/c^2$, $h=\sqrt{GM_ba_0}/c^2$, with Schwarzschild mass length
$m(R_t)$ beyond a finite edge $R_t$. Use areal radius and static proper
clocks with $g_{tt}=-e^{2\Phi}$, $g_{rr}=(1-2m/r)^{-1}$ in units with
$c=1$. Specify zero radial pressure. The radial Einstein equation and
stress conservation give respectively
\[
 \beta^2=\frac{v_{\rm circ}^2}{c^2}=r\Phi'
          =\frac{m}{r-2m},\qquad p_t=\tfrac12\rho\beta^2 .
\]
Here $\rho$ is the energy density and $p_t$ either tangential pressure.
At the edge the radial pressure and both fundamental forms match the
vacuum exterior. The density may jump there. This is the static
tangential-stress branch of the Einstein-cluster type
\cite{lakeCluster,pressureOptics}. The calculation specifies a stress
law without assigning a constituent dark particle. In a scale-free
annulus $m=hr$, choosing $p_r=w\rho$ instead gives
$r\Phi'=h(1+w)/(1-2h)$. The same enclosed mass can therefore give
different circular speeds. The radial closure and the location and
normalization of the match are physical hypotheses.

For the specified zero-radial-pressure annulus the clock function is
\[
 2[\Phi(r)-\Phi(R_t)]
   =-\log\frac{r}{R_t}
     +\frac{1}{1-2h}\log\frac{(1-2h)r-2b}{(1-2h)R_t-2b}.
\]
A null probe turning at areal radius $r_0$ has asymptotic bending
\[
 \alpha=2\int_0^1
 \frac{\sqrt{g_{rr}(r_0/u)}\,du}
 {\sqrt{\exp(2\Phi(r_0)-2\Phi(r_0/u))-u^2}}-\pi .
\]
The same metric predicts the circular, light and clock readings. For
$b=0$, $h=10^{-6}$ and $R_t/r_0=2,10,100$, the predicted angles are
$5.261,6.083,6.263$ microradians. These synthetic tests use the
supplied relativistic source. They fix neither the radial pressure nor
a spacetime metric for native collars.

\paragraph{Weak shear of a finite edge.}
For spherical excess density $A/r^2$ with a sharp cutoff at $R_t$,
integrate its column and cylinder mass separately. At projected ratio
$y=R/R_t$, its differential surface density divided by the unlimited
value $\pi A/R$ is
\[
 F(y)=
 \begin{cases}
 [4y/(1+\sqrt{1-y^2})+2\arccos y]/\pi,& 0\le y\le 1,\\
 4/(\pi y),&y\ge1 .
 \end{cases}
\]
It increases up to $4/\pi$ at the edge and decreases beyond it. The
same ceiling holds for nonnegative mixtures of truncated profiles of
unit total weight. For the six isolated bins of KiDS-bright and GAMA
\cite{brouwer2021}, choose positive weights inversely proportional to
the diagonal variances and propagate the full published covariance.
At nominal masses and bin centres, with the full-SPARC deep scale
$8.7504\times10^{-11}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$, even the maximum over a
common distribution of $g_t=GM_b/R_t^2$ lies $4.00$ and $2.78$
statistical errors below the two weighted shear measurements.
Allowing arbitrary nonnegative sampling weights within the published
bins reduces the gaps to $0.98$ and $2.09$ errors. These are
retrospective comparisons on the same survey imaging. They distinguish
an assumption about within-bin sampling from the shear measurement.

\paragraph{One normalized mass profile for dwarf orbits and galaxy shear.}
Take the outer measured ring of AGC~114905 at $r_d=9.95$ kpc with
$v_d=26.94\pm1.83\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$, and its total baryonic
mass $(1.47\pm0.14)\times10^9 M_\odot$. Assign its entire observed
squared speed to the positive spherical excess, giving
$f_d=M_A(r_d)/M_d\le v_d^2r_d/(GM_d)$. This conservative upper
bound uses only a nonnegative baryonic contribution to the squared
speed. It accommodates the measured disk's extended baryon distribution
in this inequality.

Suppose a single $f(x)=M_A(R)/M_b$, $x=R/\sqrt{GM_b}$, connects all
the compared radii and galaxies, with $f$ nondecreasing and $f/x$
nonincreasing. A linear deep mass profile and sublinear universal tapers
belong to this family. At any $x$ one has
$f(x)\le[v_d^2r_d/(GM_d)]\max(1,x/x_d)$.
A thin spherical shell of mass $dM$ outside projected radius $R$ makes
the following contribution to the weak differential surface density,
where $z=\sqrt{1-R^2/r^2}$:
\[
 \frac{\pi R^2\,d\Delta\Sigma}{dM}
 =1-z-\frac{1-z^2}{2z}=-\frac{(1-z)^2}{2z}\le0 .
\]
Enclosed positive shells give their full mass divided by $\pi R^2$.
Hence ordinary weak-field stress optics imply
$\Delta\Sigma\le M(<R)/(\pi R^2)$. Combining the bounds gives a
test independent of $a_0$ and of a particular edge. Treat the lens
baryons as compact and allow every measured bin its most favorable
pair weighting. The result in $M_\odot\,\mathrm{pc^{-2}}$ is
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lrrr}
\toprule
Lens selection & Measured weighted $\Delta\Sigma$ & Central ceiling & Generous ceiling\\
\midrule
KiDS-bright & $17.30\pm0.69$ & $5.04$ & $11.36$\\
GAMA & $22.73\pm2.99$ & $4.97$ & $11.18$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The generous ceiling lowers the dwarf mass by two quoted errors,
raises its distance and speed by two quoted errors, permits a further
inclination change from $31^\circ$ to $27^\circ$, and increases each
lens mass by $0.2$ dex. These settings are sensitivity bounds. The
statistical errors use the published shear covariance conditional on
calibration and membership. With one sharp edge acceleration the
generous ceilings are lower, $9.94$ and $9.74$. The measured dwarf
ring lies inside its observed baryonic disk. These comparisons
disfavor a universal spherical sublinear extension to that radius and
the isolated lens apertures under the stated optical and baryonic
assumptions. They leave the far exterior of the dwarf, distributed or
environment-dependent collar sources and different pressure laws
undetermined. The public archive, metric solver and projection checks
are bound in \path{code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/finite_halo}.

\paragraph{Exterior orbits of early-type galaxies.}
The 16 rotating early-type galaxies of den Heijer et al. and Lelli et al.
\cite{denheijer2015,lelli2017etg} offer a test of the preceding bound outside
most of the baryons. Take the outer neutral-hydrogen orbit and require
$R\ge5R_e$, where $R_e$ is the effective radius of the light. In the
published stellar models, a cylinder of radius $0.9R$ and half-height
$R\sqrt{1-0.9^2}$ fits inside the sphere. Integrate the disk in this
cylinder and the spherical bulge to $R$, then subtract one percent of the
total stellar mass as a numerical allowance. Take a stellar mass-to-light
ratio of 0.6 for selection, and allow all measured atomic gas plus helium,
augmented by 0.1 dex, to lie outside $R$. Nine galaxies enclose at least
90 percent of these modelled baryons and fourteen enclose at least 80 percent.
Uncounted gas and errors in the stellar profiles affect this selection.

At the catalog distances and stellar mass-to-light ratio 0.8, subtract the
released squared stellar speed and use the authors' negligible local gas
force approximation. Let $[l_i,u_i]$ be the resulting interval for the
halo-to-baryon mass ratio at scaled radius $x_i$. The smallest admissible
profile is $f_{\min}(x)=\max_i l_i\min(1,x/x_i)$. It is nondecreasing,
and $f_{\min}(x)/x$ is nonincreasing. A common profile exists exactly
when $f_{\min}(x_j)\le u_j$ for every orbit. A formal proof for a finite list of intervals appears in \texttt{HaloStress}~\cite{ophlean}. At one quoted speed error
NGC~4262 requires $f\ge1.99$ at the scaled radius of NGC~3626, which
permits $f\le0.51$. The minimal common speed-error multiplier for the
nine orbits is 4.443. Both limiting orbits have baryonic accelerations
above $1.2\times10^{-11}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$.

The stellar-force-subtracted central orbit caps bound the two weighted
shears at 6.62 and 6.54 $M_\odot\,\mathrm{pc}^{-2}$, respectively.
Deleting any one galaxy raises those ceilings to at most 8.99 and 8.89.
Moving the distance, inclination and speed by up to two quoted errors,
lowering the stellar mass-to-light ratio to 0.6 and the gas mass by
0.1 dex, and raising the lens masses by 0.2 dex permits 31.34 and 31.03.
These are sensitivity boxes. Inclination contributes to the quoted speed
errors, and the two lens selections use common imaging. The generous
ceilings therefore supply neither a joint probability nor an exclusion.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\IfFileExists{code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/outer_orbit_transfer/comparison.pdf}
  {\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/outer_orbit_transfer/comparison.pdf}}
  {\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{../code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/outer_orbit_transfer/comparison.pdf}}
\caption{Upper left: inferred excess mass at the nine exterior orbits, with
one quoted speed error at fixed central photometry and distance. The
line transfers the NGC~3626 upper bound to other scaled radii. Upper right:
acceleration scales for a linear exterior mass profile. The bars use
one quoted distance and speed error for the orbits and two statistical
errors for each mean shear. The shaded interval is the common lens range.
NGC~3626 lies outside the low-acceleration subset. Lower panels:
individual shear bins at $a_0=1.457\times10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$.
The shaded bands show the prediction range across each acceleration bin.
The plotted uncertainty uses the diagonal covariance elements.}
\end{figure}

For the untrimmed linear profile $f=\sqrt{a_0/g_b}$, each weighted mean
shear at the bin centres gives an interval for $\sqrt{a_0}$ independently
of rotation. The two intervals at two statistical errors overlap at
$a_0=(1.457\text{--}1.810)\times10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$.
At the catalog distances and stellar mass-to-light ratio, this lens
interval forecasts $235.79$--$244.02\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$ at the outer
NGC~3626 orbit, observed at $169\pm8$, and $153.36$--$160.24$ at
NGC~3838, observed at $159\pm14$, in the same units. Only NGC~3838
passes the 90-percent enclosure cut and the additional
low-acceleration cut $g_b\le1.2\times10^{-11}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$.
At 80 percent, NGC~2685 and NGC~6798 also enter. One explicit profile
at the lower end of the lens interval reproduces these three orbits
with shifts of at most 0.575 quoted errors in distance and speed.
The corresponding distances are 13.34, 24.83 and 31.96 Mpc for
NGC~2685, NGC~3838 and NGC~6798. The same feasibility test on the
nine primary objects requires 3.015 errors, at fixed stellar
mass-to-light ratio and inclination. These comparisons of weighted
shears and individual outer speeds are retrospective. The six individual bins provide a further test of the same example. At
the stated scale its bin-centre predictions give $\chi^2=24.38$ for
KiDS-bright and $12.89$ for GAMA, using the full published covariances.
Even fitting the scale separately to each profile gives minima of 22.67
and 12.18 for five residual degrees of freedom. At the fixed scale,
optimizing a separate positive weighting inside each bin reduces the
covariance distances to 4.39 and 8.05. These are lower bounds with
unknown pair weights, with no assigned sampling probability. Agreement
of the two weighted means therefore coexists with a mismatch of the
KiDS-bright bin-centre profile. The native normalization, the survey
pair weights and the transition to higher acceleration require
independent measurements.

\subsection{Energy required outside a lensing aperture}

Assume the excess is entirely a redistribution of initially
gravitating energy at equal mass per unit energy everywhere. The
total change then vanishes and the final density is nonnegative.
The initial stock outside any aperture must exceed or equal its
enclosed positive mass change. For an initially bounded donor
density $\rho_{\max}$ with outer reach $R_d$ this gives
\[
  R_d^3 \ \geq\ R^3 + \frac{3\,\delta M(<R)}{4\pi\rho_{\max}} .
\]
Mistele et al.\ report circular speeds inferred from weak lensing
for four isolated-galaxy mass bins. At the last tabulated radius below
their conservative 300 kpc isolation limit, $R=291.07$ kpc, the
required exterior mass deficits are at least $0.402$, $0.772$,
$2.030$ and $3.423$ trillion solar masses on the respective weighted
stacks. These favorable values lower the inferred acceleration by
two statistical errors and the published deprojection error and allow
twice the upper catalogue baryon mass in each bin. With an available
density equal to the cosmic mean baryonic density at $z=0.5$, the
implied outer radii are at least $1.66$, $2.06$, $2.85$ and
$3.39$ proper Mpc. The density assumption enters the reach estimate.
The exterior debit follows from conservation under the stated
gravitational interpretation. A pre-existing positive stress
component needs its own absolute abundance.%
\footnote{\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09685}{Mistele et al., isolated-galaxy weak lensing};
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/compensation}{aperture masses, independent shear comparison and conserving-shell control}.}

\section{Comparison with Rotation-Curve Data}
\label{sec:data}

Theorems~\ref{thm:rar} and \ref{thm:btfr} are compared with the SPARC sample
of 175 disk galaxies with \(3.6\,\mu\)m photometry~\cite{sparc2016} under the
declared inclusive-error deep-law diagnostic: quality flag at most 2,
inclination at least \(30^\circ\), velocity error at most ten percent, and
stellar mass-to-light ratios \(0.5\) for disks and \(0.7\) for bulges. This
leaves 2700 points in 149 galaxies. The strict published-cut replay described
below is kept separate. Both computations are released with the
paper~\cite{mls2016,ophrar}.

\paragraph{An outer-orbit bound on the transported clock shift.}
Normalize each receiver clock to its own independent-tag frequency.
For a fraction $\lambda$ of the serial source intervention the factor is
$1-\lambda u_v/[144(5-25u_v/36)]$. A full intervention gives a
two-to-three-hop differential log rate of $7.31936\times10^{-5}$.
In a weak spherical field the excess circular speed gives
$\Delta\log N=\int v_{\rm extra}^2\,dr/(c^2r)$. Enclose $rv_{\rm extra}^2$
by the cumulative lower and suffix upper bounds of the outer rotation
records, assuming nonnegative nondecreasing enclosed dark mass.
Use the two-speed-error bands, twice the catalogue distance and inclination
errors and independent allowed disk and bulge mass-to-light ratios.
Of the 136 SPARC selections, 128 give multi-radius upper bounds, with
96 positive lower bounds. Eight give one usable radius.
If the entire native edge lies inside a measured outer radial span and
its clock measures the same excess lapse, every upper bound excludes
the full intervention. Even UGC06614 permits at most $6.23\%$ and
UGCA444 at most $0.001240\%$. This comparison uses rotation speeds to
infer a lapse. The mapping from galaxy records to the active fraction
and the two radii is a separate premise \cite{sparc2016}.

\paragraph{Testing the measured radial distribution and energy budget.}
Allow all cells to receive the overlap record. On the three finite
carriers the early ordinary excess is globally compensated. All 78
enrolled shells enclose positive excess and every exterior cell loses
ordinary probability at second order in exposure time. Testing any
nonnegative decreasing enclosed excess on 1,288 outer SPARC records
excludes 81 of 136 galaxies, with common catalogue nuisance parameters
within each galaxy and the same enlarged speed bands \cite{sparc2016}.

Another regional observable counts the original load in modes confined to
one cell under all restored free delays. The twenty-cell board has three
such modes per cell. The refined boards have three to five per cell,
depending on their gateways. The leading occupancy is $C_v t^6$,
where the joint odd-visit law at that cell and its three neighbors fixes
$C_v$. It is strictly positive in every cell of the 20-, 80- and
320-cell boards, with a finite positive duration certified at each size.
Sampling 8,192 independent native courier routes provides a separate
unit-exposure acquisition on 20 cells. Assign this compact component
alone to spherical enclosed mass, locating the dispatch cell at the
center. Even allowing a free length and mass scale per galaxy across
every embedding of the five nonzero chord shells, the leading profile
is excluded on 43 of 136 galaxies. Of those, 25 exclude the entire
finite-duration envelope at $t=2^{-19}$. The 93 compatible leading fits
use scales selected using the sky data. A cosmological prediction
requires an operational way to fix those scales and the gravitating
population from galaxy records alone.

After restoration the uniform ordinary rest standard is 40 native units
and the one-load kinetic bandwidth is at most 5. The random-contact
antisymmetric-mode read resolves forward excess kinetic work of about
$2.09\times10^{-5}$ units. The excess arrival probability $1/144$ bounds
the added mean energy by $1/1152$ of the initial rest energy, for any
sharp receiving pattern in this preparation. In the base-$\Lambda$CDM
analysis of \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.06209}{Planck 2018},
$\Omega_c h^2/\Omega_b h^2 \simeq 0.120/0.0224\simeq 5.36$.
Under a baryonic identification for the load and ordinary-energy
coupling with that cosmological transfer, this switching energy is
insufficient. Deriving a different gravitational response requires
its own stress, expansion and perturbation calculation.

\paragraph{An absolute ordinary-mass bound with a measured source law.}
For one reference-comparison exposure per pre-existing independent
ordinary load, its change in the probability of lying inside any given
sphere is at most $|\Delta a|$. At the measured three word-class marginals
$(1/3,1/2,1/6)$ the ten vertices of the allowed joint distributions
bound the largest single-exposure span by $0.008878$. Allowing both
marginals to vary raises the bound to $0.245831$. These dimensionless
limits allow arbitrary movement of admitted ordinary material.

Apply them to the frozen 1,288 outer SPARC rings \cite{sparc2016}.
For a radius $r$ at catalogue distance, a lower squared speed $v_-^2$,
the largest allowed distance--inclination factor $h_{\max}$, and an
upper baryonic squared-speed term $B_{\max}$, a conservative lower
bound on the required fraction of the total ordinary inventory is
$r[v_-^2/h_{\max}-B_{\max}]_+/(G M_{\rm stock,max})$. Maximize
the stellar and gas inventory independently of the baryonic speed,
including twice the quoted luminosity error and up to 1.4 solar units
of stellar mass per luminosity unit over the entire galaxy. Use 1.33
times the atomic hydrogen mass for gas. Of 136 galaxies, 105 exclude
the fixed-marginal calibration and 83 exclude even arbitrary marginals.
Allowing the hydrogen contribution to both force and inventory to range
from half to twice its catalogue value leaves 85 and 61 exclusions.
Seventeen require more than the entire permitted stock. The
lower bounds for DDO~154 and NGC~3741 are 1.84 and 4.09 times the
stock in this expanded test. These statements assume spherical extra
acceleration from independent ordinary loads at ordinary coupling.
Modified geometric stress or cumulative collective dynamics require
their own transfer and normalization.

\paragraph{The active spherical profile from observed pair separations.}
For every pair of unit endpoint directions $a,b$, its active mass factor
$c_i$ is half the observed chord $|a-b|/2$. In the isotropic limit $c$
has density $2c$ and radius fraction $\sqrt{1-c^2}$. Integrating its
mass factor gives the normalized enclosed shape
$F(x)=1-(1-x)_+^{3/2}$ for $x=(r/L)^2$. On 5,120 angular stations the
measured fractions of the full conditional word price inside $r/L=1/2$
and $1$ are $0.233493$ and $0.666797$. The continuum predicts $0.233654$
and $2/3$. This angular continuation and the circular acceleration law
are model inputs when applied to galaxies.

Continue the enclosed mass beyond the screen edge as a constant. In the
weak spherical circular-orbit comparison, 120 of 136 enrolled SPARC
galaxies admit a single such screen, with strength and edge fitted
individually, under the doubled speed-error and baryonic allowances used
here. Sixteen exclude every single screen. Across the 1,288 measured rings,
NGC 5907, UGC 00128 and UGC 09133 exclude even arbitrary
positive concentric mixtures. Each of the other 133 admits a finite
