mixture with certified positive weights and permitted ordinary parameters.
The exclusions cover the entire continuous edge range. An independent
check is $F(x)=\int_0^1\min(x/t,1)\,3t/(4\sqrt{1-t})\,dt$, so every such
mixture lies inside the broader flat-screen mixture family. Galaxy-specific
weights remain fitted quantities. See \texttt{curved\_orbits.json}.

\paragraph{Source-bound full-RAR calibration.}
Separate from the conditional OPH deep law, a deterministic replay fits the
published empirical interpolation
\[
g_{\rm obs}=\frac{g_{\rm bar}}
 {1-\exp[-\sqrt{g_{\rm bar}/a_0}]}
\]
to a committed CDS/VizieR snapshot whose provenance, catalogue description,
and two table files are pinned by SHA-256. The catalogue cuts reproduce the
published 153-galaxy parent selection. The strict point cuts retain 2696
points in 147 contributing galaxies, three more than the published 2693-point
census. These counts are distinct from the preceding 2700-point, 149-galaxy
deep-law diagnostic: that diagnostic uses the inclusive cut
\(dV/V\le0.1\), whereas this full-RAR replay uses the strict cut
\(dV/V<0.1\) and starts from the 153-galaxy parent set. With disk and bulge
mass-to-light values 0.5 and 0.7, the unweighted
point-level log-residual fit gives
\[
a_0=(1.1613\mathbin{\pm}0.0802)\times10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}},
\qquad \sigma_{\log_{10}g}=0.1327\ \mathrm{dex},
\]
where the width is the standard deviation of a galaxy bootstrap. Equal total
weight per contributing galaxy shifts \(a_0\) by \(-9.58\%\); weighting only
by inverse fractional velocity variance shifts it by \(+15.49\%\). These are
estimator-sensitivity controls, not calibrated likelihood alternatives. The
interpolation and acceleration scale were known before this replay, no OPH
source value enters the fit, and distance, inclination, gas, stellar
population, intrinsic scatter, and correlation uncertainties are not jointly
marginalized. The result certifies public-data custody and arithmetic, not
independent evidence for OPH~\cite{mls2016,ophrar}.

\paragraph{How large a source do the outer orbits require?}
In SPARC we selected quality 1 or 2, inclination at least \(30^\circ\),
and radii at least three published disk scale lengths. We allowed
twice the quoted errors of speed, distance and inclination, a common
disk mass-to-light ratio per galaxy in \([0.1,1]\), a bulge ratio in
\([0.1,1.4]\), and the published signed gas template. Of 1,288 rings
in 136 galaxies, 816 rings in 106 galaxies require extra inward
acceleration throughout these boxes. At 7 kpc in NGC 3741 the total
is at least \(8.7082\,g_b\). An arbitrary monotone enhancement shared
across galaxies and determined solely by stellar surface brightness
fails even if only positive brightnesses differing by a factor of two
are ordered. Six rings in NGC 2403, NGC 3109 and UGC 00891 give an
exact rational inconsistency certificate. The retrospective held
galaxy subset gives a separate contradiction. Stellar light alone
therefore fails this proposed identification with the source. To
predict those accelerations from overlap records requires the physical
record-to-stress map. See \texttt{outer\_requirement.json} and
\texttt{shared\_outer\_order.json}.

\paragraph{Does fainter starlight predict more apparent gravity within a galaxy?}
We selected one pair of annuli per galaxy before reading their orbital speeds,
using the same SPARC catalogue~\cite{sparc2016}. Of 153 galaxies passing
the quality and inclination cuts, 70 supplied a pair whose local
$3.6\,\mu{\rm m}$ surface brightness differed by at least a factor of two.
Both nominal baryonic accelerations lay below
$1.2\times10^{-11}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ and agreed within 10\%.
Write $t=g_{b,f}/g_{b,h}$ for the baryonic acceleration ratio in the
fainter and brighter annuli. For $g_{\rm orb}=g_b+A\sqrt{g_b}$ with a
common $A\ge0$, the predicted orbital ratio lies between $t$ and $\sqrt t$.
Allowing $A_f\ge A_h\ge0$ preserves the lower bound.
The measured ratio $(v_f/v_h)^2\theta_h/\theta_f$ cancels the common
distance and inclination, where $\theta$ is angular radius.
Allowing each speed twice its quoted error placed two pairs above the
constant-amplitude interval, eight below and 60 overlapping.
The eight lower ratios contradict the monotone brightness prescription
at the adopted baryonic forces. Keeping these annuli and the gas force
fixed, five conflicts persist for all disk and bulge mass-to-light ratios
in $[0.4,0.6]$ and $[0.56,0.84]$, respectively, shared between the two
annuli. Seven persist when the revised forces must also satisfy the
original 10\% match. UGC~06667 remains deep, matched and discrepant
throughout both mass ranges. Every fainter annulus was also farther
from its galaxy centre. The comparison cannot distinguish these two
local properties or assign a population significance to the speed boxes.
Measuring how frequently the galactic matter retains physical records
would test the proposed source more directly. The full retrospective
selection and its wider acceleration control are deposited in
\path{code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/brightness_pairs/}.

\paragraph{Can the gas normalization account for the lower orbital ratios?}
Five of the seven discrepant pairs admit no common gas multiplier while
their baryonic forces remain matched within 10\%. At the two original
radii in UGC~06667 the lower orbital inequality requires a multiplier
at most 0.319, allowing disk mass-to-light ratios from 0.4 to 0.6.
The optically thin catalogue mass at its adopted distance then permits
at most $3.38\,\mathrm{Jy\,km\,s^{-1}}$ of integrated hydrogen flux.
The independent Fisher--Tully single-dish measurement is
$11.6\pm2.2\,\mathrm{Jy\,km\,s^{-1}}$, alongside the Westerbork value
of $11.0\pm0.4$ in the same units~\cite{ursaflux}. The single-dish
lower endpoint at twice its quoted error is 7.2. Thus changing the
total gas mass alone cannot account for this shape discrepancy with
the catalogue radial profile. At the measured inclination of
$89^\circ$, radial redistribution and projection remain relevant.

Ten of the 70 original pairs also have independent ALFALFA total
hydrogen masses tabulated at the SPARC distances~\cite{yasinflux}.
Transferring their central mass ratios to the gas force at both
original radii gives shape overlap in all ten at the nominal stellar
masses. Requiring the revised forces to meet both original cuts,
allowing the stellar calibration range, printed mass rounding and 10\%
error on each hydrogen mass, gives eight overlaps. UGC~12732 lies
below the shape interval and UGC~05750 falls outside the selection.
These integrated fluxes constrain a common gas multiplier. A radial
gas-force test requires a resolved profile.

\paragraph{Testing one compact-source normalization between galaxies.}
For compact baryons the predicted contribution to squared circular speed
is independent of radius, $v_A^2=\sqrt{GM_ba_0}$. We add this contribution
to the catalogued baryonic mass-model curve. Using total photometric and
atomic-gas masses, we select 151 outer deep-regime rings of 41 bulge-free
SPARC galaxies. The nominal baryonic acceleration
lies between zero and $1.2\times10^{-11}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$.
Each ring is at or beyond both four disk scale lengths and the H\,I radius. The comparison retains negative inferred excesses
and all quoted speed errors. With catalogue geometry and the reference
stellar mass-to-light ratio, even the best common positive $a_0$ misses
at least one speed by $33.01$ quoted errors. Allowing stellar mass-to-light
ratios between $0.4$ and $0.6$ and distance and inclination within one
catalogue error leaves a lower bound of $6.00$ such errors, even if each
ring can choose those inputs separately. At a tolerance of $3.590$ quoted
speed errors NGC~0024 requires $a_0\ge1.372\times10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$
and NGC~0801 requires $a_0\le0.845\times10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$.
Widening the two geometry bounds to twice their quoted errors admits an
explicit solution at $a_0\simeq1.146\times10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ with one
parameter set per galaxy. A control allowing any nonnegative nondecreasing spherical excess-mass
profile per galaxy covers all 151 speeds within $0.366$ quoted errors at
nominal geometry. This freedom fits the radial data without specifying
the source. A disjoint calibration fits the deep radial acceleration law
on 584 rings in 74 other galaxies. Transferring its fitted
$a_0=7.603\times10^{-11}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ into the compact
photometric prediction on the 41 exterior galaxies gives a worst-ring
lower bound of $6.25$ quoted speed errors even with the wider geometry
bounds. This checks transfer between the two baryonic prescriptions on
disjoint galaxies.

\paragraph{Sensitivity to disk geometry.}
Using the measured radial baryonic force throughout the transfer
reduces the worst-ring lower bound to $5.49$ speed errors. The limiting
ring is NGC~0024 at $11.27$ kpc, measured at $110.0\pm2.83$ km/s.
The most favourable speeds in the wider geometry boxes are $92.30$ km/s
for the compact profile and $94.47$ km/s for the local-force prescription.
The any-hit saturation of the balanced material comparison only suppresses a positive
source in its joint scaling limit. If it multiplies that same
gravitating source at fixed calibration, $v^2=v_b^2+\alpha v_A^2$
is increasing in $0\le\alpha\le1$. Even its upper endpoint reaches
only $92.30$ km/s in the compact transfer. Saturation alone therefore
cannot remove this held deficit within the stated geometry bounds.
The local-force prescription admits shared per-galaxy nuisance parameters that cover all
150 other rings within tolerance. These parameters use the exterior
speeds in their selection. The original outer H\,I velocity is
$109.7\pm2.0$ km/s. Its fitted inclination is $64\pm3$ degrees, whereas
inner H$\alpha$ kinematics give $75\pm3$ degrees
\cite{chemin24,dicaire24}. A stellar distance estimate gives
$7.67\pm0.32$ Mpc~\cite{schombert20}. Holding $a_0$ fixed, a distance
of $8.31$ Mpc, inclination of $78$ degrees and disk mass-to-light ratio
of $0.6$ put the outer ring within tolerance. The central stellar
distance and inner inclination with a ratio of $0.5$ give $91.57$ km/s.

Using the TRGB distances and H$\alpha$ inclinations for every
catalogue match (six distances and two inclinations) admits one
parameter set per galaxy for all 151 speeds at the unchanged scale.
The maximum discrepancy is $3.583$ quoted errors against the $3.590$
tolerance. Substituting only distances or only inclinations leaves a
minimum worst-ring discrepancy of at least $4.890$ or $3.783$ errors,
respectively. The combined assignments use the tested speeds and transfer
inner inclinations to the outer gas. Their existence diagnoses sensitivity
to geometry. Independent validation of the anomalous force requires a
prediction made before selecting these inputs. The speed tolerance is a simultaneous Gaussian
marginal-error reference. The geometry boxes are sensitivity choices.
This comparison constrains extrapolation of the compact profile to finite
galactic radii. The source strength and the galaxy parameters in the feasible
solution were selected with the velocities in view. None independently
measures a collar charge~\cite{ophrar,sparc2016}.

\paragraph{Four exterior rings of NGC 2685.}
The published tilted-ring hydrogen columns allow the enclosure cut to be
applied before comparing speeds~\cite{jozsa2685,denheijer2015}. Retaining
95\% of the conservative inner gas column within the adopted height
allowance gives 90.35\% baryonic enclosure. Doubling the downward
column-error allowance gives 88.10\%. With the smooth scale
$8.7504\times10^{-11}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ frozen on SPARC, the four
ring forecasts are $149.2$, $147.5$, $146.1$ and $145.5\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$
in increasing radius. The measured speeds are $143.9$, $139.6$, $146.7$
and $168.8\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$, each assigned $15\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$
uncertainty. The last ring at $32.79\,\mathrm{kpc}$ is 1.56 quoted errors
above the forecast. The four rings share a data cube and galaxy parameters.
With nominal masses and distance, agreement within twice each assigned
error requires a sharp halo edge at or beyond $29.26\,\mathrm{kpc}$.
There is no upper limit from these data. The force estimate uses the compact
baryonic monopole approximation. The columns and inclination sensitivities
are in \path{code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/warped_outer_rings/}.

\paragraph{External gas-rich ultra-diffuse galaxies.}
Fix $a_0=7.603\times10^{-11}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ at the value
estimated from 74 SPARC training galaxies above. For the six gas-rich
ultra-diffuse galaxies of Mancera Pi\~na et al.~\cite{mancera2019},
the measured baryonic masses then predict limiting circular speeds
$(G M_b a_0)^{1/4}$ of $58.0$--$69.3\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$.
The reported circular speeds are $19$--$37\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$
(Fig.~\ref{fig:udg-transfer}). The median predicted-to-measured ratio
is $2.27$. Even at the lower endpoint of the training bootstrap range,
decreasing each log mass and increasing each speed by twice its
corresponding quoted uncertainty leaves all six measured speeds
below prediction. These input ranges have no joint confidence
assignment. The sample was published for its unusually slow rotation
and constrains transfer to these galaxies. Comparing the limiting
law with measurements at approximately $8$--$18$~kpc assumes the
deep exterior profile holds at those finite radii.

In AGC~114905 deep optical imaging gives an inclination of
$31\pm2$ degrees, and the updated stars-and-gas mass is
$(1.47\pm0.14)\times10^9\,M_\odot$~\cite{mancera2024}.
Using the local radial force of the observed disc, the transferred
law gives $v_{\rm circ}^2=v_{\rm bar}^2+\sqrt{R v_{\rm bar}^2 a_0}$.
All five measured rings meet the specified acceleration cut.
At $9.95$~kpc the baryonic speed is $22.57\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$,
the predicted speed is $62.91\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$, and the
pressure-corrected H\,I speed is $26.94\pm1.83\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$.
The outer discrepancy is $19.65$ plotted speed-error widths, or
$18.15$ at the lower training-scale endpoint. At the authors'
alternative distance and inclination from their baryons-only fit,
the directly plotted photometric force at $10.13$~kpc predicts
$60.25\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$ against $24.25\pm1.83\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$.
Both reductions use the same H\,I observations. The quoted residual
units condition on the adopted geometry and mass model. They exclude
shared inclination and force uncertainties and inter-ring covariance.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\IfFileExists{code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/udg_transfer/comparison.pdf}
  {\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/udg_transfer/comparison.pdf}}
  {\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{../code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/udg_transfer/comparison.pdf}}
\caption{Transfer from SPARC to gas-rich ultra-diffuse galaxies.
Left, the six measured circular speeds with their reported errors and
the limiting speeds calculated from the independent baryonic masses.
Right, the resolved circular speed of AGC~114905 at its optically
measured inclination and the radial prediction using the measured disc
force. The band varies only the SPARC training scale over its
galaxy-bootstrap interval. Observations from
Refs.~\cite{mancera2019,mancera2024}.}
\label{fig:udg-transfer}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{Radial acceleration.}
On fixed absolute subsets \(g_{\rm bar}<f a_{\rm ref}\), with the
comparison-only value
\(a_{\rm ref}=1.2\times10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}\) fixed before fitting,
the density profile predicts the total acceleration
\(g_{\rm obs}=g_{\rm bar}+\sqrt{g_{\rm bar}a_0}\). The fixed-exponent
diagnostic gives
\[
a_0=0.843\times10^{-10},\quad 0.875\times10^{-10},\quad 0.924\times10^{-10}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}
\]
for \(f=0.3\), \(0.1\), and \(0.03\), with 1519, 960, and 203 points in 141,
115, and 40 galaxies and rms scatter between \(0.14\) and \(0.17\) dex. Fitting
an anomalous power inside the total model, using every retained point rather
than selecting on the sign of the noisy excess, gives exponents \(0.454\),
\(0.461\), and \(0.239\). Galaxy-cluster bootstrap intervals contain
\(1/2\) for the first two cuts; the deepest cut is sparse and its interval is
wide. The computation uses equal retained-point weighting and does not
marginalize distance, inclination, stellar-population, or correlated
rotation-curve uncertainties, so it is a diagnostic rather than a full
measurement likelihood.

\paragraph{Tully--Fisher and matched channels.}
The 123 quality-selected galaxies with positive flat velocity give
\(a_0=v_f^4/(GM_b)=1.55\times10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}\), with
\(0.26\) dex scatter, for \(M_b=0.5L_{[3.6]}+1.33M_{\rm HI}\).
The ordinary least-squares exponent is \(3.75\pm0.10\), placing the
fixed exponent \(4\) at \(+2.66\sigma\) in this diagnostic. Catalogued
flat speeds retain a baryonic contribution at finite radius and need not
measure the anomalous speed entering the theorem.

A matched comparison uses
\(v_A^2=v_{\rm obs}^2-v_{\rm bar}^2\) at the outermost radius,
\(g_{\rm bar}<0.1a_{\rm ref}\), and excludes nonzero bulge components
without separate luminosities. On the resulting 97-galaxy set the speed
and radial channels give respectively \(0.769\) and
\(0.853\times10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}\). Their paired log-ratio
is \(-0.045\) dex with 95 percent galaxy-bootstrap interval
\([-0.117,+0.026]\). For comparison, the mixed flat-speed/radial analysis
has interval \([0.180,0.317]\) dex and normalization intervals
\([0.730,1.037]\) and \([1.405,1.723]\times10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}\).
The matched construction changes radius, subtraction, sample and combination
together; it does not isolate one cause for the discrepancy. It is consistent
with one fitted constant on seen data, not a physical likelihood verdict.

\paragraph{Galaxies held out of calibration.}
Five folds assigned by galaxy name keep every radius of one galaxy in a
single fold. On 115 galaxies and 960 radii, the conditional deep profile
gave a root-mean-square error in total acceleration of 0.151 dex, with
equal weight per galaxy. A training-fitted multiplier on baryonic
gravity gave 0.175 dex, and baryons alone gave 0.693 dex. The empirical
radial acceleration relation also gave 0.151 dex. The paired difference
between the conditional and empirical laws was $-0.00046$ dex with a
fixed-prediction galaxy-bootstrap interval $[-0.00203,+0.00117]$.
These retrospective splits test transfer across galaxies. Neither
catalogue measures the modular charge independently.

\paragraph{Finite collar spacing against resolved rotation and lensing.}
\label{par:empirical-cut-spacing}
The arithmetic-progression construction predicts an individual enclosed
mass that departs from the smooth profile by at most one cut amplitude.
As a specified galaxy model set $N_s(R)=\lfloor R/s+\phi\rfloor$ with
one uniform phase $\phi$ per galaxy and common spacing $s$. On the
local-force disk prescription multiply the smooth anomalous squared
speed by $s N_s(R)/R$. The disk prescription extends the compact-source
theorem. A shared phase fixes the counts on every ring; integrating it
exactly gives a mixture with breakpoints at the measured fractional
radii. The average multiplier equals one at every radius.

Using all positive-error rings in the deep cut with quality at most two
and inclination at least $30^\circ$ gives 1,240 measurements in 121
SPARC galaxies. The conditional linear-speed likelihood uses published
ring errors and fits independent ring scatter and common galaxy scatter.
All five galaxy-held-out training folds select $s=0.5$ kpc from a
ten-value grid including the smooth limit. The full-sample fit gives
$a_0=8.37\times10^{-11}\,{\rm m\,s^{-2}}$ and fractional scatter
$0.049$ per ring and $0.162$ per galaxy. The held mean log predictive
density gain over the smooth profile is $0.470$ nats per galaxy
(fixed-prediction galaxy-bootstrap interval $[0.092,0.895]$).
A smooth comparison allowing additional inner-ring scatter proportional
to inverse radius reduces that difference to $0.052$ nats with interval
$[-0.481,0.735]$. The score improves in four of the five held folds. The bootstrap
resamples held galaxies at fixed predictions and therefore does not
propagate new training fits or a fresh choice of spacing. The
small-radius scatter comparison was chosen after examining the spacing
fit. These retrospective comparisons condition on the catalogue disk
force and model the speed uncertainties. The spacing has no independent
measurement in the source.

Applied at $9.95$ kpc in the fiducial AGC~114905 reduction, the fitted
model predicts a mean speed of at least $62.97$ km/s for every phase;
the reported speed is $26.94\pm1.83$ km/s. At fixed fitted scale and
baryonic force the one-cut bound requires $s\geq8.77$ kpc even to reach
the upper two-error speed with the deterministic mean. With the fitted common scatter, ring scatter
and plotted measurement error, the conditional lower-tail probability
for this single outer speed is $0.00036$. The comparison was motivated
by the published slow rotation. This probability has no sample-selection
or population significance attached to it.

The gas distribution itself tests the exterior assumption. At the
fiducial distance of 78.7 Mpc, integrate the published gas surface
density of AGC~114905~\cite{mancera2024} with the cylindrical area
measure $2\pi R\,\dd R$. The outer speed point encloses $66.9\%$ of
the fitted gas profile, including its outer extrapolation. Gas supplies
$94\%$ of the reported baryonic mass. Even assigning every star to
the interior yields at most $68.9\%$ baryonic enclosure at the nominal
parameters. A sphere of the same radius contains no more mass than
this cylinder. The fitted gas profile reaches $90\%$ cylindrical
enclosure at 14.30 kpc. These quantities use photometry and gas
surface density independently of the speed residual.

The central outer-ring excess $v^2-v_b^2$ is $6.26\%$ of the least
value from the fitted disk prescription. The equivalent spherical
excess mass $R(v^2-v_b^2)/G$ is $0.50$ billion solar masses, against
a predicted minimum of $7.99$ billion. Keeping $v\sin i$ fixed would
require $i\simeq12.7^\circ$ at the minimum speed; the independent
photometric estimate is $31\pm2^\circ$. This projection check neglects
changes in pressure support and disk geometry. The authors' second
reduction of the same gas data gives $24.25\pm1.83$ km/s at 10.13 kpc,
against a minimum of $61.20$ km/s at that geometry.

Even a spherical exterior leaves the interior acceleration undetermined.
Let $M_A(r)=Cr$ for $r\ge R_s$ with $C,R_s>0$. Inside $R_s$, the
profiles $C R_s(r/R_s)^3$ and $C R_s(r/R_s)^5$ both match the
exterior continuously and have finite nonnegative density. The first
gives four times the acceleration of the second at $R_s/2$. An
interior disk prediction therefore requires an independent relation
between the extended baryonic distribution and the proposed collar
stress. The measured galaxy constrains such a relation. It cannot
decide the compact asymptote at an unmeasured radius.

The same rotation-fitted parameters can be transferred to the sixteen
early-type galaxies with exterior H~I measurements described above.
Select the orbit by its baryonic enclosure, radius beyond five effective
radii and point-mass acceleration at most
$1.2\times10^{-11}\,{\rm m\,s^{-2}}$. At nominal geometry, with the
catalogued baryonic mass treated as compact, NGC~3838 alone qualifies
at $90\%$ enclosure. Its allowed phases predict $136.8$--$138.3$ km/s,
against $159\pm14$ km/s. At $80\%$ enclosure, NGC~2685 gives
$146.8$--$148.0$ against $144\pm10$ km/s, and NGC~6798 gives
$186.2$--$187.5$ against $190\pm8$ km/s. None of these galaxies
enters the rotation fit. The predicted ranges vary the cut phase at
fixed mass and distance. The acceleration scale itself comes from the
local-force extension fitted on disks. This comparison tests transfer of
that normalization to compact exteriors; a measurement of the physical
cut statistics would provide an independent normalization. Figure~\ref{fig:gas-enclosure-transfer}
compares this selection with the measured radial domain of the dwarf.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\IfFileExists{code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/cut_spacing/comparison.pdf}
  {\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/cut_spacing/comparison.pdf}}
  {\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{../code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/cut_spacing/comparison.pdf}}
\caption{Left: integrated fiducial gas surface density of AGC~114905;
the points locate the radii of the measured speeds. Right: the three
early-type galaxies passing the deep exterior cuts at $80\%$ enclosure.
The percentages give conservative lower bounds on baryonic enclosure,
rounded down. The phase ranges condition on the rotation calibration,
mass and geometry. The measured intervals show published speed errors.}
\label{fig:gas-enclosure-transfer}
\end{figure}

For a compact spherical source with phase independent of lens selection
and weights, the mean enclosed mass is linear in $R$ at every spacing.
Weak shear, including an annular average, is a linear projection of the
mass distribution, so the phase-averaged prediction at fixed $a_0$ is
identical to the smooth one. At the rotation-fitted normalization the
six bins wholly above the published isolation threshold give $\chi^2=44.37$ for KiDS-bright and $17.07$
for GAMA with their separate published covariances, bin-centre
evaluation and zero slip. The exact phase mixture, comparison fits and
input assumptions are in the \texttt{cut\_spacing} study of the
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime}{galaxy data comparison}.

\paragraph{An independent weak-lensing observable.}
Transfer the primary SPARC radial fit
$a_0=8.75\times10^{-11}\,{\rm m\,s^{-2}}$ to the isolated
galaxy shear stacks of Brouwer et al.\ \cite{brouwer2021}, with no
lensing fit. For a compact spherical baryonic source, the surface
density contrast is $M_b/(\pi R^2)$. Projecting the $1/r^2$ anomalous
density of Eq.~\eqref{eq:profile}, with the usual weak-field
light-deflection law and negligible gravitational slip, gives
\[
 4G\,\Delta\Sigma(R)=\tfrac4\pi g_b(R)+\sqrt{a_0 g_b(R)} .
\]
Using the released multiplicative corrections and full covariance on
seven bin centres per stack in
$10^{-13}\le g_b<1.2\times10^{-11}\,{\rm m\,s^{-2}}$, the fixed
profile gives $\chi^2=71.1$ (KiDS-bright) and $16.7$ (GAMA).
The lowest included bin straddles the KiDS isolation floor. On the
six bins wholly above it, KiDS-bright gives $40.7$ for the fixed profile.
Compact baryons alone on all seven bins give $739.7$ and $58.0$. The cross shear against
zero gives $4.3$ and $6.4$ respectively. Rebinning on the published hot-gas mass estimate gives $43.0$ for
KiDS-bright under the same compact-baryon approximation. Extended gas
would require its own surface-density projection. The frozen projection
underpredicts six of seven primary KiDS-bright bins. The stacks share
source data and their statistics are reported separately. These
comparisons assume a pointlike baryonic lens, evaluate the profile at
the reported bin centres and condition on the catalogue mass scales
and covariance. They expose tension in this particular relativistic
extension. The finite theorem does not fix gravitational slip.

One diagnostic allows an arbitrary signed compact-core coefficient
on the six isolated bin centres. Projecting out the baryonic-acceleration
column with the published covariance gives an outer amplitude of
$1.730\pm0.123$ times the frozen SPARC amplitude for KiDS-bright.
At the frozen amplitude the minimum $\chi^2$ over all compact coefficients
is $37.06$ in five residual dimensions. If both coefficients vary, the
minimum is $1.80$ in four residual dimensions and the fitted compact
coefficient is negative. This tests the bin-centre and fixed-mass
prescription. Pair weighting and baryonic geometry affect the inference.

A shape test eliminates two constant normalizations. For common
within-bin sampling across logarithmic bins, the projected density
divided by the square root of the bin-centre acceleration must be flat
or increasing. Across the six fully isolated bins the KiDS-bright
low-minus-high contrast has the opposite sign at $2.44$ statistical
standard errors. With uniform sky-area weighting and a common mass
shift within $\pm0.2$ dex, the best sampled $\chi^2$ is 27.10.
Allowing each bin independent weights within its published edges
lowers the statistic to 4.93. The data release supplies neither those
optimizing weights nor a direct measurement of the slip of these
outer haloes. A discriminating comparison needs the actual
lens--source weights and matched orbital and lensing measurements.
The archived \href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime}{rotation and shear calculations} include the catalogue provenance,
selection, source units and covariance.

\paragraph{Galaxy type and a finite radial-pressure test.}
Brouwer et al.\ released the cross-covariance between galaxy types
for their Figure~8 excess surface density profiles. Six full bins satisfy
the sample isolation and deep-acceleration cuts. Project the early-minus-late
tangential excess surface density on the outer profile calibrated on SPARC
using the full covariance. At the published bin centres the contrasts
are $0.586\pm0.121$ by colour and $0.467\pm0.122$ by S\'ersic index.
The corresponding cross-component projections are $-0.016$ and $-0.068$.
The two splits reuse galaxies. A separate bin-edge test holds the nominal
compact baryons fixed and allows a shared nonnegative square-root envelope
and arbitrary positive source--lens pair weights within each bin. In
place of $Y_{\rm high}-Y_{\rm low}$ it projects
$Y_{\rm high}-kY_{\rm low}$ with its own covariance weights, where
$k=\sqrt{b_{\rm upper}/b_{\rm lower}}$. It exceeds its common-envelope
upper bound by $1.51$ and $0.50$ statistical standard errors for the two
splits. The first statistic quantifies the type dependence at bin centres.
The second tests how much a common envelope can vary with pair sampling.
Type-dependent circumgalactic gas can also affect these profiles.
Neither statistic measures local collar pressure.

In units $G=c=1$, consider a static spherical anomalous stress
conserved separately through its outer transition. If $p_r(r_0)<0$,
$p_r(R_t)=0$, $\rho\ge0$, $\rho+p_r\ge0$, $\Phi'\ge0$ and
$p_t\le\rho$, conservation gives
\[
 (r^2p_r)'=2rp_t-r^2(\rho+p_r)\Phi'\le2r\rho .
\]
Hence $r_0^2 |p_r(r_0)|\le2\int_{r_0}^{R_t}r\rho\,dr$ and
$\Delta m=4\pi\int_{r_0}^{R_t}r^2\rho\,dr
\ge2\pi r_0^3|p_r(r_0)|$. In the scale-free weak-field interior,
$m_A=hr$ and $p_r=w_r h/(4\pi r^2)$. Where the same scale-free
projection applies, the lens excess per unit orbital excess is
$q=(2+w_r)/[2(1+w_r)]$. Assigning the entire red-stack bin-centre
value $q=1.413\pm0.080$ to this mechanism would require
$w_r\simeq-0.452$ and at least $0.226\,m_A(r_0)$ in the transition.
This cost assumes smooth matching without a surface layer. Neither the
pressure nor its outer transition was measured in these galaxies.
The source and both covariance calculations are in
\path{code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/pressure_transfer/}.

The three-guide material experiment gives a microscopic check on a
different step in the proposed explanation. A change of the joint
corner probabilities toward equal weights raises the entropy of the three-guide records
at fixed one- and two-guide marginals. Its leading occupation response
is an octupole. The total outward current has exactly zero derivative
at every enclosing radius. Thus a change visible only in joint records
can carry an angular signal without adding any monopole to the
transport measured by these observers. Obtaining a galaxy-scale source
requires an independent positive active stress and its gravitational
coupling on the same physical regions. Local record density in the
source enters this proposal. Distance from the telescope enters the
observation, and provides no such coupling.

\paragraph{Covariance and nuisance sensitivity.}
A separate penalized Gaussian objective profiles disk mass-to-light,
distance and inclination, and scans an equal-correlation family
\(\rho\in\{0,0.2,0.4,0.6\}\). A log-normal mass-to-light prior centered
at \(0.5\) is a penalty rather than an auxiliary-data likelihood.
The fixed catalogue-frame mean is
\(v_{\rm model}\sin i/\sin i_{\rm cat}\). Transforming observations and
errors by \(s(i)=\sin i_{\rm cat}/\sin i\) also requires the density
Jacobian \(s(i)^n\), which cancels the Gaussian factor \(s(i)^{-n}\).

On the \(f=0.1\) subset the optimum shifts from \(0.804\) to
\(0.582\times10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}\), or \(0.140\) dex,
as \(\rho\) increases from zero to \(0.6\). The data quadratic form per
nominal fixed-nuisance degree of freedom lies between \(3.6\) and \(16.4\);
the family has no intrinsic scatter and understates observed variation.
Its threshold sets are uncalibrated sensitivity contours, not confidence
intervals. The paired comparison contains zero at every scanned setting,
but channel-B nuisances are held at full-sample estimates while channel A
is refitted, so it is only partially paired. Independent replay and synthetic
controls verify the subtraction, fixed observation frame, covariance algebra
and component enumeration~\cite{ophrar}. A physical test requires a
calibrated covariance and intrinsic-scatter likelihood.

\paragraph{Shear from stationary transport of records.}
An independent lensing shape prediction follows on the stationary,
homogeneous transport branch if its positive quadratic record score
$q=\sum_e v_e^2$ supplies the gravitating density. Away from record
injectors the continuum transport fields obey
$\nabla^2v_e=\mu^2v_e$, so $\nabla^2 q\ge0$. Projection through a complete
source-free annulus with vanishing end gradients gives
$\nabla_\perp^2\Sigma\ge0$. For weak thin-lens density optics, put
$S=\Delta\Sigma$, $t=R^{-2}$, and $x=\ln R$.
Azimuthal averaging yields
$4t^2 S_{tt}=-\partial_x^2\langle\Sigma\rangle_\phi\le0$.
A compact baryonic core adds a linear term in $t$.
Across all 20 three-bin comparisons in each of the KiDS-bright and
GAMA stacks of Brouwer et al.~\cite{brouwer2021}, the largest
departures from concavity are 0.40 and 1.46 standard errors against
a one-sided within-stack 95\% threshold of 2.81. This retrospective
test needs a common mixture of lenses across bins and independently
source-free annuli. It tests shape at any positive normalization;
the amplitude and the physical conversion to mass remain independent
inputs.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/record_shear}{Derivation, survey covariance and finite-bin tests.}}

\paragraph{A matched orbit and shear prediction.}
Let $u(R)=v_{\rm obs}^2(R)-v_{\rm bar}^2(R)$ and
$D(R)=\Delta\Sigma_{\rm obs}(R)-\Delta\Sigma_{\rm bar}(R)$
be the excess squared circular speed and surface-density contrast
of the same galaxy. Suppose the excess has $\rho=A/r^2$ outside
$R_0$, extends along the line of sight, is spherical and has negligible
gravitational slip. For $R>R_0$, the compensating annular lens statistic
of Baldauf et al.\ \cite{baldauf2010} and the orbital mass difference obey
\[
 4G\,[R^2D(R)-R_0^2D(R_0)] = R u(R)-R_0u(R_0).
\]
Indeed $d(Ru)/dR=4\pi GA$, while
$d(R^2D)/dR=-R^2\Sigma'(R)=\pi A$ since $\Sigma(R)=\pi A/R$.
Differencing removes an arbitrary spherical mass confined inside $R_0$.
Neither $A$ nor $a_0$ is needed for the equality. If the outer density
cuts off sharply at $L$, then $\Sigma(R)=2A\arccos(R/L)/R$ and the
ratio of the two sides for $(R_0,R)=(L/4,L/2)$ is $1.014799$.
Direct numerical projection of spherical shells recovers both predictions
with two different compact cores. The comparison requires matched physical
radii, a common galaxy sample, measured pair weights and baryonic
subtraction, including extended disks and gas. A particle halo with
the same density and metric response obeys the same relation.

\paragraph{Spectra and lensing in the same galaxies.}
The Sloan Lens Advanced Camera for Surveys sample provides stellar velocity dispersions and
lensed arcs in the same galaxies. With a spherical isothermal total
field, negligible slip and isotropic de Vaucouleurs stellar tracers,
the measured half-light radii, seeing and aperture fix the conversion
from stellar dispersion to circular speed. Using the spectra to predict
the Einstein angles of 53 single early-type lenses gives a median
predicted-to-observed ratio of $1.101$ and a median absolute fractional
error of $16.1\%$. The arcs cover $1.14$--$6.92$ kpc.
These are measurements of the total field in galaxy interiors. Stellar
orbital anisotropy and radial profile variation enter the inference
\cite{bolton2008}.

The 35 eligible lenses outside the weak-shear sample give a median
observed-to-predicted angle ratio of $0.855$. Using this factor as one
common rescaling raises the median absolute error on 18 held inner
lenses from $11.6\%$ to $15.8\%$. For the 22-lens outer stack, extending
the spectroscopic circular speeds flat and applying the same factor
gives central surface-density contrasts of $44.02$ and $25.19$ in
$h\,M_\odot\,{\rm pc}^{-2}$. This
increases the two-bin discrepancy from $\chi^2=3.23$ to $4.41$.
The calibration and held galaxies are disjoint. The comparison tests
the transfer of a single multiplicative correction within this
spherical stellar and lensing model.

The same experiment extends outward in 22 lenses with stellar spectra,
stellar-population masses and a published weak-shear stack
\cite{gavazzi2007,auger2009}. At $164.5$ and $287.5\,h^{-1}$ kpc the
tangential surface-density contrasts are $52\pm17$ and $60\pm17$ in
$h\,M_\odot\,{\rm pc}^{-2}$. At the bin centres, the SPARC-calibrated
compact-baryon profile gives $21.21$ and $11.60$ using Chabrier
stellar masses or $29.08$ and $15.70$ using Salpeter masses. Continuing
the inner spectroscopic circular speeds with an isothermal total field
gives $51.51$ and $29.47$. The bin-centre calculation assumes uniform
sky sampling and weights lenses by squared mean lensing efficiency.
The cross component, whose expectation vanishes for axisymmetric lenses,
is $52\pm17$ and $34\pm17$. Its large value limits interpretation of the
tangential residuals. Allowing each stellar mass up to its quoted
Salpeter value plus one error and the upper rotation-scale endpoint,
the outermost bin admits an upper bound of $58.02$ if the lens-specific
mean source efficiencies remain calibrated under the bin weights.
With only the individual efficiency range $[0,1]$, the upper bound
is $96.42$. These bounds include the allowed within-bin radii and
reduced-shear correction. Even the smaller bound is only $0.12$ quoted
errors below the observation. The inner spectra and outer weak shear share the
lenses, but they sample different radii. Measuring outer orbital tracers
in the lensing annuli and recovering the actual lens--source weights would
permit the amplitude-free comparison above.

\paragraph{A pressure measurement from orbits and light.}
Consider the static spherical weak-field branch in physical units.
Let $m(r)$ be the mass in the radial metric coefficient,
$M_o=rv_c^2/G$ the circular-orbit mass and $M_w$ the mass that
deflects light. To leading order the radial Einstein equation gives
$M_o=m+4\pi r^3p_r/c^2$ and weak deflection gives
$M_w=(m+M_o)/2$~\cite{pressureOptics}. A spherical shell at $r>R$
places the fraction $1-\sqrt{1-R^2/r^2}$ of its mass inside the
cylinder of radius $R$. Integrating by parts gives
$M_{\rm cyl}(R)=\int_0^{\pi/2} M(R\sec t)\cos t\,dt$ for either
profile, provided the outer boundary term vanishes. Projecting
$M_o-M_w$ and substituting $z=R\tan t$ gives the signed pressure column
\begin{equation}
 P(R)\equiv\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}p_r\!\left(\sqrt{R^2+z^2}\right)dz
 =c^2\,[\overline\Sigma_o(<R)-\overline\Sigma_w(<R)],
 \qquad \overline\Sigma=\frac{M_{\rm cyl}}{\pi R^2}.
 \label{eq:radial-pressure-column}
\end{equation}
Thus an optical excess over the projected orbital mass measures a
negative column of total radial pressure. The orbital projection
requires the force along the ray. Since
$d\overline\Sigma/dR=-2\Delta\Sigma/R$, the contrast
$P(R_1)-P(R_2)=2c^2\int_{R_1}^{R_2}(\Delta\Sigma_o-\Delta\Sigma_w)\,dR/R$
cancels an additive mass sheet. Absolute ring masses depend on
independent lens calibration and external convergence.

The 53 lens galaxies permit a conditional test with one spectrum and
one ring per object. Assume steady nonrotating spherical tracers with
deprojected de Vaucouleurs luminosity density $\nu$. Their radial
and one-component tangential second moments per unit volume,
$j_r$ and $j_t$, obey $(r^2j_r)'=2rj_t-r\nu v_c^2$. Positivity,
with $r^2j_r\to0$ at infinity, implies
$0\le j_r\le J(r)=r^{-2}\int_r^\infty s\nu v_c^2\,ds$.
For a circular fibre of radius $a$, set
$u=\sqrt{\max(0,1-a^2/r^2)}$ and $B=1-u-(1-u^3)/3$.
Integrating the line-of-sight stellar moment over the accepted
solid angles, substituting for $j_t$ and integrating $j'_r$ by
parts gives its luminosity-weighted numerator divided by $4\pi$,
\[
 \frac12\int_0^\infty r^2 B\nu v_c^2\,dr
 +\frac{a^2}{2}\int_0^\infty j_r(\sqrt{a^2+z^2})\,dz .
\]
The circular and radial stellar moment solutions ($j_r=0$ and $j_r=J$)
bound every radial variation of stellar orbital anisotropy. Atmospheric
blurring preserves the bound because a centred circular fibre with
Gaussian seeing has a decreasing radial acceptance, a positive mixture
of circular top hats. We use the 1.5 arcsec fibre radius and a common
1.4 arcsec seeing FWHM. A nonnegative stellar distribution function
can tighten these bounds.

Continue the square-root acceleration calibrated on SPARC into the
stellar body and along the ray, with $v_A^2=\sqrt{GM_*(<r)a_0}$,
$v_*^2=GM_*(<r)/r$ and $a_0=8.7504\times10^{-11}\,{\rm m\,s^{-2}}$.
Light fixes the shape of $M_*$ and the bounded spectral moment constrains
its normalization. If stars lens at the ordinary coupling and $q_A$
is the anomalous optical weight, the ring mass is the projected stellar mass
plus $q_A$ times the projected anomalous orbital mass. Allowing each
measured stellar dispersion two quoted errors yields the following
intersections of the individual necessary intervals~\cite{bolton2008,auger2009}.
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lcc}
\hline
Sample and stellar mass constraint & Isothermal ellipsoid & Light traces mass\\
\hline
53 lenses, free stellar normalization & $[0.955,1.441]$ & $[0.925,1.342]$\\
18 lenses, stellar-population bracket & $[1.038,1.233]$ & $[0.943,1.174]$\\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The columns use two reconstructions of the same arcs. For the 18
matched galaxies, the stellar mass ranges from the Chabrier estimate
minus one quoted logarithmic error to the Salpeter estimate plus one.
These population brackets set both active endpoints. For J2300+0022
the isothermal-ellipsoid ring gives $P/c^2$ between $-1872$ and $-52$
in $M_\odot\,{\rm pc}^{-2}$ at 4.534 kpc. The alternative reconstruction
gives $[-1796,+79]$ at 4.424 kpc. Reducing the ellipsoid ring radii
by $2\%$ also admits unit weight across the 18 galaxies. These
intervals describe parameter boxes with no assigned joint confidence
level. The central continuation, spherical light profile and
external mass calibration enter the comparison. Assigning a measured
$P$ to the collar requires an independent prediction of its sign and
size after ordinary stresses and foreground mass are accounted for.
The per-lens values and sensitivity calculations accompany the
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/aperture_virial}{aperture calculation}.

\paragraph{Pressure between two rings of the same galaxy.}
SDSS~J0946+1006 lenses two sources at spectroscopic redshifts 0.609
and 2.035. The rings at $1.43\pm0.01$ and $2.07\pm0.02$ arcsec sample
the foreground galaxy at 5.115 and 7.404 kpc for the stated flat
distance calibration~\cite{jackpot2008,jackpot2021}. Consider their
aligned circular two-plane interpretation. Write $C_i$ for the critical
surface density of the primary lens for source $i$, $S$ for a constant
external surface density on the primary plane, and $b_{12}$ for the
reduced radial bend of the outer ray by the intervening source.
The equations are $\overline\Sigma_{w1}+S=C_1$ and
$\overline\Sigma_{w2}+S=C_2(1-b_{12}/\theta_2)$. For a centered
positive secondary with the outer ray on the same side of the axis,
$b_{12}\ge0$. Equation~\eqref{eq:radial-pressure-column} therefore gives
\[
 \frac{P(R_1)-P(R_2)}{c^2}
 \le \overline\Sigma_o(<R_1)-\overline\Sigma_o(<R_2)-(C_1-C_2).
\]
The primary sheet cancels. An exterior spherical mass shell places
more mass per unit aperture area inside the outer cylinder than
inside the inner one. Indeed, for $r\ge R_2$ its fractional
contribution divided by $\pi R^2$ is
$[\pi r^2(1+\sqrt{1-R^2/r^2})]^{-1}$, increasing with $R$.
Consequently, at fixed interior orbital mass profile, deleting all
mass outside $R_2$ gives an upper bound over every nonnegative
exterior orbital mass density.

Use the two observed stellar light components~\cite{jackpot2012} and
the preceding square-root force law only out to $R_2$. Over the
individual Chabrier stellar-mass and light-profile error ranges,
both quoted ring errors and the SPARC bootstrap scale interval,
the truncated orbital contrast is at most
$941\,M_\odot\,{\rm pc}^{-2}$. The critical-density difference is
$1055.58\,M_\odot\,{\rm pc}^{-2}$. Hence the inner pressure column
must lie below the outer by at least $114\,c^2 M_\odot\,{\rm pc}^{-2}$
on this branch. To obtain a nonnegative pressure contrast by
adding a positive spherical orbital source requires at least
$1.72\times10^{10}M_\odot$ inside $R_2$, since its maximum weight
per unit mass is $(R_1^{-2}-R_2^{-2})/\pi$. Doubling both ring
errors gives a minimum contrast deficit of 76 in the same surface-density
units. Including the alternative inner-ring fits of
Ref.~\cite{bolton2008} lowers that bound to 10.

The nominal Salpeter component masses admit an explicit zero-pressure
alternative if the square-root continuation extends along the ray.
It requires $S=0.145\,C_1$ and $b_{12}=0.108$ arcsec, equivalent to
an isothermal secondary of dispersion $80.8\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$.
The secondary luminosity gives $59\pm13\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$ on a
Tully--Fisher calibration~\cite{jackpot2008}. In the same Salpeter
orbital field an isotropic Jeans model for the two measured light
components gives a primary fibre dispersion of $259.4\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$,
against the independent spectrum at $263\pm21\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$.
The nominal Chabrier field gives $196.9\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$ under
isotropy. The observed arcs are
partial and the two fitted stellar components have projected axis
ratios 0.66 and 0.71. Departures from circular lensing, additional
lens planes and the stellar mass calibration affect the pressure
inference. The pressure contrast constrains a proposed total stress;
its assignment to a collar requires independent material data.
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/double_ring_pressure}{Two-plane equations, interval bounds and observational inputs.}}

The optical contrast can also be read from an elliptical fit to both
sets of Hubble arcs. Collett and Auger fit the foreground mass, external
shear and a secondary deflector~\cite{jackpot2014}. Integrating their
primary convergence in the same two \emph{circular} apertures gives
$\Delta\bar\Sigma_w=1165.8\,M_\odot\,{\rm pc}^{-2}$ at the median
parameters and discovery radii. Over the rectangle of their marginal
axis-ratio, slope and lens-scale ranges, the optical contrast exceeds
the spherical Chabrier orbital upper bound by at least
$149\,M_\odot\,{\rm pc}^{-2}$ with the quoted radius errors, and by
at least $61\,M_\odot\,{\rm pc}^{-2}$ over the extended radius
ranges. This compares two aperture means within the fitted optical
profile. The parameter rectangle has no assigned joint confidence
level. A radial-pressure interpretation requires a spherical effective
stress description of both aperture measurements.

\paragraph{A resolved stellar test of the orbital field.}
The same galaxy has independent MUSE stellar spectra~\cite{jackpot2024}.
At the central bin the observed root-mean-square line-of-sight speed is
$276\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$. The nominal spherical isotropic Jeans predictions,
after $0.5$ arcsec seeing, are $208$ and $278\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$ for
the Chabrier and Salpeter mass scales. A second stellar bin centred at
$1.01$ arcsec limits the freedom to increase the central speed by adding
gravity beyond $S=2.07$ arcsec. A spherical Jeans shell at $s\ge S$
contributes the nonnegative kernel
$\sqrt{(1-R^2/s^2)_+}$ at sky radius $R$. After Gaussian seeing of
width $\sigma$, that kernel lies between
$1-(x^2+2\sigma^2)/S^2$ and one at projected position $x$.
With an upper reading of $247\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$ in the second bin,
even the two upper Chabrier component masses and upper SPARC acceleration
give a central upper limit of $262\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$ over arbitrary
nonnegative exterior force. The observed central bar extends down to
$270\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$ after outward rounding. Thus the lighter branch
that requires the signed pressure contrast also misses a separate observable
under the adopted assumptions. To cover both bars with an added spherical
force, its enclosed orbital mass equivalent $r\,\delta v_c^2/G$ must
reach at least $8.4\times10^8 M_\odot$ somewhere inside the outer ring.
This follows since the seeing-averaged kernel of any shell is bounded by
the Gaussian probability of its disk, at most $\min(1,s^2/2\sigma^2)$.
The comparison uses fixed published light
scales, a spherical isotropic stellar tracer, and positive weights within
$R\le0.16$ and $0.64\le R\le1.37$ arcsec for the two bins.
These footprints allow for a spaxel and for reading the plotted bin widths.
The two graphical error bars carry no joint significance level here.
Flattening, stellar orbital anisotropy or an extra central mass change
this test. The
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/double_ring_pressure/resolved_kinematics}{archived comparison}
gives the shell bound and the measured stellar bins.

A second telescope tests the stellar-orbit assumption. The Keck/LRIS
central $1.00\times0.21$ arcsec slit measurement gives
$287\pm11\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$ for the dispersion~\cite{jackpot2012};
including the mean velocity leaves the lower endpoint at 276.
Combining this slit with the outer MUSE bin gives a central upper
sum of $265.4\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$ under isotropy. Here both light
scales are fixed, the Chabrier component masses and acceleration may
vary over their quoted ranges, and exterior gravity is free and
attractive. Allow any spherical stellar anisotropy profile
$0\le\beta(r)\le r^2/(r^2+a^2)$, with $\beta=1-\langle v_\theta^2\rangle/
\langle v_r^2\rangle$. The bound stays below 276 for $a\ge3.11$ arcsec,
even when the outer orbits become radial. The calculation uses the
separate $0.8$ arcsec Keck and $0.5$ arcsec MUSE seeing and allows
a $0.03$ arcsec slit-centering error. Using the complete frozen force
and nominal Salpeter stellar masses without adjustment predicts
$272.9\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$ centrally. Three of the 11 signed slit bins
bracket this profile. Seven predictions stay below their observed lower endpoints
even after the centering allowance. Nominal Chabrier gives
$205.0\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$ centrally and falls below all 11 bins.

Stronger radial anisotropy illustrates the ambiguity of two speeds.
An Osipkov--Merritt tracer with $a=0.4$ arcsec, the upper Chabrier
force and a spherical exterior shell of $8\times10^{10}\,M_\odot$
gives $287.3\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$ for Keck and $241.2\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$
at the outer MUSE bin center. In the held central MUSE aperture its
predicted speed exceeds $290\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}}$ throughout the footprint;
the observed upper bar rounds out to 282. This Jeans moment construction
shows how the third aperture discriminates between orbital explanations.
The \href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/double_ring_pressure/resolved_kinematics/slit_anisotropy}{two-instrument calculation}
gives the projection bounds and the uncombined observational errors.

\paragraph{The stress required by the two sightlines.}
Let $L$ be the optical contrast above the upper bound on the truncated
orbital contrast, in surface-density units. Write
$W(r)=2[\sqrt{(r^2-R_1^2)_+}-\sqrt{(r^2-R_2^2)_+}]$.
Integrating $p'_r$ between the two radii encountered at each
line-of-sight position gives $P(R_2)-P(R_1)=\int W p'_r\,dr$.
In a static spherical metric with lapse $e^\nu$, conservation of total
stress gives $p'_r=-(\epsilon+p_r)\nu'+2(p_t-p_r)/r$, where $p_t$
is either tangential pressure and $\epsilon$ is energy density.
For an attractive field $\nu'\ge0$ and nonnegative radial enthalpy
$\epsilon+p_r\ge0$, use $\int W\,dr/r=\pi(R_2-R_1)$ to obtain
\[
 \frac{\int (W/r)(p_t-p_r)\,dr}{c^2\pi(R_2-R_1)}
 \ \ge\ \frac{L}{2\pi(R_2-R_1)} .
\]
The two Chabrier comparisons at the quoted radius errors require this
weighted mean to be at least $0.007570\,M_\odot\,{\rm pc}^{-3}$ for
the aligned arcs and $0.009895\,M_\odot\,{\rm pc}^{-3}$ for the
elliptical optical fit under its spherical pressure interpretation.
Both use the same images and the independently calibrated stellar
force continuation. The bounds extend along the full sightlines.
A static isotropic total stress cannot produce this sign under the
stated conditions. The heavier stellar calibration admits zero pressure.

There is a further test if the total source is collisionless with all
local speeds bounded by $\beta c$. Its two tangential stresses obey
$2p_t\le\beta^2\epsilon$ and $p_r\ge0$, so $m(r)\le M_o(r)$.
Bounding $W/r^3$ above by a nonincreasing function permits the cumulative
comparison $m\le M_o$ in the kinetic integral. For $\beta\le1/\sqrt2$,
the combined exterior orbital shell kernel is then nonpositive, giving
$\beta^2 M_o(R_2)\ge3\sqrt3\,\pi R_1^2 L$. The quoted radius box
allows at most $4.071\times10^{11}\,M_\odot$ of orbital mass inside
$R_2$, even placing both allowed stellar components wholly inside it.
The necessary speed ceilings on the two respective branches are at
least $0.343c$ and $0.392c$. These lower bounds test the kinetic
interpretation of this particular force and optical model. Assigning
the excess to an OPH collar requires its own radial and tangential
stress predictions and the ordinary contributions on the same sightlines.
The \href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/double_ring_pressure/stress_conservation}{projected conservation calculation}
gives the exterior-shell proof and the widened angular bounds.

\paragraph{Where the excess must gravitate in a merger.}
The western component of the Bullet Cluster supplies a position test
independent of a rotation calibration. Consider a total projected
lensing convergence that is a constant sheet plus two circular,
radially nonincreasing contributions about the two X-ray plasma centres.
Every strict local maximum must lie on the segment joining the centres.
Indeed, moving from an exterior point toward its orthogonal projection
onto the segment decreases its distance to each centre. Neither
contribution can decrease along that displacement. The result holds
for arbitrary weights absorbed into the two nonincreasing profiles
and also permits flat plateaux.

From the plasma centres in Table~2 and the western weak-lensing peak
in Section~3 of Clowe et al.~\cite{clowe2006}, the nearest allowed
point is the western plasma centre, $36.61$ arcsec away, or $162$ kpc
at their plate scale. Their bootstrap places the peak about eight
standard deviations from its plasma centre. The distance to the
allowed segment is a central-value comparison. A later free-form
reconstruction using the James Webb Space Telescope finds a western
mass-peak to gas-core offset of about $150$ kpc~\cite{cha2025}.
Thus even overlapping spherical profiles centred on the gas miss the
reported western maximum. A model with separate stellar structure or
transported overlap histories lies outside this test. Its discriminator
is the projected charge field predicted from the merger history before
using the lensing peaks to place it. The static spherical galaxy law
supplies no such transport rule.

The two observed peaks together test independent elliptical profiles
with arbitrary fixed axis ratios and orientations. For a sum of two
nonincreasing profiles centred on the gas, every strict peak away
from the centres must lie where their two quadratic normals are
antiparallel. Along this curve, projection onto any fixed sky direction
can overshoot at most one end of the projected interval between the
centres. In one measured direction, the western peak is $36.475$ arcsec
beyond one end and the eastern peak is at least $21.811$ arcsec beyond
the other~\cite{clowe2006}. With the peaks held fixed, one centre would
have to move at least $96.25$ kpc at the published scale. This bound
uses the nominal peak positions. Including position uncertainties
requires a combined allowance for centres and peaks. A prediction of
projected collar stress from the merger history could be tested against
both displacements. The proof and coordinate reconstruction are in
\path{code/cosmology/rar_deep_regime/merger_peak/}.

\section{Cosmological Scaling}
\label{sec:cosmo}

If the total anomalous charge in a comoving region is conserved,
Theorem~\ref{thm:cold} gives \(\rho_A\propto a^{-3}\): the anomalous medium
has the homogeneous dilution law of a pressureless component. On the
sourced causal order of the finite-source cosmology paper the same statement
reads: the layered read law in comoving coordinates is the flat order for
every scale-factor profile, a comoving cell at layer \(j\) has physical
volume \(a_j^3\) times its comoving volume, and a layer-independent
anomalous charge per comoving cell is the density \(\rho_A\propto a_j^{-3}\)
by definition of that count measure. Conservation of the charge under comoving
strain is the operational input: fixed-volume records of the same \(Q\) do not
select the exponent \(\alpha=0\) of \(H_\alpha(V)=Q(V/V_0)^{-\alpha}\), for which
\(p/\rho=\alpha\)~\cite{ophobservers}.
This does not
derive its pressure perturbations, anisotropic stress, sound speed, or
clustering. Premise~\ref{pre:scale} associates its carrier with galactic
collars but supplies no perturbation dynamics. The abundance \(\Omega_A\),
initial data, transfer functions, and the claim that it clusters with galaxies are not derived here.

A finite example isolates a missing input for the absolute abundance.
In the stationary seam counter, active and inactive cells differ by a fixed
placement probability $u$. An active--inactive seam contributes
$q_u=(zu/3)\operatorname{atanh}(szu/3)$ at comparator contrast $z$ and
pairing fraction $s$. On two separated triangular faces with $L^2$ cells
each, keep the random preparation bit in the record and use independent
conditional seam counters. When half the cells are active on each trial
and each cell has activation probability one half, the background charge is
\[
 Q_{\rm bg}/q_u=3L+\sum_{ij\ {\rm internal}}\Pr(A_i\ne A_j).
\]
Choosing a whole face or alternating adjacent cells attains $3L$ and
$3L^2$ respectively at identical total injection. On the 1,280-cell
carrier ($L=8$), the two homogeneous charges differ eightfold. Adding
the same localized galaxy gives a factor of 7.836 in the total volume
mean. At fixed distances a uniform density difference cancels from both
the periodic peculiar-field source $\rho-\bar\rho$ and the weak-lensing
contrast $\bar\Sigma(<R)-\langle\Sigma(R)\rangle$. The two preparations
therefore give the same nonzero galaxy signals under the same supplied
gravitational conversion. Expansion can distinguish the mean densities.
Predicting that mean requires the joint source law, including the rate of
disagreement across adjacent cells, and its conversion to mass per proper
volume.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/record_abundance}{Finite source construction and control fields.}}

An independently specified screen process provides this pair statistic
on its own material. A scheduled exchange either interchanges the sharp
occupations of its two cells or leaves them in place, with equal
probabilities. Starting with the half-board load of the original screen
experiment, propagating the two-cell occupation products for eleven
scheduled services predicts 43.028, 83.223 and 177.096 mismatched seams
on the 80-, 320- and 1,280-cell boards. In 4,096 new simulated realizations
per board the means were respectively $43.120\pm0.098$, $83.188\pm0.143$
and $177.067\pm0.208$ (one standard error). Replacing the joint law by
products of individual occupation means gives 40.873, 78.443 and 167.599.
On the uniform ensemble with exactly $K$ occupied cells on a three-regular
board with $N$ cells the stationary prediction is $3K(N-K)/(N-1)$.
Using the screen flag to switch on collar placement is an additional
coupling. The screen process predicts boundary counts in native units.
Its physical population and the mass per occupied boundary at cosmological
epochs remain to be determined.

There is a finite upper bound on the clustering of this local
source if each site occupies the same proper volume. In the stationary
independent occupation law with $p=0.16$, assign each cell half the sum
of its three incident disagreements. Its mean is $3p(1-p)$ and its
maximum is $3/2$. Thus even an arbitrarily inhomogeneous rearrangement
gives a local cut density at most $1/[2p(1-p)]=3.721$ times its
homogeneous mean (upper bound rounded up). The ordinary double density
has maximum enhancement $1/p=6.25$. A positive sum of the two has
enhancement at most 6.25 with either mass conversion. In Abell 1689
the lower quoted deprojected mass at a fixed radius of
$1.554\,h^{-1}\,{\rm Mpc}$ gives an enclosed mean density exceeding
107 times the cosmic matter density even at $\Omega_{m,0}=1$. Equal proper volume per site and equal mass per
local record therefore fail together as the source of the cluster
density.\footnote{\href{https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1365-2966.2008.13116.x}{Lemze et al., Table 3},
and \href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/record_abundance}{finite source calculation and observational assumptions}.}


A second bound permits variable proper cell volumes. Assign each cut to its
active endpoint. An aperture with $K$ active ordinary cells owns at most
$3K$ cuts, and the cosmic mean is $3(1-p)$ cuts per active cell.
Let $f$ be the failed-recovery probability of a cut. For a retained flag
the conditional full-erasure score has multiplier $f$, because a
successfully recovered block contributes zero. Assume the same full-erasure
charge per mismatch and positive mass conversion throughout, and let $C$
be the cosmic cold-to-baryon mass ratio. The maximal source-owned ratio is
\[
 \frac{M_{\rm dark}}{M_b}\le
 \frac{C}{1-p}\frac{\bar f_{\rm local}}{\bar f_{\rm cosmic}},
\]
where each mean is over mismatches. Splitting seams between both ends
admits an exterior boundary term. At the Planck base-model calibration
$C=5.32248$, constant recovery caps total mass at $7.34$ times the
ordinary mass (rounded up). At its outer gas rotation point NGC~3741 has
an inferred $4.03\times10^9\,M_\odot$ against
$1.828\times10^8\,M_\odot$ in catalogued stars and helium-corrected gas.
Constant recovery needs another $3.67\times10^8\,M_\odot$ in the
aperture at the adopted distance. With the catalogued baryons alone the
mean local failure fraction must instead reach at least $3.32$ times
the cosmic fraction. This is a necessary contrast under the rotation,
source ownership and mass assumptions. Diffuse baryons or transport
from outside the aperture change the budget. The observations measure
neither recovery fraction.\footnote{\href{https://academic.oup.com/mnras/article/383/2/809/995293}{Begum et al., Table 1},
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.06209}{Planck Collaboration VI, Table 2}, and
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/openwave/interval_echo/row_19/recovery_identity}{flagged recovery and aperture calculation}.}

A concrete access rule tests whether sparser records can generate that
contrast. On the 80-cell stationary screen a neighbouring active cell
can archive the right record at a mismatch. The two original endpoints
are excluded. In the retained-flag instrument a successful route restores
the pair, while a failed route restores the product reference. With $m$
eligible witnesses at homogeneous occupation $p$, the failure probability
is $(1-p)^m$ exactly. Eleven native exchange services preserve the product
occupation law. Independent simulated boards measure both the flags and
the associated source score. With one-step access, reducing $p$ from 0.32
to 0.08 raises the measured score from $[0.00045,0.00091]$ to
$[0.00152,0.00296]$ nats per mismatch. These are simultaneous 99\%
counting intervals conditional on the routed cohorts.

All seams have four one-step witnesses. At the reference $p=0.16$ even
complete local failure gives a gain below $2.009$. With two-step access
half the seams have eleven witnesses and half have twelve. In this
homogeneous family the charge per ordinary active cell is proportional
to $(1-p)[(1-p)^{11}+(1-p)^{12}]/2$. The NGC~3741 inventory and
outer rotation estimate then require $p_{\rm local}\le0.06210$. The
comparison supplies the access rule and the source assignment. It gives
a threshold for an independent measurement of galactic recovery.
Neither the simulated source nor this mass budget supplies the metric
response.\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/openwave/interval_echo/row_19/recovery_identity}{Flagged recovery as a function of the local occupancy.}}

Photometry can bound the number of potential sites per proper volume
without measuring how many are active. With fixed baryonic mass per
occupied site, $\rho_b=m_b n_s p$, so light and size constrain the
product of site density $n_s$ and occupation $p$. In Draco's 291 pc
deprojected half-light sphere the enclosed light is
$1.10\times10^5 L_\odot$. For stellar $V$-band mass-to-light ratio one,
stars alone require $n_s/\bar n_s\ge 27400$ at the adopted cosmic
baryon density and reference occupation 0.16. On two finite screens
of 80 and 320 cells, eight ordinary marks at the same assigned volume
give the same mean ordinary count per ancestral face. The expected
numbers of failed two-step recoveries per ordinary mark are 0.8530 and
2.2422. The ordinary coarse census admits both.

At an assumed stellar mass-to-light ratio of three, including the stated
allowances for neutral and warm ionized gas, the central stellar-velocity
mass estimate at 291 pc requires $p\le0.04871$ on the homogeneous
two-step branch. At that occupation the stars alone imply at least
$1.69\times10^6$ times the cosmic site density. The occupation ceiling
uses the dynamical mass. Predicting the mass from overlap records
requires an independent occupation or physical site density. The
stellar conversion, gas phases and composition of the aperture enter
this bound.\footnote{\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/0908.2995}{Wolf et al., half-light mass and luminosity},
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/astro-ph/0301228}{Gallagher et al., warm gas limits}, and
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/cosmology/record_abundance}{neutral gas spectra, physical inventories and conditional bounds}.}

\paragraph{A cosmological test of the dilution exponent.}
For a separately conserved homogeneous fluid with constant equation
of state $w$, write $\bar\rho_A\propto a^{-n}$. The continuity
equation gives $n=3(1+w)$, so conservation of comoving anomalous
charge predicts $n=3$ with no normalization entering this test.
Thomas, Kopp and Skordis~\cite{thomasGDM} varied the dark equation of
state, sound speed and viscosity in a flat generalized-dark-matter
cosmology. Their 99.7\% credible interval using Planck 2015 primary
spectra, CMB lensing and baryon acoustic oscillations gives
$2.997312<n<3.007140$. Equivalently, a doubling of scale factor
leaves a density fraction between 0.12438 and 0.12524, compared with
the conserved-charge value $1/8$. This precision applies to a
constant exponent for the whole fitted dark component.

For the isotropic midpoint construction, restoring the screen radius
\(L\) gives \(M=15\kappa/(32\pi L)\) in natural units with fixed
ordinary conversion. The rest-ball kernel scales as a length and the
charge of a fractional aperture is unchanged at fixed \(\kappa\).
For screens expanding with \(L\propto a\), write \(K(a)\) for their
total word price per comoving volume, weighted by inverse comoving
screen size. The mean gravitational density would scale as \(K(a)/a^4\).
Fixed \(K\) gives \(1/16\) of the density after doubling \(a\),
outside the quoted range. The same fit requires \(K(2a)/K(a)\) to
lie between \(1.9901\) and \(2.0038\) on this constant-power branch.
Conserved comoving numbers of bound screens with fixed physical
radii and masses give \(1/8\) instead. These conditional relations
identify the required cosmic record production or mass conservation.
The finite angular refinement does not measure either cosmic history.

Allowing eight independent equation-of-state bins tests a different
departure. With vanishing sound speed and viscosity, the gain in
$2\log L_{\max}$ relative to cold dark matter is 11.09 for Planck
spectra and 8.70 after adding CMB lensing and baryon acoustic
oscillations (Appendix C, Table IV of Ili\'c et al.~\cite{ilicGDM}).
Allowing the sound speed and viscosity to vary in the same nine
bins on both sides gives gains of 10.56 and 8.25, respectively, from
the published likelihood maxima.
The background abundance is fitted on both sides of each comparison.
Both tests admit homogeneous dilution on the conserved-charge branch.
Collisionless cold matter gives the same exponent. Distinguishing the
carriers requires a further observable; an OPH microwave spectrum
requires its own perturbations and transfer to the measured sky.

There is also a timing requirement on this interpretation. Allowing
the equation of state to vary over cosmic history with vanishing
sound speed and viscosity, Kopp et al.~\cite{koppHistory} obtained
a scaled dark density at matter--radiation equality of
$\omega_g^{\rm eq}=0.1193^{+0.0036}_{-0.0035}$ (95\%, Planck
spectra). An account in which all anomalous stress first appears in
settled galaxies would miss this early source in the assumed
cosmological dynamics. An early population of gravitating overlaps,
or an independently demonstrated alteration of the predicted
microwave observables, would address that requirement.
One of the eight individual bins, at $3161\lesssim z\lesssim9999$,
has a marginal 95\% interval $[0.0005,0.0162]$ for $w$ with
primary spectra and baryon acoustic oscillations. Primary spectra
alone give $[-0.0003,0.0157]$ for the same bin. These overlapping
data combinations estimate the same bin. A significance for the
whole history requires the joint sampling law. The published likelihood
maxima and marginal intervals are reproduced in
\path{code/cosmology/cold_dilution/}.

\paragraph{How much modular price must expanding screens generate?}
For the spherical source determined by two apertures, the active mass per
screen is $Kf/(2\pi L)$, with total modular price $K$ and price-weighted
mean cosine of depth $f\leq 1$. If the screen radius follows $a$ at fixed
comoving screen number, the density ratio on doubling is
$(K_2/K_1)(f_2/f_1)/16$. The measured finite initial profile gives
$f_1\geq0.696496$. Even moving all existing pairs to maximum mass allows a
conserved price to retain at most $0.089735$ of the initial density. The
constant-equation-of-state bound of Thomas et al.~\cite{thomasGDM} requires
at least $0.12438289$. The price must therefore grow by a factor of at
least $1.38611$ if these screens supply the cosmological dark component.
An initially isotropic continuum has $f_1=2/3$ and requires at least
$1.32675$. The experiment does not establish the cosmological initial
profile or a price-production law. See \texttt{curved\_shells.json}.

\paragraph{The same homogeneous charge with different structure growth.}
Even granting that both baryons and the anomalous source behave as
separately conserved pressureless fluids, their background charges do
not select the spatial perturbation. Consider a subhorizon Fourier
mode in a flat matter-dominated background. Let $b$ be the constant
baryonic mass fraction, $0<b<1$, and $\delta_i$ the linear density
contrast of either component. The continuity, Euler and Poisson
equations give, for derivatives with respect to $\log a$,
\[
 \delta_i''+\tfrac12\delta_i'
    =\tfrac32\,[b\delta_b+(1-b)\delta_A].
\]
Set $a=1$ initially, take the same small baryonic contrast
$\delta_b=\epsilon$, and start both fluids at zero peculiar velocity
in both preparations. If $\delta_A=\epsilon$, both contrasts evolve
as $\epsilon(3a/5+2a^{-3/2}/5)$ by direct substitution. If
$\delta_A=-b\epsilon/(1-b)$, the total contrast vanishes and both
contrasts stay at their initial values. This is the compensated mode
\cite{grinCompensated}. At $a=4$ the baryonic contrasts are
$49\epsilon/20$ and $\epsilon$, respectively. Both examples have the
same homogeneous anomalous density and the same gravitational coupling.
Selecting an adiabatic branch from the patch histories, propagating its
stress through radiation domination, and predicting the observed
microwave and lensing spectra would supply physical information beyond
the homogeneous conservation theorem.

\section{Physical closure conditions}
\label{sec:open}
The source interpretation requires the same cap modular generator, record
recovery law, local stress, physical clock and geometric response. The finite
bulk/collar split supplies the anomaly under its Gibbs-on-bulk premise;
it does not identify a physical boost generator. The common recovery envelope
suppresses compact sources only in its stated fixed-parameter limit and does
not force a nonzero galactic defect. The physical location, density and
statistics of those cuts must therefore be supplied separately.

Native collar measurements supply a finite fourth-order precursor.
Place two conserved words uniformly on distinct cells and select
independent fair correlation tags in the twenty ancestral patches.
The normalized connected modular-charge changes are $0.001713$ and
$0.001761$ on the two finest surveyed annuli. On doubling the latter
radius the coefficient is $0.001765$. The mixed charge vanishes for
far annuli in distinct ancestors and survives between adjacent
disjoint annuli in the same ancestor. A local co-located preparation
fails the fourth-power test. The observed scaling arises because two
admissions to the approximately quadratic screen region are required.
The routing radius, conditional writer and selected modular reference
are specified. A locally normalized gravitating stress requires
independent geometric response at fixed ordinary matter on proper
three-dimensional balls.

\paragraph{Which collar record the observer retains.}
\label{par:collar-index-variation}
Let $\sigma$ be the faithful joint state of two written seam heads after
a freely moving vacancy or excess has passed, $\pi=\sigma_A\otimes\sigma_B$
and $D=\sigma-\pi$. Sample the heads from the same preparation with probability
$f$, and otherwise independently from the two marginals. A range receiver can
supply $f$ by selecting a uniformly launched port in a clocked ball.
Discarding its hit flag gives $\rho_f=\pi+fD$. To measure a modular
variation, hold this reference fixed and change the joint component in
the source by $D$ per unit source variation. The observed tangent is $fD$.
Since both partial traces of $D$ vanish, the first-law slope in nats is
\[
 E(f)=-f\,{\rm Tr}\,D(\log\rho_f-\log\pi)
      =-f^2 J+O(f^3),\qquad
 J=\int_0^\infty {\rm Tr}\,[D(\pi+tI)^{-1}D(\pi+tI)^{-1}]\,dt .
\]
The resolvent derivative of the matrix logarithm gives the expansion.
Faithfulness makes $J$ finite and $J>0$ for $D\ne0$. Keeping the flag
instead yields the direct sum $f\sigma\oplus(1-f)\pi$ and slope $fE(1)$.
Strengthening these correlations has negative modular variation in this
reference convention. Weakening them reverses the sign. The single-head
marginals and the ordinary energy of the two parked heads agree in both arms.

Four prepared sources on 20 and 80 cells give $J=0.00500$ to $0.13209$.
Using the independent clocked-range census on the 327680-cell mesh,
the doubling exponent of the unflagged magnitude is $3.977$ to $3.996$;
the flag-preserving exponent is $1.9994$. The smaller meshes and the finite
bounds are in the
\texttt{collar\_pairing} package of the
\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/model/experiments/interval_echo_einstein_station}{finite Einstein station}.
For a fixed source, if $f/\ell^2\to a$ on physical balls, the resulting
coefficient is $E(f)/\ell^4\to-a^2J$. The calculation uses a supplied sampling
instrument, separate source and ranging preparations, and a graph-range
radius. A physical anomalous stress requires a selected regional algebra,
a common proper radius and a gravitational response to this same signed charge.

A probe can compare the same freely formed correlation before and after
an observer replaces one record with an independent draw from its
marginal. On the six-station source, changes in reciprocal range ratios
have opposite signs for the two probe charges on eighteen quartet
comparisons. The ratios cancel gains on each station and fixed
calibrations on each edge. The correlated and independent stocks have
identical ordinary parked exchange energy. This is an operational
test for a proposed collar correction. A successful physical optical
meter must recover the same signed geometry from its independently
calibrated probes.%
\footnote{\href{https://github.com/FloatingPragma/observer-patch-holography/tree/main/code/matched_einstein_limit/source_implication/full_geometry/station_balls/reciprocal_screen/range_contraction/formed_ranges}{Six-station range records, calibration equivalence and enclosures.}}

The profile theorem determines the exponents and leaves \(a_0=Gn^2c\).
A constant-capacity horizon dictionary supplies a separate dimensional scale,
\begin{equation}
 a_{\rm dS}=c^2\sqrt{\Lambda/3}
 =\frac{c^2}{\ell_P}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{N}}.
 \label{eq:horizon-scale}
\end{equation}
At the Planck-central \(\Lambda=1.089\times10^{-52}\,\mathrm{m^{-2}}\),
this is \(5.42\times10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}\). The full-RAR
calibration gives \(a_0/a_{\rm dS}=0.214\), ranging from \(0.176\) to
\(0.268\) as disk mass-to-light varies from \(0.6\) to \(0.4\);
the deep-law fits give \(0.156\) to \(0.171\). These comparisons do not
select \(n\) or the per-cut variance constant. In this paragraph the
\(c\) in \(a_{\rm dS}\) is the speed of light, distinct from the variance
constant in the per-cut dictionary.

Reversing the calibration tests whether the choice of training
instrument accounts for the discrepancy. With the SPARC scale
withheld, the covariance-weighted shear mean at nominal masses and
bin centres gives untrimmed spherical scales of $1.530\times10^{-10}$
(KiDS-bright) and $2.813\times10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ (GAMA).
At the measured outer AGC~114905 ring, assigning its entire rotation
to the untrimmed anomaly gives $a_0\le2.699\times10^{-12}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$.
The generous dwarf allowance gives $9.195\times10^{-12}$. Giving the
lenses the upper bin edges, $+0.2$ dex mass, maximum spherical edge
enhancement and a two-statistical-error downward shift, their respective
lower amplitudes are $3.139\times10^{-11}$ and $3.649\times10^{-11}$
in the same units. The physical meaning of these comparisons depends on
the region where the exterior law applies. The common sublinear-profile
bound in Section~\ref{sec:lensing} eliminates the amplitude altogether.

Rotation and lensing are independent ways to calibrate the effective
profile. A source-based normalization instead requires a measured cut
density per unit proper length, a calibrated variance per unit source
mass and the same Newton coupling seen by ordinary matter. The change
$n\mapsto\lambda n$, $c_{\rm var}\mapsto c_{\rm var}/\lambda^2$ preserves
$a_0$ for every $\lambda>0$. Even exact profile measurements under this
dictionary would therefore leave the underlying number and distribution
of record-making patches underdetermined. On the protected native annuli,
the variance of the connected modular defect conditional on two
32-unit words runs from $0.00690305$ to $0.00900036$ nats squared as
their joint writer changes. Conditioning on two 65-unit words gives
the same three variance values, with independent held samples in both
bins. The count of distinct occupied word radii per normalized routed
length nearly doubles between adjacent annuli. Neither conditional
word-energy variance nor this radial count identifies the variance per
unit baryonic mass or the proper density of gravitating cuts.

The rotation data test several distinct claims. The fitted spacing gives
little held predictive gain over a smooth model with extra inner-ring
scatter. In the gas-rich dwarf the nominal excess squared speed is about
one sixteenth of even the least excess allowed by the tested disk
prescription. The retained exterior sample has only one qualifying orbit
at $90\%$ baryonic enclosure and two additional orbits at $80\%$.
Its speed intervals provide a limited test at a normalization fixed on
other galaxies. At this same normalization the two separate six-bin
mean-shear profiles give covariance-weighted squared residuals of 44.37
and 17.07 for nominal masses, negligible slip and bin-centre evaluation.
The surveys share imaging, and their statistics are reported separately.
Variation of mass calibration and weights within each bin affects the
comparison. These measurements give no independent estimate of local
record density.

Measurements of a joint thermal reference identify a signed interaction
energy on one repair seam. In the supplied homogeneous Einstein geometry
the coupling measured on ordinary matter predicts its curvature exactly
(p.~\pageref{par:seam-energy-test}).
A galactic explanation must predict the signed regional overlap stress and the
ordinary stress on the same regions, with the same clock and ruler.
Matching the weak gravitational response to an ordinary source fixes
the coupling before the overlap source is varied. If a positive
overlap stress measured in this way gave no corresponding geometric
response at that coupling, the proposed identification would fail on
that domain. Predictions for a gas disk, a lens or a merging cluster
also require the spatial distribution, pressures and transport of that
stress. The static spherical calculation specifies these only on its
declared branch.
